Immediately, a couple of airjeeps pounced in, to strafe the fleeing enemy. Somebody must have already been issuing orders on another wavelength; a number of Kragans, riding hipposaurs, were galloping into the light of the flares.

“Now, let’s have a look at the native barracks and the maintenance-yards,” he said. “And then, we’ll make a circuit around the Reservation, about two or three miles out. I’m not happy about where Firkked’s army is.”

The driver looked at him. “I’ve been worrying about that, too, sir,” he said. “I can’t understand why he hasn’t jumped us, already. I know it takes time to get one of these geek armies on the road, but.﻿ ﻿…”

“He’s hoping our native troops and the mine laborers will be able to wipe us out, themselves,” von Schlichten said. “For the timidity and stupidity of our enemies, Allah make us truly thankful, amen. It’s something no commander should depend on, but be glad when it happens. If Firkked had had a couple of regiments on hand outside the reservation to jump us as soon as the Tenth and the Zirks mutinied, he could have swamped us in twenty minutes and we’ll all have had our throats cut by now.”

===Chinese===
「好吧，你自己酌情办。东西放到土著兵营后头去，放到发电厂和公司办公楼之间，以及别的搁得下的地方。」中尉用胳膊肘轻轻碰了碰他，按下仪表板上的几个按钮。

「照明弹放了。」

眨眼间，两架飞行吉普猛扑过去，扫射逃跑的敌人。想必已有人在另一条波长上下了命令；一队骑着河马龙的克拉冈人正冲进照明弹的光亮里，疾驰而来。

「现在，去看看土著兵营和维修场吧，」他说。「然后我们绕特区外圈转一圈，隔个两三英里。菲尔克德（Firkked）王的军队眼下在哪儿，我心里不踏实。」

驾驶员看了看他。「这事我也一直担心，长官，」他说，「我真不明白，他为什么到现在还没扑上来。我知道这种土佬军队开拔起来费工夫，可是……」

「他指望着我们的土著部队和矿工们自己就能把我们干掉，」冯·施利希滕说。「愿真主让我们为敌人的怯懦和愚蠢诚心感恩，阿门。这种事任何指挥官都不该指望，可碰上了就该庆幸。倘若菲尔克德早有准备，在特区外屯上一两个团，等第十团和齐尔克（Zirk）骑兵一哗变就扑上来，那他二十分钟就能把我们淹没，我们此刻个个都已被割了喉咙。」

===Original===
There was nothing going on in the area between the native barracks and the mountains except some sporadic firing as small patrols of Kragans clashed with clumps of fleeing mutineers. All the barracks, even those of the Rifles, were burning; the red-and-yellow danger-lights around the power-plant and the waterworks and the explosives magazines were still on. Most of the floodlights were still on, and there was still some fighting around the maintenance-yard. It looked as though the survivors of the Tenth N.U.N.I. were in a few small pockets which were being squeezed out.

There was nothing at all going on north of the Reservation; the countryside, by day a checkerboard of walled fields and small villages, was dark, except for a dim light, here and there, where the occupants of some farmhouse had been awakened by the noise of battle. The airjeep dropped lower, and the driver slid open the window beside him; von Schlichten could hear the grunts and snorts and squawks of farm-animals, similarly aroused.

Then, two miles east of the Reservation, he caught a new sound﻿—the flowing, riverlike, murmur of something vast on the move.

“Hear that, lieutenant?” he asked. “Head for it, at about a thousand feet. When we’re directly above it, let go some flares.”

===Chinese===
土著兵营与群山之间的地带没什么动静，只有零星交火——克拉冈人的小股巡逻队撞上了成伙溃逃的哗变兵。所有兵营，连来复枪团的营房也在燃烧；发电厂、供水厂和炸药库四周的红黄警示灯还亮着。泛光灯大多还亮着，维修场周围也还有些战斗。看样子，第十北乌勒尔土著步兵团（Tenth N.U.N.I.）的残部被困在几个小小的口袋阵里，正被逐一挤垮。

特区以北全无动静；这片白日里像围墙田垄和小村落拼成的棋盘一般的乡野，一片漆黑，只余星星点点昏黄的灯火——那是被炮声惊醒的农舍人家。飞行吉普降得更低了些，驾驶员推开他身旁的舷窗；冯·施利希滕听得见同样被惊醒的家畜们的呼噜、喷鼻和嘎嘎叫声。

随后，在特区以东两英里处，他捕捉到一种新的声响——某个庞然大物开进时那流水般绵延不断的低沉喧声。

「听见了吗，中尉？」他问。「朝那声音飞，高度一千英尺上下。到了正上方，就放照明弹。」

===Original===
“Yes, sir.” The younger man had lowered his voice to a whisper. “That’s geek, headed for the Reservation.”

“Maybe Firkked’s army,” von Schlichten thought aloud. “Or maybe a city mob.”

“Not quite noisy enough for a mob, is it, sir?”

“A tired mob,” von Schlichten told him. “They’d start out on a run, yelling ‘*Znidd Suddabit*!’ By the time they got across the bridges to this side of the river, they’d be winded. They’d stop for a blow, and then they’d settle down to steady slogging to save their wind. Sometimes a mob like that’s worse than a fresh mob. They get stubborn; they act more deliberately.”

The noises were growing clearer, louder. He picked up the phone and punched the wavelength of the military airport.

“Von Schlichten, my compliments to Colonel Jarman. Tell him there’s a geek mob, or possibly Firkked’s regulars, on the main highway from Skilk, two miles east of the Reservation. Get some combat contragravity over here, at once. We’ll light them up for you. And tell Colonel Jarman to start flying patrols up and down along the Hoork River; this may not be the only gang that’s coming out to see us.”

===Chinese===
「是，长官。」年轻军官压低了嗓音，几乎成了耳语。「那是土佬，正朝特区来。」

「也许是菲尔克德的军队，」冯·施利希滕自言自语道，「也许是城里聚起的暴民。」

「吵闹得还不够凶，不像暴民，是吧，长官？」

「是一伙累坏了的暴民，」冯·施利希滕告诉他。「出发时他们会一路狂奔，高喊『Znidd suddabit!』（兹尼德·苏达比特）等过了桥、到了河这一边，早跑得气喘吁吁了。他们会停下来喘口气，然后省着劲儿，闷头稳步赶路。这种暴民有时比一伙新攒起来的暴民更难缠。他们会犯倔，行动也更沉着。」

声响越来越清晰，越来越响。他拿起话机，拨通了军用机场的波长。

「冯·施利希滕，向贾曼上校代为致意。告诉他，从斯基尔克（Skilk）来的公路上有一伙土佬暴众，也可能是菲尔克德的正规军，位置在特区以东两英里。立刻调些作战用的反重力载具过来。我们会替你们把他们照亮。另外告诉贾曼上校，开始沿霍尔克河上下飞行巡逻；跑来看我们的，未必就这一伙。」

===Original===
The sounds were directly below, now﻿—the scuffing of horny-soled feet on the dirt road, the clink and rattle of slung weapons, the clicking and squeeking of Ulleran voices.

The lieutenant said, “Here go the flares, sir.”

Von Schlichten shut his eyes, then opened them slowly. The driver, upon releasing the flares, had nosed up, banked, turned, and was coming in again, down the road toward the advancing column. Von Schlichten peered into his all-armament sight, his foot on the machine-gun pedal and his fingers on the rocket buttons. The highway below was jammed with geeks, and they were all stopped dead and staring upward, as though hypnotized by the lights. A second later, they had recovered and were shooting﻿—not at the airjeep, but at the four globes of blazing magnesium. Then he had the close-packed mass of non-humanity in his sights; he tramped the pedal and began punching buttons. He still had four rockets left by the time the mob was behind him.

“All right, let’s take another pass at them. Same direction.”

The driver put the airjeep into a quick loop and came out of it in front of the mob, who now had their backs turned and were staring in the direction in which they had last seen the vehicle. Again, von Schlichten plowed them with rockets and harrowed them with his guns. Some of the Skilkans were trying to get over the high fences on either side of the road﻿—really stockades of petrified tree-trunks. Others were firing, and this time they were shooting at the airjeep. It took one hit from a heavy shellosaur-rifle, and, immediately, the driver banked and turned away from the road.

“Dammit, why did you do that?” von Schlichten demanded, lifting his foot from the gun-pedal. “Are you afraid of the kind of popguns those geeks are using?”

===Chinese===
此刻，声响已在正下方——角质硬底踏在土路上的嚓嚓声，肩挎武器的叮当哗啦声，乌勒尔人叽叽嘎嘎的说话声。

中尉说：「照明弹放了，长官。」

冯·施利希滕闭上眼，又缓缓睁开。驾驶员放完照明弹，已拉起机头、侧倾、转弯，正顺着公路朝挺进的纵队再次冲去。冯·施利希滕凑近全武备瞄准具，一脚踩住机枪踏板，手指搭上火箭按钮。下方公路被土佬挤得水泄不通，他们全都僵在原地，仰头望天，像被那灯光催眠了似的。一秒钟后，他们回过神来，纷纷开火——不打飞行吉普，打的是那四团炽燃的镁光球。随即，那挤作一团、密不透风的非人类躯体已进入他的瞄准具；他一脚踩死踏板，接连按动按钮。等暴民被甩到身后，他手里还剩四枚火箭。

「好，再冲他们来一轮。方向不变。」

驾驶员让飞行吉普迅疾翻了个筋斗，从暴民前方钻出——这伙人此刻都背过了身，正望着他们最后望见那飞行器的方向。冯·施利希滕再度用火箭犁他们，用机枪耙他们。一些斯基尔克人试图翻越公路两侧的高栅栏——那其实是石化树干围成的栅墙。另一些人开了火，这一回打的是飞行吉普。机身挨了一发重型壳龙步枪（shellosaur-rifle）的弹，驾驶员当即侧倾机身，转离了公路。

「混账，你这是干什么？」冯·施利希滕质问道，脚从机枪踏板上抬了起来。「你还怕土佬使的那种小破枪不成？」

===Original===
“I am not afraid to risk my vehicle, or myself, sir,” the lieutenant replied, with the extreme formality of a very junior officer chewing out a very senior one. “I am, however, afraid to risk my passenger. Generals are not expendable, sir; neither are they issued for use as clay pigeons.”

He was right, of course. Von Schlichten admitted it. “I’m too old to play cowboy, like this,” he said. “Back to the Reservation, telecast station.”

Looking back over his shoulder, he saw eight or ten more flares alight, and the ground-flashes of exploding shells and rockets; the air above the road was sparkling with gun-flames. Jarman must have had some contragravity ready to be sent off on the instant.

While he had been out, somebody had gotten a TV-pickup mounted on a contragravity-lifter and run up to two thousand feet, on the end of a steel-tough tensilon mooring-line. The big circular screen was lit, showing the whole Company Reservation, with the surrounding countryside foreshortened by perspective to the distant lights of Skilk. The map had been taken up from the floor, and a big terrain-board had been brought in from the Chief Engineer’s office and set up in its place. In front of the screen, Paula Quinton, Barney Mordkovitz, Colonel Cheng-Li, and, conspicuously silent, Jules Keaveney sat drinking coffee and munching sandwiches. Half a dozen Terrans, of both sexes, were working furiously to get the markers which replaced the pink and white pills placed on the board, and one of Captain Inez Malavez’s noncoms, with a headset, was getting combat reports directly from the switchboard. Everything was clicking like well-oiled machinery.

===Chinese===
「要我拿座机冒险，或是拿自己冒险，我都不怕，长官，」中尉答道，那副郑重其事的腔调，活像个极年轻的军官在训斥极资深的长官。「可是，要我拿乘客冒险，我怕。将军不属于消耗品，长官；也不是配发下来当泥鸽靶子使的。」

他当然没错。冯·施利希滕认了。「我这把年纪，不该再这么扮牛仔了，」他说。「回特区，电视广播站。」

他回头望去，又见八九枚照明弹次第点亮，炮弹和火箭在地面炸出团团闪光；公路上空满是枪口火焰的星芒。贾曼手边想必早备好了一批反重力载具，一声令下便能即刻出动。

他出去的这当口，有人已把一台电视摄像机装上反重力升降器，系在一根钢索般坚韧的坦西隆（tensilon）系留索末端，升上了两千英尺高空。巨大的圆形屏幕亮着，显示出整个公司特区，四周乡野在透视中层层远去，一直缩到斯基尔克远处的灯火。地上的地图已经撤走，从总工程师办公室搬来一块大地形板，摆在了原处。屏幕前，保拉·昆顿（Paula Quinton）、巴尼·莫德科维茨（Barney Mordkovitz）、郑立（Cheng-Li）上校，还有格外沉默的朱尔斯·基夫尼（Jules Keaveney），坐着喝咖啡、嚼三明治。六名男女地球人正飞快地把那些取代粉白丸的标子摆上地形板；伊内丝·马拉维兹（Inez Malavez）上尉手下的一名军士戴着耳机，直接从总机台获取战斗报告。一切滴答运转，像上足了油的机器一样顺滑。

===Original===
On the TV-screen, the Residency area was ablaze with light, and so were the ship-docks, the airport and spaceport, the shops, and the maintenance-yard. On the terrain-board, the latter was now marked as completely in Company hands. The ruins of the native-troops barracks were still burning, and there was a twinkle of orange-red here and there among the ruins of the labor-camp. Much of the equipment for the polar mines had already been shifted into defensible ground. The rest of the circle was dark, except for the distant lights of Skilk, where the nuclear power plant was apparently still functioning in native hands.

Then, without warning, a spot of white light blazed into being southeast of the Company area and southwest of Skilk, followed by another and another. Instantly, von Schlichten glanced up at the row of smaller screens, and on one of them saw the view as picked up by a patrolling airjeep.

The army of King Firkked of Skilk had finally put in its appearance, coming in two columns, one southward from Skilk and the other northward along the west bank of the dry river. The former had crossed over and joined the latter, about three miles south of the Reservation. The scene in the screen was similar to the one he had, himself, witnessed through his armament-sight. The Skilkan regulars had been marching in formation, some on the road and some along parallel lanes and paths. They had the look of trained and disciplined troops, but they had made the same mistake as the rabble that had been shot up on the north side of the Reservation. Unused to attack from the air, they had all halted in place and were gaping open-mouthed, their opal teeth gleaming in the white flare-light. However, before the aircar had passed over them, the lead company of one regiment, armed with Terran rifles, had begun firing.

In the big screen, it could be seen that Colonel Jarman had thrown most of his available contragravity at them, including the combat-cars, that had already started to form the second wave of the attack on the mob to the north. Other flares bloomed in the darkness, and the fiery trails of rockets curved downward to end in yellow flashes on the ground.

===Chinese===
电视屏幕上，驻扎官府一带灯火通明，船坞、机场和太空港、各车间以及维修场也一样。地形板上，维修场这一处已标注为完全在公司手里。土著兵营的废墟仍在燃烧，劳工营的废墟间也处处闪着橘红色的火苗。极地矿场用的大批设备，已经转移到可守的地带。圆圈其余部分一片漆黑，只有斯基尔克远处的灯火——那里的核电站显然还在土著手里运转着。

接着，毫无征兆地，一团白光在公司区东南、斯基尔克西南轰然亮起，随即又是一团、再一团。冯·施利希滕立刻抬头看那一排小屏幕，在其中一屏上看到了一架巡逻飞行吉普摄取的画面。

斯基尔克的菲尔克德王大军终于露面了，分作两路开来：一路从斯基尔克向南，另一路沿干河的西岸向北。前者已渡过河床，在特区以南约三英里处与后者会合。屏幕上的景象，与他方才透过武器瞄准具亲眼所见的如出一辙。斯基尔克正规军原本成建制行军，一部分走公路，一部分沿平行的巷陌小径。他们瞧上去像一支训练有素、军纪严明的部队，却犯了与特区北面那伙被打烂的乌合之众同样的错误——不习惯空袭，全员当场立定，张口仰望，蛋白石般的牙齿在白色照明弹的光芒下闪亮。不过，还没等那架飞车掠过他们头顶，某个团的一个装备地球造步枪的先头连，已经开火了。

大屏幕上可以看到，贾曼上校已把手头可用的反重力载具大半投向了他们，连战斗车也包括在内——那些战斗车本已开始集结，准备对北面的暴民发动第二波攻击。又有照明弹在黑暗中绽放，火箭的火红尾迹弯垂而下，在地面炸出黄色的闪光。

===Original===
The airjeep with the pickup circled back; the troops on the road and in the adjoining fields had broken. The former were caught between the fences which made Ulleran roads such deathtraps when under air-attack. The latter had dispersed, and were running away, individually and by squads; at first, it looked like a panic, but he could see officers signaling to the larger groups of fugitives to open out, apparently directing the flight. By this time, there were ten or twelve combat-cars and about twenty airjeeps at work. In the moving view from the pickup-jeep, he saw what looked like a 90 mm rocket land in the middle of a company that was still trying to defend itself with small-arms fire on the road, wiping out about half of them.

“Make the most of it, boys,” Barney Mordkovitz, his mouth full of sandwich, was saying. “Heave it to them; you won’t get another chance like that at those buggers.”

“Why not?” Colonel Paula Quinton wanted to know. Her military education was progressing, but it still had a few gaps to fill in.

“The next time they’re air-struck, they won’t stay bunched,” Mordkovitz replied. “A lot of them didn’t stay bunched this time, if you noticed. And they’ll keep out from between the fences.”

In the large screen, a quick succession of gun-flashes leaped up from the direction of the Hoork River and shells began bursting over the scene of the attack. The screen tuned to the pickup on the airjeep went dead; in the big screen, there was a twinkling of falling fire. Almost at once, thirty or forty rocket-trails converged on the gun-position, and, for a moment, explosions burned like a bonfire.

===Chinese===
载着摄像机的那架飞行吉普兜了回来；公路上和两侧田地里的部队已经溃散。前者被夹在两道栅栏之间进退不得——正是这种栅栏，让乌勒尔的公路一遇空袭便成了死亡陷阱。后者四散奔逃，有单溜的，有成班跑的；乍一看像是惊慌失措的大溃逃，但他看得出军官们正向较大的逃亡群打手势，令其散开，分明是在指挥这场溃逃。到这时，投入作战的战斗车已有十一二辆，飞行吉普约二十架。在摄像吉普传回的移动画面里，他看见一枚像是 90 毫米的火箭，正落在公路上一个仍以轻武器火力自卫的连队中央，一下子报销了他们约半数人马。

「抓紧痛打吧，小伙子们，」巴尼·莫德科维茨满嘴三明治地说。「狠狠招呼他们；对付这帮家伙，这样的机会可不会再有了。」

「为什么不会？」保拉·昆顿上校问道。她的军事学修养正稳步长进，只是尚有几处空白待补。

「下回再挨空袭，他们就不会扎堆了，」莫德科维茨答道，「你若留意，这回就有不少人没扎堆。而且他们也不会再待在两道栅栏中间了。」

大屏幕上，霍尔克河方向陡然腾起一连串炮口闪光，炮弹开始在攻击现场上空炸开。调谐到飞行吉普摄像机的那块屏幕熄了；大屏幕上，只见一道坠落的火光闪烁而灭。几乎同一瞬间，三四十条火箭尾迹汇聚到那处炮兵阵地上，刹那间，爆炸像一堆篝火般熊熊燃起。

===Original===
“They had a 75 mm at the rear of the column,” somebody called from the big switchboard. “Lieutenant Kalanang’s jeep was hit; Lieutenant Vermaas is cutting in his pickup on the same wavelength.”

The small screen lighted again. In the big screen, a cluster of magnesium-lights appeared above where the Skilkan gun had been; in the small screen, there was a stubbled grainfield, pocked with craters, and the bodies of fifteen or twenty natives, all rather badly mangled. An overturned and apparently destroyed 75 mm gun lay on its side.

Five or six fairly large fires had broken out, by this time, around the point of attack. Von Schlichten nodded approvingly.

“I was wondering how long it’d take somebody to think of that,” he said. “Granaries and forage-stacks on some of these farms. They’ll burn for half an hour, at least.” He looked at his watch. “And by that time, it’ll be daylight.”

===Chinese===
「他们在纵队尾上藏了一门 75 毫米炮，」大总机台那头有人喊道。「卡拉南（Kalanang）中尉的吉普被打中了；弗马斯（Vermaas）中尉正把自己的摄像机切入同一波长。」

小屏幕重新亮起。大屏幕上，原先斯基尔克火炮所在处的上空出现了一簇镁光照明弹；小屏幕里，是一片割过茬的谷田，坑坑洼洼布满弹坑，横着十五二十具土著尸体，个个残破得不成样子。一门翻倒的、显然已毁的 75 毫米炮侧躺在地上。

到这时，攻击点周围已燃起五六处相当大的火头。冯·施利希滕赞许地点了点头。

「我正纳闷，要多久才有人想出这一招，」他说。「这些农庄里有的是谷仓和草料垛。至少能烧上半个钟头。」他看了看表。「到那时候，天也该亮了。」

===Original===
“As far as we know, that was the only 75 mm gun Firkked had,” Colonel Cheng-Li said. “He has at least six, possibly ten, 40 mm’s. It’s a wonder we haven’t seen anything of them.”

“Well, there’s no way of being sure,” Jules Keaveney said, “but I have an idea they’re all at or around the Palace. Firkked knows about how much contragravity we have. He’s probably wondering why we aren’t bombing him, now.”

“He doesn’t know we’ve sold the Palace to King Jonkvank for an army,” von Schlichten said. “And that reminds me﻿—how much contragravity could Firkked scrape together, for an attack on us? I’ve been expecting a geek Luftwaffe over here, at any moment.”

Colonel Cheng-Li studied the smoking tip of his cigarette for a moment. “Well, Firkked owns, personally, three ten-passenger aircars, a thing like a troop-carrier that he transports some of his courtiers around in, four airjeeps armed with a pair of 15 mm machine-guns apiece, and two big lorries. There are possibly two hundred vehicles of all types in Skilk and the country around, but some of them are in the hands of natives friendly to us and or hostile to Firkked. I can get the exact figures from the Constabulary office at Company House.”

“That’s close enough,” von Schlichten told him. “And there’ll be oodles of thermoconcentrate-fuel, and blasting explosives. Colonel Quinton, suppose you call Ed Wallingsby, the Chief Engineer, right away; have him commissioned colonel. Tell him to get to work making this place secure against air attack; tell him to consult with Colonel Jarman. Tell him to get those geeks Leavitt has penned in the repair-dock at the airport and use them to dig slit-trenches and fill sandbags and so on. He can use Kragan limited-duty wounded to guard them.﻿ ﻿… Mr. Keaveney, you’ll begin setting up something in the way of an A.R.P.-organization. You’ll have to get along on what nobody else wants. You will also consult with Colonel Jarman, and with Colonel Wallingsby. Better get started on it now. Just think of everything around here that could go wrong in case of an air attack, and try to do something about it in advance.”

===Chinese===
「就我们所知，那是菲尔克德仅有的一门 75 毫米炮，」郑立上校说。「他手里至少还有六门、可能十门 40 毫米炮。奇怪的是，至今竟一门都没见着。」

「唔，这可说不准，」朱尔斯·基夫尼说，「不过依我看，它们全在王宫里，要不就在王宫左近。菲尔克德大致摸得清我们有多少反重力载具。这会儿他八成正在纳闷，我们怎么还不去炸他。」

「他还不知道，我们已经把王宫作价卖给了琼克万克（Jonkvank）王，换了一支军队，」冯·施利希滕说。「这倒提醒了我——菲尔克德东拼西凑，能凑出多少反重力载具来打我们？我一直在等着土佬的『空军』（Luftwaffe）随时开过来呢。」

郑立上校对着香烟燃着的烟头端详了片刻。「这么说吧：菲尔克德个人拥有三架十座飞车，一架类似运兵艇的玩意儿——他用来载着一部分廷臣四处转悠——四架各装一对 15 毫米机枪的飞行吉普，还有两辆大卡车。斯基尔克城和四乡各地各型载具加起来，可能有二百之多，不过其中一部分在亲我们的、或与菲尔克德为敌的土著手里。具体数字，我可以到公司大楼（Company House）的宪兵队办事处去查。」

「这就够准的了，」冯·施利希滕对他说。「而且，还会有大批的热凝燃料（thermoconcentrate-fuel）和爆破炸药。昆顿上校，请你这就打电话给总工程师埃德·沃林斯比（Ed Wallingsby），给他授个上校军衔。叫他着手把这个地方修得能抗空袭；叫他同贾曼上校商议。叫他去调利维特（Leavitt）关在机场修理坞里的那些土佬，用他们挖散兵坑、装沙袋等等。可以派可服轻役的克拉冈伤员去看管他们。……基夫尼先生，你来着手组建一个防空组织（A.R.P.）之类的东西。你只能拿别人挑剩下的东西凑合。你也要同贾曼上校、沃林斯比上校商议。最好现在就着手。把这里一遭空袭就可能出乱子的每件事都想一遍，尽量预先想法子补救。」

## Introduction Dr. John D. Clark / 引言 约翰·D·克拉克博士

## I The Silicone World / 第一部分 硅酮世界

1
 The Star and Its Most Important Planet / 恒星及其最重要的行星

===Original===
The planet is named Uller (it seems that when interstellar travel was developed, the names of Greek Gods had been used up, so those of Norse gods were used). It is the second planet of the star Beta Hydri, right angle 0:23, declension −77:32, G0 (solar) type star, of approximately the same size as Sol; distance from Earth, 21 light years.
Uller revolves around it in a nearly circular orbit, at a distance of 100,000,000 miles, making it a little colder than Earth. A year is of the approximate length of that on Earth. A day lasts 26 hours.
The axis of Uller is in the same plane as the orbit, so that at a certain time of the year the north pole is pointed directly at the sun, while at the opposite end of the orbit it points directly away. The result is highly exaggerated seasons. At the poles the temperature runs from 120° C to a low of −80° C. At the equator it remains not far from 10° C all year round. Strong winds blow during the summer and winter, from the hot to the cold pole; few winds during the spring and fall. The appearance of the poles varies during the year from baked deserts to glaciers covered with solid CO2. Free water exists in the equatorial regions all year round.

===Chinese===
这颗行星名叫乌勒尔（Uller）（看来星际航行发展起来的时候，希腊神话诸神的名字已经用尽，于是便用上了北欧诸神的名字）。它是水蛇座β（Beta Hydri）的第二颗行星；该恒星赤经 0:23，赤纬 −77:32，属 G0 型（太阳型）恒星，大小与太阳大致相当；距地球 21 光年。
乌勒尔沿一条近乎正圆的轨道绕它运行，距离为 100,000,000 英里，因此比地球略冷一点。一年的长度与地球上大体相当。一天则有 26 个小时。
乌勒尔的自转轴与公转轨道同处一个平面，因此一年之中总有某个时节，北极正对太阳，而到了轨道的另一端，又恰好背对太阳。结果便是被极度放大的四季。两极的温度高可至 120°C，低可至 −80°C。赤道一带则终年保持在 10°C 上下。冬夏两季狂风大作，从炎热的极区刮向寒冷的极区；春秋两季则少风。两极的景象一年之间从烤炙的荒漠变为覆满固态 CO2 的冰川。赤道地区则终年都有游离态的水存在。

2
 Solar Movement as Seen from Uller / 从乌勒尔所见的太阳运行

===Original===
As seen from the north pole—no sun is visible on January 1. On April 1, it bisects the horizon all day, swinging completely around. April 1 to July 1, it continues swinging around, gradually rising in the sky, the spiral converging to its center at the zenith, which it reaches July 1. From July 1 to October 1 the spiral starts again, spreading out from the center until on October 1 it bisects the horizon again. On October 1 night arrives to stay until April 1.
At the equator, the sun is visible bisecting the southern horizon for all 26 hours of the day on January 1. From January 1 to April 1, the sun starts to dip below the horizon at night, to rise higher above it during the day. During all this time it rises and sets at the same hours, but rises in the southeast and sets in the southwest. At noon it is higher each day in the southern sky until April 1, when it rises due east, passes through the zenith and sets due west. From April 1 to July 1, its noon position drops down to the north, until on July 1, it is visible all day, bisected by the northern horizon.

===Chinese===
从北极看——1 月 1 日，太阳不可见。到 4 月 1 日，它终日为地平线所平分，环天整整一周。从 4 月 1 日到 7 月 1 日，它继续环行，逐日升上天空，螺旋一圈圈向中心收拢，至 7 月 1 日收于天顶。从 7 月 1 日到 10 月 1 日，螺旋重新开始，自中心向外张开，到 10 月 1 日再次为地平线所平分。10 月 1 日起，长夜降临，一直停留到来年 4 月 1 日。
在赤道处，1 月 1 日全天 26 个小时里，太阳都半沉半露，横切于南面地平线上。从 1 月 1 日到 4 月 1 日，太阳开始在夜间沉到地平线以下，白昼则升得更高。在这整段日子里，它升降的时刻始终如一，只是从东南方升起，在西南方落下。正午时分，它在南天一天比一天高，到 4 月 1 日，便从正东升起，经天顶而过，在正西落下。从 4 月 1 日到 7 月 1 日，它的正午位置转而向北、逐日降低，到 7 月 1 日，终日可见，为北面地平线所平分。

3
 Chemistry and Geology of Uller / 乌勒尔的化学与地质

===Original===
Calcium and chlorine are rarer than on earth, sodium is somewhat commoner. As a result of the shortage of calcium there is a higher ration of silicates to carbonates than exists on earth. The water is slightly alkaline and resembles a very dilute solution of sodium silicate (water glass). It would have a pH of 8.5 and tastes slightly soapy. Also, when it dries out it leaves a sticky, and then a glassy, crackly film. Rocks look fairly earthlike, but the absence or scarcity of anything like limestone is noticeable. Practically all the sedimentary rocks are of the sandstone type.
All rivers are seasonal, running from the polar regions to the central seas in the spring only, or until the polar cap is completely dried out.

===Chinese===
钙和氯比地球上少见，钠则略多。由于钙的短缺，这里硅酸盐与碳酸盐之比高于地球上的情形。水呈弱碱性，类似一种极稀的硅酸钠（水玻璃）溶液，pH 值当在 8.5 上下，尝起来略带皂味。而且水干之后，会留下一层先是发黏、继而变得如玻璃般酥脆的薄膜。岩石看上去与地球上的相当相似，但石灰岩之类或绝迹或稀少，颇为显眼。沉积岩几乎清一色都是砂岩一类。
所有河流都是季节性的，只在春天从极区流向中部的诸海，直到极冠完全干涸方止。

4
 Animal Life / 动物

===Original===
As on Earth life arose in the primitive waters and with a carbon base, but because of the abundance of silicone, there was a strong tendency for the microscopic organisms to develop silicate exoskeletons, like diatoms. The present invertebrate animal life of the planet is of this type and is confined to the equatorial seas. They run from amoeba-like objects to things like crayfish, with silicate skeletons. Later, some species of them started taking silicone into their soft tissues, and eventually their carbon-chain compounds were converted to silicone type chains, from
with organic radicals on the side links. These organisms were a transitional type, with silicone tissues and water body fluids, resembling the earthly amphibians, and are now practically extinct. There are a few species, something like segmented worms, still to be seen in the backwaters of the central seas.

===Chinese===
与地球上一样，生命也诞生于原始水域，以碳为根基；但由于硅酮（silicone）俯拾皆是，微生物便有一种强烈的倾向，要发育出硅酸盐的外骨骼来，正如硅藻一般。该行星现存的无脊椎动物便属此类型，而且局限于赤道诸海。它们小者如变形虫般的个体，大者如小龙虾般的东西，都长着硅酸盐的骨骼。后来，其中一些物种开始把硅酮纳入自己的软组织，最终，它们的碳链化合物便转化成了硅酮型的链——
侧链上连接着有机基团。这类生物属于过渡类型：硅酮的组织配上含水的体液，近似地球上的两栖动物，如今已几乎绝迹。只有寥寥几个物种，样子像分节的蠕虫，仍能在中部诸海的洄水湾里见到。

===Original===
A further development occurred when the silicone chain animals began to get short-chain silicones into their circulatory systems, held in solution by OH or NH2 groups on the ends and branches of the chains. The proportion of these compounds gradually increased until the water was a minor and then a missing constituent. The larger mobile species were, then, practically anhydrous. Their blood consists of short-chain silicones, with quartz reinforcing for the soft parts and their armor, teeth, etc., of pure amorphous quartz (opal). Most of these parts are of the milky variety, variously tinted with metallic impurities, as are the varieties of sapphires.
These pure silicone animals, due to their practical indestructibility, annihilated all but the smaller of the carbon animals, and drove the compromise types into odd corners as relics. They developed into a fish-like animal with a very large swim-bladder to compensate for the rather higher density of the silicone tissues, and from these fish the land animals developed. Due to their high density and resulting high weight, they tend to be low on the ground, rather reptilian in look. Three pairs of legs are usual in order to distribute the heavy load. There is no sharp dividing line between the quartz armor and the silicone tissue. One merges into the other.
The dominant pure silicone animals only could become mobile and venture far from the temperate equatorial regions of Uller, since they neither froze nor stiffened with cold, nor became incapacitated by heat. Note that all animal life is cold-blooded, with a negligible difference between body and ambient temperatures. Since the animals are silicones, they don’t get sluggish like cold snakes.

===Chinese===
更进一步的发展，发生在硅酮链动物开始把短链硅酮纳入循环系统之时——这些短链靠链端与支链上的 OH 基或 NH2 基团维系于溶液之中。这类化合物的比例逐渐升高，直到水先是沦为次要成分、继而荡然无存。于是，那些较大的、能活动的物种，实际上已不含水分。它们的血液由短链硅酮构成，软体部分以石英加固，而甲壳、牙齿等则由纯的非晶质石英（蛋白石）构成。这些部件大多呈乳白品种，因金属杂质而染出种种色调，一如蓝宝石的诸多变种。
这些纯硅酮动物由于几乎坚不可摧，把碳基动物诛灭殆尽——只留下较小的种类——又把那些折中类型当作残遗，赶进了偏僻的角落。它们演化成一种鱼类般的动物，长着很大的鳔，以补偿硅酮组织相当高的密度；陆生动物正是由这类鱼演化而来。由于密度高、体重沉，它们往往低伏于地面，模样颇像爬行动物。为了分散沉重的负荷，通常生着三对足。石英甲壳与硅酮组织之间并无截然的分界线，这一方渐渐融入那一方。
唯有占统治地位的纯硅酮动物才能四处活动，远赴乌勒尔气候温和的赤道区域之外闯荡，因为它们既不会冻僵发硬，也不会因酷热而失能。请注意，这里的一切动物都是冷血的，体温与环境温度之差微不足道。而正因为这些动物是硅酮构成的，它们不会像受冻的蛇那样变得迟钝。

5
 Plant Life / 植物

===Original===
The plants are of the carbon-metabolism, silicate-shell type, like the primitive animals. They spread out from the equator as far as they could go before the baking polar summers killed them. They have normal seasonal growth in the temperate zones and remain dormant and frozen in the winter. At the poles there is no vegetation, not because of the cold winter, but because of the hot summer. The winter winds frequently blow over dead trees and roll them as far as the equatorial seas. Other dead vegetation, because of the highly silicious water, always gets petrified unless it is eaten first. What with the quartz-speckled hides of the living vegetation and the solid quartz of the dead, a forest is spectacular.
The silicone animals live on the plants. They chew them up, dehydrate them, and convert their silicious outer bark and carbonaceous interiors into silicones for themselves. When silicone tissue is metabolized, the carbon and hydrogen go to CO2 and H2O, which are breathed out, while the silicone goes into SiO2, which is deposited as more teeth and armor. (Compare the terrestrial octopus, which makes armor-plating out of calcium urate instead of excreting urea or uric acid.) The animals can, of course, eat each other too, or make a meal of the small carbonaceous animals of the equatorial seas.
Further note that the animals cannot digest plants when they are cold. They can eat them and store them, but the disposal of the solid water and CO2 is too difficult a problem. When they warm up, the water in the plants melts and can be disposed of, and things are simpler.

===Chinese===
植物与那些原始动物一样，属于碳代谢、硅酸盐外壳的类型。它们从赤道向外蔓延，能走多远走多远，直到酷烈的极区之夏把它们烤死为止。它们在温带地区随季节正常生长，入冬便休眠冻结。两极没有植被，倒不是因为寒冬，而是因为炎夏。冬季的风时常把枯树刮倒，一路滚到赤道诸海。其余枯死的植物，由于水中硅质极高，若不先被吃掉，终归要石化。活着的植物表皮缀满石英斑点，死了的则通体凝成实心石英——如此景象，一片森林蔚为壮观。
硅酮动物以植物为生。它们把植物嚼碎、脱水，将植物的硅质外皮与含碳内里转化成自身的硅酮。硅酮组织代谢时，碳和氢变成 CO2 与 H2O，随呼吸排出；硅则变成 SiO2，沉积为更多的牙齿与甲壳。（试比较地球上的章鱼：它不排泄尿素或尿酸，而是用尿酸钙造出甲板。）当然，这些动物也可以彼此相食，或者拿赤道诸海那些小小的含碳动物当一顿美餐。
还要注意，这些动物浑身冰凉时无法消化植物。它们可以把植物吃下、贮存起来，但处置固态的水和 CO2 实在是个太棘手的难题。等到身体转暖，植物里的水融化，可以排掉了，事情便简单了。

## II The Fluorine Planet / 第二部分 氟星

1
 The Star and Planet / 恒星与行星

===Original===
The planet named Niflheim is the fourth planet of Nu Puppis, right angle 6:36, declension −43:09; B8 type star, blue-white and hot, 148 light years distant from Earth, which will require a speed in excess of light to reach it.
Niflheim is 462,000,000 miles from its primary, a little less than the distance of Jupiter from our sun. It thus does not receive too great a total amount of energy, but what it does receive is of high potential, a large fraction of it being in the ultraviolet and higher frequencies. (Watch out for really super-special sunburn, etc., on unwarned personnel.)
The gravity of Niflheim is approximately 1 g, the atmospheric pressure approximately 1 atmosphere, and the average ambient temperature about −60° C; −76° F.

===Chinese===
名为尼福尔海姆（Niflheim）的行星，是船尾座ν（Nu Puppis）的第四颗行星；该恒星赤经 6:36，赤纬 −43:09，属 B8 型恒星，色蓝白而炽热，距地球 148 光年——要抵达那里，非得有超光速的速度不可。
尼福尔海姆距其主星 462,000,000 英里，略短于木星到我们太阳的距离。因此它接收的能量总量并不算太大，但所得到的能量位势很高，其中很大一部分位于紫外及更高的频段。（当心未获警示的人员会被晒出货真价实的特级灼伤，等等。）
尼福尔海姆的重力约为 1 个 g，气压约为 1 个大气压，平均环境温度约为 −60°C，即 −76°F。

2
 Atmosphere / 大气

===Original===
The oxidizer in the atmosphere is free fluorine (F2) in a rather low concentration, about 4 or 5 percent. With it appears a mad collection of gases. There are a few inert diluents, such as N2 (nitrogen), argon, helium, neon, etc., but the major fraction consists of CF4 (carbon tetrafluoride), BF3 (boron trifluoride), SiF4 (silicon tetrafluoride), PF5 (phosphorous pentafluoride), SF6 (sulphur hexafluoride) and probably others. In other words, the fluorides of all the nonmetals that can form fluorides. The phosphorous pentafluoride rains out when the weather gets cold. There is also free oxygen, but no chlorine. That would be liquid except in very hot weather. It sometimes appears combined with fluorine in chlorine trifluoride. The atmosphere has a slight yellowish tinge.

===Chinese===
大气中的氧化剂是游离氟（F2），浓度相当低，约 4% 到 5%。伴随它出现的，是五花八门的一大群怪气体。其中有少量惰性稀释气体，如 N2（氮气）、氩、氦、氖等，但主要成分是 CF4（四氟化碳）、BF3（三氟化硼）、SiF4（四氟化硅）、PF5（五氟化磷）、SF6（六氟化硫），可能还有别的。换句话说，凡是能生成氟化物的非金属，它们的氟化物这里一应俱全。天气转冷时，五氟化磷便化作雨落下来。此外还有游离氧，却没有氯。氯这东西除非在极热的天气，否则本该是液体。它有时与氟化合，以三氟化氯的姿态现身。大气略带一抹淡黄。

3
 Soil and Geology / 土壤与地质

===Original===
Above the metallic core of the planet, the lithosphere consists exclusively of fluorides of the metals. There are no oxides, sulfides, silicates or chlorides. There are small deposits of such things as bromine trifluoride, but these have no great importance. Since fluorides are weak mechanically, the terrain is flattish. Nothing tough like granite to build mountains out of. Since the fluoride ion is colorless, the color of the soil depends upon the predominant metal in the region. As most of the light metals also have colorless ions, the colored rocks are rather rare.

===Chinese===
在该行星的金属核心之上，岩石圈全由金属氟化物构成。没有氧化物、硫化物、硅酸盐，也没有氯化物。有三氟化溴之类的小型矿床，但无足轻重。氟化物机械强度低，因此地形相当平缓。没有花岗岩那样坚硬的东西可用来堆山造岭。氟离子无色，故土壤的颜色取决于当地占主导的金属。而大多数轻金属的离子同样无色，所以带色的岩石颇为罕见。

4
 The Waters Under the Earth / 地下之水

===Original===
They consist of liquid hydrofluoric acid (HF). It melts at −83° C and boils at 19.4° C. In it are dissolved varying quantities of metallic and nonmetallic fluorides, such as boron trifluoride, sodium fluoride, etc. When the oceans and lakes freeze, they do so from the bottom up, so there is no layer of ice over free liquid.

===Chinese===
这些水由液态的氢氟酸（HF）构成。氢氟酸在 −83°C 熔化，在 19.4°C 沸腾。其中溶有数量不等的金属与非金属氟化物，如三氟化硼、氟化钠等。海洋与湖泊冻结时，是从底往上冻的，因此自由液体的表面不会有冰层覆盖。

5
 Plants and Plant Metabolism / 植物与植物代谢

===Original===
The plants function by photosynthesis, taking HF as water from the soil, and carbon tetrafluoride as the equivalent of carbon dioxide from the air to produce chain compounds, such as:
and at the same time liberating free fluorine. This reaction could only take place on a planet receiving lots of ultraviolet because so much energy is needed to break up carbon tetrafluoride and hydrofluoric acid. The plant catalyst (doubling for the magnesium in chlorophyll) is nickel. The plants are colored in various ways. They get their metals from the soil.

===Chinese===
植物靠光合作用运作：从土壤中摄取 HF 充当水分，从空气中摄取四氟化碳充当二氧化碳的等价物，以生成链状化合物，例如：
——与此同时释放出游离氟。这一反应只可能发生在接收大量紫外线的行星上，因为分解四氟化碳与氢氟酸，需要的能量实在太多。植物体内的催化剂（顶替叶绿素中镁的职位）是镍。这些植物色彩各异，它们的金属取自土壤。

6
 Animals and Animal Metabolism / 动物与动物代谢

===Original===
Animals depend upon two main reactions for their energy, and for the construction of their harder tissues. The soft tissues are about the same as the plant molecules, but the hard tissues are produced by the reaction:
resulting in a teflon boned and shelled organism. He’s going to be tough to do much with. Diatoms leave strata of powdered teflon. The main energy reaction is:
The blood catalyst metal is titanium, which results in colorless arterial blood and violet veinous, as the titanium flips back and forth between tri and tetravalent states.

===Chinese===
动物依靠两个主要反应来获取能量、构筑较硬的组织。其软组织与植物的分子大致相同，硬组织则由下述反应生成：
——反应产物是一种骨骼与外壳皆由特氟隆（teflon）构成的生物。想拿它派什么用场，可不容易。硅藻死后留下一层层特氟隆粉末的地层。主要的能量反应则是：
血液中的催化金属是钛；钛在三价与四价之间来回跳变，于是动脉血无色，静脉血呈紫色。

7
 Effect on Intruding Items / 对闯入之物的影响

===Original===
Water decomposes into oxygen and hydrofluoric acid. All organic matter (earth type) converts into oxygen, carbon tetrafluoride, hydrofluoric acid, etc., with more or less speed. A rubber gas mask lasts about an hour. Glass first frosts and then disappears. Plastics act like rubber, only a little slower. The heavy metals, iron, nickel, copper, monel, etc., stand up well, forming an insoluble coat of fluorides at first and then doing nothing else.

===Chinese===
水会分解成氧和氢氟酸。一切（地球型）有机物都会或快或慢地转化为氧、四氟化碳、氢氟酸等物。橡胶防毒面具大约撑得了一个钟头。玻璃先结出霜面，继而整个消失。塑料的表现与橡胶相仿，只是慢上一些。重金属——铁、镍、铜、蒙乃尔合金（monel）等——则挺得住，先形成一层不溶的氟化物包衣，此后便再无变化。

8
 Why Go There? / 为何要去那里？

===Original===
Large natural crystals of fluorides, such as calcium difluoride, titanium tetrafluoride, zirconium tetrafluoride, are extremely useful in various forms of optical instruments. Uranium appears as uranium hexafluoride, all ready for the diffusion process. Compounds of such nonmetals as boron are obtainable from the atmosphere in high purity with very little trouble. All metallurgy must be electrical. There are considerable deposits of beryllium, and they occur in high concentration in its ores.

===Chinese===
大块的天然氟化物晶体，如二氟化钙、四氟化钛、四氟化锆，在形形色色的光学仪器中极为有用。铀以六氟化铀的形态出现，一切现成，正好直接送去进行扩散法处理。硼之类非金属的化合物可从大气中获取，纯度极高，毫不费事。一切冶金都必须是电冶金。铍的矿藏相当可观，而且矿石中铍的品位很高。

## III Four-and-Twenty Geek Heads / 第三章 廿四颗土佬头颅

===Original===
Governor-General Sidney Harrington sat on the comfortably upholstered bench on the dais of the Audience Hall, flanked by von Schlichten and Eric Blount. He didn't look particularly regal, even on that high seat—with his ruddy outdoorsman's face and his ragged gray mustache and his old tweed coat spotted with pipe-ashes, he might have been any of the dozen-odd country-gentleman neighbors of von Schlichten's boyhood in the Argentine. But then, to a Terran, any of the kings of Uller would have looked like a freak birth in a lizard-house at a zoo; it was hard to guess what impression Harrington would make on an Ulleran.

He took the false palate and tongue-clicker, officially designated as an "enunciator, Ulleran" and, colloquially, as a geek-speaker, out of his coat pocket and shoved it into his mouth. Von Schlichten and Blount put in theirs, and Harrington pressed the floor-button with his toe. After a brief interval, the wide doors at the other end of the hall slid open, and the Konkrookan notables, attended by a dozen Company native-officers and a guard of Kragan Rifles, entered. The honor-guard advanced in two columns; between them marched an unclad and heavily armed native carrying an ornate spear with a three-foot blade upright in front of him with all four hands. It was the Konkrookan Spear of State; it represented the proxy-presence of King Jaikark. Behind it stalked Gurgurk, the Konkrookan equivalent of Prime Minister or Grand Vizier; he wore a gold helmet and a thing like a string-vest made of gold wire, and carried a long sword with a two-hand grip, a pair of Terran automatics built for a hand with six four-knuckled fingers, and a pair of matched daggers. He was considerably past the Ulleran prime of life—seventy or eighty, to judge from the worn appearance of his opal teeth, the color of his skin, and the predominantly reddish tint of his quartz-speckles. An immature Ulleran would be a very light gray, white under the arms, and his quartz-specks would run from white to pale yellow. The retinue of nobles behind Gurgurk ran through the whole spectrum, from a princeling who was almost oyster-gray to old Ghroghrank, the Keegarkan Ambassador, who was even blacker and more red-speckled than Gurgurk. All of them carried about as much ironmongery as the Prime Minister—the pistols were all Terran, and the swords and daggers were mostly made either on Terra or at the Terran-operated steelworks on Volund.

Four slaves brought up the rear carrying an ornately inlaid box on poles. When the spear-bearer reached the exact middle of the hall, he halted and grounded his regalia-weapon with a thump. Gurgurk came up and halted a couple of paces behind and to the left of the spear, and all the other nobles drew up in two curved lines some ten paces to the rear, with considerable pushing and jostling and a sotto voce argument, with overtones of weapon-fingering, about precedence. All, that is, but Ghroghrank and another noble, who came up and planted themselves beside Gurgurk. Von Schlichten regarded the assemblage sourly through his monocle. Maybe Sid Harrington *did* look regal, after all.

The Governor-General rose slowly and descended from the dais, advancing to within ten paces of the Spear, von Schlichten and Blount accompanying him. Out of the corner of his eye, von Schlichten watched a couple of Kragan mercenaries with fifty-shot machine-rifles move unobtrusively to positions from whence they could, if necessary, spray the visitors with bullets without endangering the Terrans.

===Chinese===
总督希德尼·哈灵顿（Governor-General Sidney Harrington）坐在谒见厅（Audience Hall）高台的软垫长椅上，左右是冯·施利希滕（von Schlichten）和埃里克·布朗特（Eric Blount）。即便坐在那样的高座上，他看上去也谈不上有什么王者气派——一张常年跑野外的红润脸膛，一蓬参差斑白的小胡子，一件沾满烟斗灰的旧花呢外套，活脱脱就是冯·施利希滕少年时在阿根廷左近那一打乡下绅士邻居中的随便哪一个。不过话说回来，在地球人（Terran）眼里，乌勒尔的任何一位国王，瞧着都像动物园蜥蜴馆里产下的怪胎；哈灵顿会给乌勒尔人（Ulleran）留下个什么印象，实在难以悬揣。

他从外套口袋里掏出那副假腭连舌响器——官方定名「乌勒尔语发音器」（enunciator, Ulleran），俗名就叫土佬语发声器（geek-speaker）——塞进嘴里。冯·施利希滕和布朗特也各自装上，哈灵顿用脚尖踩下地板上的按钮。片刻之后，大厅另一端的两扇大门滑开，孔克鲁克（Konkrook）的显贵们在十二名公司土著军官和一队克拉冈来复枪团（Kragan Rifles）卫兵的随护下走了进来。仪仗队分作两列前进；两列之间，一名赤身露体、重装荷械的土著迈步而行，用全部四只手在身前竖捧着一支华丽的礼矛，矛刃足有三英尺长。那是孔克鲁克的国矛（Spear of State），代表贾伊卡克（Jaikark）王亲临在场。矛后昂首阔步跟着古古尔克（Gurgurk）——相当于孔克鲁克的首相，或者说大维齐尔；他头戴金盔，身上罩着一件金线编成的、状如网眼背心的物件，佩一柄双手长剑、两把专为六指、每指四节的手掌打造的地球自动手枪，外加一对成对的匕首。他早已过了乌勒尔人的盛年——看那口磨损的蛋白石牙齿、皮肤的颜色，还有石英斑点中偏红的色调，约莫七十到八十岁了。未成年的乌勒尔人该是很浅的灰色，腋下发白，石英斑点从白到淡黄不等。古古尔克身后那队贵族的肤色则铺满整个色谱：从一位近乎牡蛎灰的少年亲王，到老格罗格林克（Ghroghrank）——基加克（Keegark）驻节大使，他比古古尔克更黑，红斑也更密。他们个个携带的铁器家伙都跟这位首相不相上下——手枪清一色是地球货，刀剑匕首则多半产自地球本土，或者出自沃隆德（Volund）上地球人经营的钢铁厂。

四名奴隶抬着一口用木杠架起、镶嵌华美的箱子走在队尾。执矛人行至大厅正中站定，把那杆礼矛往地上重重一顿。古古尔克上前，在矛后偏左两步处停住；其余贵族在后面约十步处排成两道弧线，其间推推搡搡，为位次先后低声争执，指间拨弄兵刃的声息隐约可闻。唯有格罗格林克和另一名贵族例外——他俩走上前来，在古古尔克身旁站定。冯·施利希滕透过单片眼镜没好气地打量着这一干人等。这么看，席德·哈灵顿倒说不定真有几分王者气象。

总督缓缓起身，走下高台，行进到距国矛十步之内；冯·施利希滕和布朗特随行在侧。冯·施利希滕用眼角余光留意着两名挎五十弹匣自动步枪的克拉冈雇佣兵，看他们不动声色地移到有利位置——必要时可从那里向来客倾泻弹雨，而不致危及地球人。

===Original===
"Welcome, Gurgurk," Harrington gibbered through his false palate. "The Company is honored by this visit."

"I come in the name of my royal master, His Sublime and Ineffable Majesty, Jaikark the Seventeenth, King of Konkrook and of all the lands of the Konk Isthmus," Gurgurk squeaked and clicked. "I have the honor to bring with me the Lord Ghroghrank, Ambassador of King Orgzild of Keegark to the court of my royal master."

"And I," Ghroghrank said, after being suitably welcomed, "am honored to be accompanied by Prince Gorkrink, special envoy from my master, his Royal and Imperial Majesty King Orgzild, who is in your city to receive the shipment of power-metal my royal master has been honored to be permitted to purchase from the Company."

More protocol about welcoming Gorkrink. Then Gurgurk cleared his throat with a series of barking sounds.

===Chinese===
"欢迎，古古尔克，"哈灵顿含着假腭叽里咕噜地说。"贵客到此，公司深感荣幸。"

"我奉我家王主——至高无上、不可名状的陛下，孔克鲁克及孔克地峡全境之主贾伊卡克十七世——之名而来，"古古尔克尖声咔嗒地说道。"我有幸偕格罗格林克大人同来，他是基加克奥格齐尔德（Orgzild）王驻我家王主宫廷的大使。"

格罗格林克在受到得体的欢迎之后说道："而我，有幸由戈克林克（Gorkrink）亲王陪同——他是我主上、皇家帝国至尊奥格齐尔德王陛下派来的特使，此刻正在贵城接收那批动力金属（power-metal）；承蒙公司俯允我家王主购进此物，实属无上荣光。"

接着又是欢迎戈克林克的一番仪节。然后，古古尔克发出一连串犬吠似的声响，清了清喉咙。

===Original===
"My royal master, His Sublime and Ineffable Majesty, is prostrated with grief," he stated solemnly. "Were his sorrow not so overwhelming, he would have come in His Own Sacred Person to express the pain and shame which he feels that people of the Company should be set upon and endangered in the streets of the royal city."

If he weren't doped to the ears, von Schlichten substituted mentally. There was a native drug which had, on its users, the combined effects of hashish, heroin and yohimbine; Jaikark and all his court circle were addicts. He probably hadn't even heard of the riot.

"The soldiers of His Sublime and Ineffable Majesty came most promptly to the aid of the troops of the Company, did they not, General von Schlichten?" Harrington asked.

"Within minutes, Your Excellency," von Schlichten replied gravely. "Their promptness, valor, and efficiency were most exemplary."

===Chinese===
"我家王主、至高无上不可名状的陛下，正因哀恸而卧不能起，"他庄严地宣告。"若非悲痛如此压倒一切，陛下必当亲身圣驾前来，一抒他的痛心与羞愧——公司的子民，竟在王城街头横遭袭击、身陷险境。"

他要不是磕药磕到了耳朵根上——冯·施利希滕在心里替他把话改了。当地有一种土产药物，对服用者兼有哈希什、海洛因和育亨宾（yohimbine）三者的复合效力；贾伊卡克和满朝亲贵个个都是瘾君子。那场暴乱，他八成压根就没听说过。

"至高无上不可名状的陛下麾下的士兵极其迅速地赶来援助公司部队，是不是啊，冯·施利希滕将军？"哈灵顿问。

"几分钟之内就赶到了，总督阁下，"冯·施利希滕郑重答道。"他们的迅捷、骁勇与干练，堪为楷模。"

===Original===
Gurgurk spoke at length, expressing himself as delighted, on behalf of his royal master, at hearing such high praise from so distinguished a soldier. Eric Blount then contributed a short speech, beseeching the gods that the deep and beautiful friendship existing between the Chartered Uller Company and His Sublime etcetera would continue unimpaired, and that His Sublime etcetera would enjoy long life and peaceful reign, managing, by a trick of Konkrookan grammar, to imply that the second would be conditional upon the first. The Keegarkan Ambassador then spoke his piece, expressing on behalf of King Orgzild the deepest regret that the people of the Company should be so molested, and managing to hint that things like that simply didn't happen at Keegark.

The Prince Gorkrink then spoke briefly, in sympathy for the great and good friend of all Ulleran peoples, Mohammed Ferriera, who had been injured, and hoping that he would soon enjoy full health again. He also managed to convey King Orgzild's pleasure at having obtained the plutonium. Von Schlichten noticed that a few of his more recent quartz-specks were slightly greenish in tinge, a sure sign that he had, not long ago, been exposed to the fluorine-tainted air which men and geeks alike breathed on Niflheim. When a geek prince hired out as a laborer for a year on Niflheim, he did so for only one purpose—to learn Terran technologies.

Gurgurk then announced that so enormous a crime against the friends of His Sublime etcetera had not been allowed to go unpunished, signaling behind him with one of his lower hands for the box to be brought forward. The slaves carried it to the front, set it down, and opened it, taking from it a rug which they spread on the floor. On this, from the box, they placed twenty-four newly severed opal-grinning heads, in four neat rows. They had all been freshly scrubbed and polished, but they still smelled like crushed cockroaches.

The three Terrans looked at them gravely. A double-dozen heads was standard payment for an attack in which no Terran had been killed. Ostensibly, they were the heads of the ringleaders: in practice, they were usually lopped from the first two-dozen prisoners or overage slaves at hand, without regard for whether the victims had even heard of the crime which they were expiating. If the Extraterrestrial's Rights Association were really serious about the rights of these geeks, they'd advocate booting out all these native princes and turning the whole planet over to the Company. That had been the Terran Federation's idea, from the beginning; why else give the Company's chief representative the title of Governor-General?

===Chinese===
古古尔克接着发表长篇大论，代他家王主表示：能从如此杰出的一位军人嘴里听到这般盛赞，不胜欣悦。随后埃里克·布朗特献上一段简短演说，祈求诸神保佑特许乌勒尔公司（Chartered Uller Company）与至高无上的等等陛下之间深厚而美好的友谊长存不损，保佑至高无上的等等陛下万寿无疆、君位安宁——并借孔克鲁克语法上的一点巧劲，暗示后者须以前者为前提。基加克大使接着致辞，代表奥格齐尔德王对公司子民竟受如此滋扰深表遗憾，还顺带暗示：这等事情，在基加克是断然不会发生的。

随后戈克林克亲王简短致词，向全体乌勒尔各族人民伟大而善良的朋友、负伤在身的穆罕默德·费雷拉（Mohammed Ferriera）表示慰问，祝他早日完全康复。他还着意传达了奥格齐尔德王获得那批钚的欣悦之情。冯·施利希滕注意到，他身上新添的几粒石英斑点微微泛绿——这是确凿的迹象，说明不久之前，他曾在尼福尔海姆（Niflheim）那种掺氟的空气里呼吸过；在尼福尔海姆，地球人和土佬（geek）吸的都是这一口气。一个土佬亲王若去尼福尔海姆受雇做一年劳工，为的只有一件事——偷学地球人的技术。

古古尔克随即宣布：如此滔天的罪行，既然冒犯了至高无上的等等陛下的朋友们，便绝不容姑纵、不受惩处；说着，他用下面的一只手朝身后打手势，示意把箱子抬上前来。奴隶们把箱子抬到前面，放下，打开，先从里面取出一方地毯铺在地上，然后从箱中搬出二十四颗新斩下的头颅，蛋白石牙齿龇露着，整整齐齐码成四排。颗颗都刚经洗刷抛光，可仍旧透着一股踩烂的蟑螂味。

三位地球人神色凝重地望着这些头颅。凡无地球人丧命的袭击事件，照例赔付两打头颅。名义上，这些都是为首暴徒的脑袋；实际上，多半是从手边最先抓到的两打囚犯或超龄奴隶身上剁下来的，全不管这些受戮者可曾听说过自己正在抵偿的那桩罪。地外种族权利协会（Extraterrestrials' Rights Association）若是真心计较这些土佬的权利，就该主张把这一众土著王公统统撵走，把整颗行星移交给公司。这本就是地球联邦（Terran Federation）打一开始的用意；不然，何必给公司的首席代表安上总督（Governor-General）的头衔？

===Original===
There was another long speech from Gurgurk, with the nobles behind him murmuring antiphonal agreement—standard procedure, for which there was a standard pun, geek chorus—and a speech of response from Sid Harrington. Standing stiffly through the whole rigamarole, von Schlichten waited for it to end, as finally it did.

They walked back from the door, whence they had escorted the delegation, and stood looking down at the saurian heads on the rug. Harrington raised his voice and called to a Kragan sergeant whose chevrons were painted on all four arms.

"Take this carrion out and stuff it in the incinerator," he ordered. "If any of you think you can clean up this rug and this box, you're welcome to them."

"Wait a moment," von Schlichten told the sergeant. Then he disgorged and pouched his geek-speaker. "See that head, there?" he asked, rolling it over with his toe. "I killed that geek, myself, with my pistol, while Them and Hid were getting Ferriera into the car. Miss Quinton killed that one with the bolo; see where she chopped him on the back of the neck? The cut that took off the head was a little low, and missed it. And Hid O'Leary stuck a knife in that one." He walked around the rug, turning heads over with his foot. "This was cut-rate head-payment; they just slashed off two-dozen heads at the scene of the riot. I don't like this butchery of worn-out slaves and petty thieves any better than anybody else, but this I don't like either. Six months ago, Gurgurk wouldn't have tried to pull anything like this. Now he's laughing up his nonexistent sleeve at us."

===Chinese===
古古尔克又发表了一通长篇演说，身后众贵族低声应和，此起彼落——这是标准程序，还配有一句现成的俏皮话，叫「土佬合唱团」（geek chorus，戏仿「希腊合唱队」）——随后席德·哈灵顿致答辞。冯·施利希滕直挺挺地站着熬完全套冗文缛节，一直等到它终于收场。

他们从大门那边踱了回来——方才就是在门口送走代表团的——站定脚，俯视地毯上那些蜥蜴般的头颅。哈灵顿提高嗓门，唤来一名克拉冈中士，此公四条手臂上都漆着军衔角标。

"把这堆臭烂货抬出去，塞进焚化炉，"他下令道。"你们谁要是觉得自己能把这块地毯和这口箱子拾掇干净，尽管拿去好了。"

"且慢，"冯·施利希滕对中士说。随即他吐出土佬语发声器，装回袋里。"看见那颗脑袋没有？"他用脚尖把一颗头翻过来。"那个土佬是我亲自动用手枪打死的，当时地米（Them）和希德（Hid）正把费雷拉往车上抬。那颗是昆顿小姐（Miss Quinton）用波洛砍刀（bolo）砍的；看见她劈在他后颈上的那一刀没有？后来割下头颅的那一刀低了那么一点，没落在同一个地方。还有那颗，是希德·奥利里（Hid O'Leary）捅了一刀的。"他绕着地毯走，用脚把头颅一颗颗翻看过去。"这是打折的头颅赔付；他们不过是暴乱现场胡乱剁了两打脑袋交差。拿超龄奴隶和小毛贼开刀的这番屠戮，我不比任何人看得惯；可如今这一手，我同样看不惯。半年之前，古古尔克绝不敢来这一套。如今他正躲在那并不存在的袖管里，冲我们窃笑呢。"

===Original===
"That's what I've been preaching, all along," Eric Blount took up after him. "These geeks need having the fear of Terra thrown into them."

"Oh, nonsense, Eric; you're just as bad as Carlos, here!" Harrington tut-tutted. "Next, you'll be saying that we ought to depose Jaikark and take control ourselves."

"Well, what's wrong with that, for an idea?" von Schlichten demanded. "Don't you think we could? Our Kragans could go through that army of Jaikark's like fast neutrons through toilet-paper."

"My God!" Harrington exploded. "Don't let me hear that kind of talk again! We're not conquistadores; we're employees of a business concern, here to make money honestly, by exchanging goods and services with these people. …"

===Chinese===
"这话我一直都在讲，"埃里克·布朗特接过话头。"这帮土佬，就得把对地球的恐惧狠狠打进他们骨头里去。"

"哦，胡扯，埃里克；你跟这儿的卡洛斯（Carlos）一样不成器！"哈灵顿啧啧连声。"再往下说，你就要主张咱们该废黜贾伊卡克、自己接管全局了。"

"哦？这个主意又有什么不好？"冯·施利希滕诘问。"你当我们办不到？咱们的克拉冈人冲贾伊卡克那支军队，就像快中子穿过厕纸。"

"我的天！"哈灵顿炸了。"这种话别让我再听见一遍！咱们不是征服者（conquistadores）；咱们是一家商号的雇员，到这儿来，是要靠同这些人交换货物与劳务、老老实实挣钱来的。……"

===Original===
He turned and walked away, out of the Audience Hall, leaving von Schlichten and Blount to watch the removal of the geek-heads.

"You know, I went a little too far," von Schlichten confessed. "Or too fast, rather. He's got to be conditioned to accept that idea."

"We can't go too slowly, either," Blount replied. "If we wait for him to change his mind, it'll be the same as waiting for him to retire. And that'll be waiting too long."

Von Schlichten nodded seriously. "Did you notice the green specks in the hide of that Prince Gorkrink?" he asked. "He's just come back from Niflheim. Not on the *Pretoria*, I don't think. Probably on the *Canberra*, three months ago."

===Chinese===
他一转身走了出去，出了谒见厅，撇下冯·施利希滕和布朗特看着人把那些土佬头颅抬走。

"你知道，我方才有点过头了，"冯·施利希滕坦白道。"或者说，是太快了。总得先把他渐渐调适得能接受那个念头才行。"

"可咱们也慢不得，"布朗特答道。"要是等他回心转意，那就跟等他退休没什么两样。那可就等得太久了。"

冯·施利希滕郑重地点了点头。"你注意到那位戈克林克亲王皮上的绿斑没有？"他问。"他刚从尼福尔海姆回来。我想，不是搭「比勒陀利亚」号（Pretoria）来的。多半是三个月前，乘「堪培拉」号（Canberra）来的。"

===Original===
"And he's here to get that plutonium, and ship it to Keegark on the *Oom Paul Kruger*," Blount considered. "I wonder just what he learned, on Niflheim."

"I wonder just what's going on at Keegark," von Schlichten said. "Orgzild's pulled down a regular First-Century-model iron curtain. You know, four of our best native Intelligence operatives have been murdered in Keegark in the last three months, and six more have just vanished there."

"Well, I'm going there in a few days, myself, to talk to Orgzild about this spaceport deal," Blount said. "I'll have a talk with Hendrik Lemoyne and MacKinnon. And I'll see what I can find out for myself."

"Well, let's go have a drink," von Schlichten suggested, consulting his watch. "About time for a cocktail."

===Chinese===
"而他到这儿来，就是为了取那批钚，再用「奥姆·保罗·克鲁格」号（Oom Paul Kruger）运去基加克，"布朗特思忖道。"真不知道他在尼福尔海姆究竟学到了些什么。"

"我倒想知道基加克那边眼下在搞什么名堂，"冯·施利希滕说。"奥格齐尔德拉起了一道地道的第一世纪款式的铁幕。你知道，过去三个月里，我们最得力的四名土著情报员在基加克遇害，另有六名干脆在那里失踪了。"

"过几天我自己就要过去一趟，找奥格齐尔德谈那桩太空港交易，"布朗特说。"我会同亨德里克·勒莫因（Hendrik Lemoyne）和麦金农（MacKinnon）谈谈。我也要亲自打探打探，看能摸出点什么来。"

"走吧，去喝一杯，"冯·施利希滕看了看表，提议道。"差不多是喝一杯鸡尾酒的时候了。"

## IV If You Read It in Stanley-Browne / 第四章 只要出自斯坦利-布朗的书

===Original===
Von Schlichten and Blount entered the bar together﻿—the Broadway Room, decorated in gleaming plastics and chromium in enthusiastic if slightly inaccurate imitation of a First Century New York nightclub. There were no native servants to spoil the illusion, such as it was: the service was fully automatic. Going to a bartending machine, von Schlichten dialed the cocktail they had decided upon and inserted his key to charge the drinks to his account, filling a four-portion jug.

As they turned away, they almost collided with Hideyoshi O’Leary and Paula Quinton. The girl wore a long-sleeved gown to conceal a bandage on her right wrist, and her face was rather heavily powdered in spots; otherwise she looked none the worse for recent experiences.

“Well, you seem to have gotten yourself repaired, Miss Quinton,” he greeted her. “Feel better, now?﻿ ﻿… Miss Quinton, this is Lieutenant-Governor Blount. Eric, Miss Paula Quinton.”

“Delighted, Miss Quinton,” Blount said. “Carlos tells us he found you standing over poor Mohammed Ferriera, fighting like a commando. How is Mohammed, by the way? No danger, I hope; we all like him.”

Mohammed Ferriera was still unconscious, the girl reported; he had a minor concussion, but the medics were not greatly disturbed, and expected him to be fully recovered in a few weeks. Von Schlichten invited her and her escort to join him and Blount. Colonel O’Leary was carrying a cocktail jug and a couple of glasses; finding a table out of the worst of the noise, they all sat down together.

===Chinese===
冯·施利希滕和布朗特（Blount）一同走进酒吧——「百老汇厅」（Broadway Room），那地方用亮闪闪的塑料和镀铬件装点，热情有余却略有走样地模仿着第一世纪（First Century）的纽约夜总会。店里没有土著侍者来戳破这份幻象——姑且不论幻象本身成色如何：待客服务全是自动的。冯·施利希滕走到一台调酒机前，拨好他们商定的那款鸡尾酒，插入钥匙把酒钱记到自己的账上，灌满了一壶四份装的酒。

两人转身要走，差点撞上秀吉·奥利里（Hideyoshi O’Leary）和保拉·昆顿（Paula Quinton）。姑娘穿了件长袖礼服，遮住右腕上的绷带，脸上几处扑了颇厚的粉；除此之外，她看上去毫发无损，全不像刚经历了那场劫难的样子。

“哟，昆顿小姐，你看来已经把自己修补好了，”他向她招呼道，“现在感觉好些了吧？……昆顿小姐，这位是副总督布朗特。埃里克，这位是保拉·昆顿小姐。”

“幸会，昆顿小姐，”布朗特说。“卡洛斯告诉我们，他发现你的时候，你正站在可怜的穆罕默德·费雷拉（Mohammed Ferriera）身旁，打得像个突击队员。对了，穆罕默德情况怎么样？我想应该没有性命之忧吧；我们大家都喜欢他。”

姑娘说，穆罕默德·费雷拉仍未苏醒；他有些轻度脑震荡，但医护人员并不太担心，预计几周后便能完全康复。冯·施利希滕邀请她和她的男伴同自己与布朗特坐到一处。奥利里上校（Colonel O’Leary）已经端着一壶鸡尾酒和两只杯子；他们寻了张桌子，避开闹声最凶的地方，一同坐了下来。

===Original===
“I suppose you think it’s a joke, our being nearly murdered by the people we came to help,” Paula began, a trifle defensively.

“Not a very funny joke,” von Schlichten told her. “It’s been played on us till it’s lost its humor.”

“Yes, geek ingratitude’s an old story to all of us,” Blount agreed. “You stay on this planet very long and you’ll see what I mean.”

“You call them that, too?” she asked, as though disappointed in him. “Maybe if you stopped calling them geeks, they wouldn’t resent you the way they do. You know, that’s a nasty name; in the First Century Pre-Atomic, it designated a degraded person who performed some sort of revolting public exhibition.﻿ ﻿…”

“Biting off live chickens’ heads, in a sideshow wild-man act,” Hideyoshi O’Leary supplied. “When you get up north, watch how the peasants kill these little things like six-legged iguanas that they raise for food.”

===Chinese===
“我看你们八成觉得这是个笑话——我们差点被自己前来帮助的人谋害，”保拉开口道，语气里带着几分辩卫的意味。

“这笑话可不大好笑，”冯·施利希滕对她说。“这一套在我们身上开了一回又一回，早就笑不出来了。”

“是啊，土佬（geek）忘恩负义，对我们大家来说早已是老生常谈，”布朗特附和道。“你在这颗行星上待久了，就会明白我的意思。”

“你们也这么叫他们？”她问，语气像是对他有些失望。“也许你们要是别再管他们叫土佬，他们就不会那样怨恨你们了。要知道，那是个恶毒的字眼；在原子前纪元的第一世纪，它指的是一种自甘下贱的人，专做什么令人作呕的公开表演。……”

“就是在杂耍班的『野人』戏码里，活生生咬掉鸡头，”秀吉·奥利里接话道。“等你到了北方，留意看看庄稼人怎么宰那种六条腿、活像鬣蜥的小东西——那是他们养来当吃食的。”

===Original===
“That isn’t the reason, though,” von Schlichten said. “As we use it, the word’s pure onomatopoeia. You’ve learned some of the languages; you know what they sound like. *Geek-geek-geek.*”

“As far as that goes, you know what the geek name for a Terran is?” Blount asked. “*Suddabit.*”

She looked puzzled for a moment, then slipped in her enunciator. Even in the absence of any native, she used her handkerchief to mask the act.

“Suddabit,” she said, distinctly. “Sud-da-a-bit.” Taking out the geek-speaker, she put it away. “Why, that’s exactly how they’d pronounce it!”

“And don’t tell me you haven’t heard it before,” O’Leary said. “The geeks were screaming it at you, over on Seventy-second Street, this afternoon. *Znidd suddabit*; kill the Terrans. That’s Rakkeed the Prophet’s whole gospel.”

===Chinese===
“不过，我们这么叫倒不是为了那个缘故，”冯·施利希滕说。“在我们嘴里，这个词纯属拟声。你学过几门他们的语言；总知道他们说起话来是什么动静——*Geek-geek-geek*。”

“话既说到这份上，你可知道土佬管地球人（Terran）叫什么？”布朗特问。“*Suddabit*。”

她一时面露困惑，随即把乌勒尔语发音器（enunciator）含进嘴里。四下虽无一个土著在场，她仍用手帕掩住了这个动作。

“Suddabit，”她一字一顿地说，“Sud-da-a-bit。”说罢取出口中的土佬语发声器，收了起来。“哎呀，他们念出来正是这个动静！”

“还说你没听人喊过呢，”奥利里说。“今天下午，在第七十二街那头，土佬们冲着你尖叫的就是这个。*Znidd suddabit*（土佬语：杀死地球人）。这就是先知拉基德（Rakkeed）那套福音的全部。”

===Original===
“So you see,” Eric Blount rammed home the moral, “this is just another case of nobody with any right to call anybody else’s kettle black.﻿ ﻿… Cigarette?”

“Thank you.” She leaned toward the lighter-flame O’Leary had snapped into being. “I suspect that of being a principle you’d like me to bear in mind at the polar mines, when I see, let’s say, some laborer being beaten by a couple of overseers with three foot lengths of three-quarter-inch steel cable.”

“Well, you could also remember that a native’s skin is about half an inch thick, and a good deal tougher than a human’s,” von Schlichten told her. “And it wouldn’t hurt any if you found out how these laborers are treated at home. Mostly they’re serfs hired from the big landowners; it’s a fact you can easily verify that permission to join the labor-companies at the polar mines is regarded as a privilege, granted as a reward or denied as a punishment. And most of the geek landowners are bitterly critical of the way we treat our labor at the mines; they claim we make them dissatisfied with the treatment they get at home.”

“Of course, they’re always glad to have the peasants taken off their hands during a slack agricultural season,” Blount added, “and we train workers to handle contragravity power-equipment. I won’t deny that there’s a lot of unnecessary brutality on the part of the native foremen and overseers, which we’re trying, gradually, to eliminate. You’ll have to remember, though, that we’re dealing with a naturally brutal race.”

“Of course, mistreatment of native labor is always blamed on other natives, never on the gentle and kindly Terrans,” she replied. “That’s been S.O.P. on every planet our Association’s had any experience with.”

===Chinese===
“所以你瞧，”埃里克·布朗特把道理一锤敲实，“这不过是又一桩谁也没资格嫌别人锅底黑的公案。……来支烟？”

“谢谢。”她朝着奥利里啪地打出的火苗俯身凑了过去。“我疑心这是一条你们巴望我牢记在心的原则——好让我在极地矿区看到，比方说，某个劳工被几名监工用三英尺长、四分之三英寸粗的钢索抽打的时候，也照此办理。”

“不过呢，你也不妨记住，土著的皮肤约有半英寸厚，比人类的坚韧得多，”冯·施利希滕对她说。“再者，你去打听打听这些劳工在家乡受的是什么待遇，也没什么坏处。他们多半是从大地主手里雇来的农奴；有一个你轻易就能查证的事实：获准加入极地矿区的劳工队，被视为一种特权，可作赏赐恩准，亦可作惩罚褫夺。而大多数土佬地主对我们厚待矿上劳工的做法怨气冲天；他们声称，是我们把这帮人惯得连在家乡受的待遇都不满意了。”

“当然喽，农闲时节能把手下的农民打发出去，他们向来是乐意的，”布朗特补充道，“何况还由我们负责训练工人操纵反重力（contragravity）动力机械。我不否认，土著工头和监工手下存在大量不必要的暴行，我们正设法逐步革除。不过你也得记住，我们打交道的，是一个天性残暴的种族。”

“当然啦，虐待土著劳工的账，永远都算到别的土著头上，绝算不到温良仁厚的地球人身上，”她答道。“凡是我们地外种族权利协会（Extraterrestrials’ Rights Association）打过交道的行星，这一直是标准作业程序。”

===Original===
“Now look; you just came here from Niflheim,” von Schlichten objected. “The Company employs quite a few geeks there; how much brutality did you run into there?”

“Well, I must admit, the Ullerans who work there are very well treated. Except that I don’t think it’s right to employ any people with silicone body-tissues where they’re going to breathe fluorine-tainted air.”

“Nobody ought to be employed on that planet!” Hideyoshi O’Leary declared. “I did a two-year hitch there, when I was first commissioned in the Company service.”

“I put in two years there, too,” Blount supported him. “And I might add that that’s a year longer than any Ulleran native is ever allowed to spend on Niflheim. You know what the setup is, there, don’t you? The Terran Federation Space Navy discovered and explored both Uller and Niflheim, which made both planets public domain. The Company was originally formed to exploit Uller alone, but the Federation insisted that both planets would have to be franchised to the same company. They wanted Niflheim exploited, mainly because of the uranium-deposits there. As it turned out, the Company’s making as much money out of Niflheim as we are out of Uller.”

“What you miss is this,” von Schlichten pointed out. “On Niflheim, there are about a thousand Terrans, and not more than five hundred geeks, all employed on construction-work and in the mines, on the planet itself, working directly under Terran supervision. We use them because they have four hands, and in the power-driven contragravity armor that’s necessary there, they can manipulate more controls and do more things at once than we can. Here on Uller, at the polar mines, there are about ten thousand geeks working under five hundred Terrans, and most of the latter are engineers or technicians who don’t do supervisory work. So we have to use native foremen, and they’re guilty of what mistreatment the workers suffer.”

===Chinese===
“听我说，你刚从尼福尔海姆（Niflheim）过来，”冯·施利希滕提出异议。“乌勒尔公司（Uller Company）在那边也雇了不少土佬；你在那边撞见过多少暴行？”

“这个嘛，我必须承认，在那边干活的乌勒尔人待遇很好。只是我认为，让硅酮体质（silicone body-tissues）的人在要呼吸含氟空气的地方干活，总归不对。”

“那颗行星上根本就不该雇任何人！”秀吉·奥利里宣称。“我初次授衔、加入公司服役的时候，就在那边干过一届两年的差。”

“我也在那边待过两年，”布朗特帮腔道。“而且我还可以补一句：这比任何乌勒尔土著获准在尼福尔海姆居留的期限还多出一年。你知道那边的格局，对吧？地球联邦（Terran Federation）的太空海军发现并勘察了乌勒尔与尼福尔海姆这两颗行星，这就使两颗行星都成了公共领地。公司当初是为单独开发乌勒尔而组建的，但联邦坚持两颗行星的特许经营权必须授予同一家公司。他们要求开发尼福尔海姆，主要是冲着那里的铀矿床。结果呢，公司从尼福尔海姆赚的钱，跟我们从乌勒尔赚的一样多。”

“你没看到的是这一点，”冯·施利希滕指出。“在尼福尔海姆，大约有一千名地球人，土佬不超过五百，全数受雇于那颗行星本体上的建筑工程和矿场，直接处在地球人的监督之下。我们用他们，是因为他们长着四只手，而在那边必不可少的动力驱动反重力护甲里，他们能比我们操纵更多的控制器，同时干更多的事情。可在乌勒尔这边，极地矿区里约有一万名土佬在五百名地球人手下干活，而这五百人大多数是不担任监督职务的工程师或技术员。所以我们只好使用土著工头，工人们吃的那些虐待的苦头，罪责全在工头们身上。”

===Original===
“And remember, too,” O’Leary added, “work at the polar mines can only go on for about two months out of the year﻿—mid-September to mid-November at the Arctic, and mid-March to mid-May at the Antarctic. Naturally, things have to be done in a hurry and under pressure.”

“Well, why do you work mines at the poles? Aren’t there mineral deposits in places where you can work all year ’round?”

“Not as rich, or as accessible,” Blount said. “You know what the seasons are like, at the poles of this planet. The temperature will range from about two-fifty Fahrenheit in midsummer to a hundred and fifty below in winter. There’s the most intense sort of thermal erosion you can imagine﻿—the icecap melts in the spring to a sea, which boils away completely by the middle of the summer. There will be violent circular storms of hot wind, blowing away the light sand and dust and leaving the heavier particles of metallic ores and metals behind. Then, when the winds fall, we move in for a couple of months. It isn’t really mining, or even quarrying; we just scoop up ore from the surface, load it onto ore-boats, and fly it down to Skilk and Krink and Grank, where it’s smelted through the winter. The natives run the smelters; use the heat to thaw frozen food for themselves and their livestock while they’re melting the ore. In the north, metallurgy and food-preparation have always been combined that way.”

“Yes, if you think the natives who work at the mines feel themselves ill-treated, you might propose closing them down entirely and see what the native reaction would be,” von Schlichten told her. “Independently hired free workers can make themselves rich, by native standards, in a couple of seasons; many of the serfs pick up enough money from us in incentive-pay to buy their freedom after one season.”

===Chinese===
“还有，别忘了，”奥利里补充道，“极地矿区的作业，一年里只有大约两个月能进行——北极是九月中旬到十一月中旬，南极是三月中旬到五月中旬。自然，什么都得赶着办，都在压力底下干。”

“那么，你们为什么偏要到两极去开矿？难道没有一年到头都能开采的矿藏地吗？”

“没有这么富、这么好采的，”布朗特说。“你知道这颗行星的两极季节是什么样子。气温从仲夏的华氏二百五十度上下，一直降到冬季的零下一百五十度。那里有你能想象到的最猛烈的热力侵蚀——冰盖春天化成一片海，到仲夏便彻底沸腾蒸干。还会刮起狂暴的圆形热风暴，吹走轻沙浮尘，把较重的金属矿石和金属颗粒留在原地。等风势一歇，我们便进场干上两个月。那其实算不得采矿，连采石都算不上；我们只是从地表把矿石舀起来，装上运矿船，空运到斯基尔克（Skilk）、克林克（Krink）和格兰克（Grank），在冶炼炉里炼上一冬。冶炼炉由土著操持；他们趁着熔矿的炉火，给自己和牲口解冻食物。在北方，冶金和吃食的烹制向来就是这么合在一块儿的。”

“对了，你要是觉得在矿上干活的土著自觉受了虐待，大可以提议把矿区整个关停，看看土著会作何反应，”冯·施利希滕对她说。“独立受雇的自由工人，按土著的标准，干上两季就能发家；许多农奴从我们这儿挣的奖励工资，干上一季就够赎身的了。”

===Original===
“Well, if the Company’s doing so much good on this planet, how is it that this native, Rakkeed, the one you call the Mad Prophet, is able to find such a following?” Paula demanded. “There must be something wrong somewhere.”

“That’s a fair question,” Blount replied, inverting a cocktail jug over his glass to extract the last few drops. “When we came to Uller, we found a culture roughly like that of Europe during the Seventh Century Pre-Atomic, or, more closely, like that of Japan before the beginning of the First Century P. A. We initiated a technological and economic revolution here, and such revolutions have their casualties, too. A number of classes and groups got squeezed pretty badly, like the horse-breeders and harness-manufacturers on Terra by the invention of the automobile, or the coal and hydroelectric interests when direct conversion of nuclear energy to electric current was developed, or the railroads and steamship lines at the time of the discovery of the contragravity-field. Naturally, there’s a lot of ill-feeling on the part of merchants and artisans who weren’t able or willing to adapt themselves to changing conditions; they’re all backing Rakkeed and yelling ‘*Znidd suddabit!*’ now. You know, it’s a shame that geek messiah isn’t a smart crook, instead of an honest fanatic; he could take in the equivalent of a couple of million sols a year off the North Uller merchants and the Equatorial Zone shipowners. But it is a fact, which not even Rakkeed can successfully deny, that we’ve raised the general living standard of this planet by about two hundred percent.”

“Rakkeed is a Zirk,” von Schlichten said. “They’re the nomads who hire out to the northern merchants as caravan-drivers, and also prey, or used to prey, on the caravans as brigands. Since our air-freighters got into operation, neither caravan-driving nor caravan-raiding has been a paying business, and our air-patrols have made caravan-raiding suicidal as well. So the Zirks don’t like us. The only thing they know or are willing to learn is handling these six-legged riding- and pack-animals we call hipposaurs. We employ a few of them as cavalry, and a few more of them work as the local equivalent of gauchos, and the rest just sit around and listen to Rakkeed’s sermons.”

Both jugs were empty. Colonel O’Leary, as befitted his junior rank, picked them up; after a good-natured wrangle with von Schlichten, Blount handed the colonel his credit-key.

===Chinese===
“好吧，如果公司在这颗行星上做了这么多好事，那这个拉基德——就是你们叫做疯狂先知的那个土佬——怎么还能纠起这么大一股追随者？”保拉质问道。“总有什么地方不对劲儿。”

“这个问题问得公道，”布朗特答道，一面把鸡尾酒壶倒竖在杯口上，控出最后几滴酒。“我们初到乌勒尔时，发现这里的文化大抵相当于原子前纪元第七世纪的欧洲，或者更贴切些，相当于原子纪元第一世纪发端之前的日本。我们在此地掀起了一场技术革命和经济革命，而这类革命也有它自己的牺牲者。若干阶层和群体被挤压得很惨，就像地球上因汽车发明而失势的养马人和马具制造商，因核能直接转化为电流而受挫的煤炭与水电利益集团，还有反重力场（contragravity-field）发现之时的各条铁路和轮船航线。自然，那些无力或无意适应时变的商人和工匠满腹怨气；如今他们全都在给拉基德撑腰，扯着嗓子喊『Znidd suddabit!』。要知道，这位土佬救世主要是个精明的骗子，而不是个实心的狂热信徒，那才叫可惜——他本可以每年从北乌勒尔的商人和赤道区的船主身上，捞进相当于几百万索尔（sol）的钱财。但有一点是事实，任凭拉基德如何巧辩也否认不了：我们已把这颗行星的总体生活水平提高了大约百分之二百。”

“拉基德是个齐尔克人（Zirk），”冯·施利希滕说。“这个游牧民族受雇给北方商人赶商队，同时也打商队的主意——至少从前是明火打劫。自从我们的空运货船投入运营，赶商队和劫商队都成了无利可图的营生，我们的空中巡逻队又让劫商队成了送命的买卖。所以齐尔克人不待见我们。他们唯一会干、也唯一肯学的，就是摆弄我们唤作河马龙（hipposaur）的那种六条腿的骑乘驮运兽。我们雇了少数人当骑兵，另有少数干着此地相当于高乔牧人的营生，其余的，就闲坐着听拉基德布道。”

两只酒壶都空了。奥利里上校顾念自己军衔居末，起身把壶收走；同冯·施利希滕好脾气地推让了一阵之后，布朗特把自己的记账钥匙交给了这位上校。

===Original===
“The merchants in the north don’t like us; beside spoiling the caravan-trade, we’re spoiling their local business, because the land-owning barons, who used to deal with them, are now dealing directly with us. At Skilk, King Firkked’s afraid his feudal nobility is going to try to force a Runnymede on him, so he’s been currying favor with the urban merchants; that makes him as pro-Rakkeed and as anti-Terran as they are. At Krink, King Jonkvank has the support of his barons, but he’s afraid of his urban bourgeoisie, and we pay him a handsome subsidy, so he’s pro-Terran and anti-Rakkeed. At Skilk, Rakkeed comes and goes openly; at Krink he has a price on his head.”

“Jonkvank is not one of the assets we boast about too loudly,” Hideyoshi O’Leary said, pausing on his way from the table. “He’s as bloody-minded an old murderer as you’d care not to meet in a dark alley anywhere.”

“We can turn our backs on him and not expect a knife between our shoulders, anyhow,” von Schlichten said. “And we can believe, oh, up to eighty percent of what he tells us, and that’s sixty percent better than any of the other native princes, except King Kankad, of course. The Kragans are the only real friends we have on this planet.” He thought for a moment. “Miss Quinton, are you doing sociographic research-work here, in addition to your Ex-Rights work?” he asked. “Well, let me advise you to pay some attention to the Kragans. You’ll only find them treated at any length at all in that compendium of misinformation, Willard Stanley-Browne’s *Short Sociographic History of Beta Hydrae II*, and ninety percent of what Stanley-Browne says about them is completely erroneous.”

“Oh, but they’re just a parasite-race on the Terrans,” Dr. Paula Quinton objected. “You find races like that all through the explored galaxy﻿—pathetic cultural mongrels.”

===Chinese===
“北方的商人不待见我们；除了毁掉商队买卖，我们还砸了他们的本地生意，因为从前同他们做交易的拥有土地的贵族男爵，如今直接同我们打交道了。在斯基尔克，菲尔克德王（King Firkked）生怕他的封建贵族逼他上演一出『兰尼米德』（Runnymede，贵族迫王签署《大宪章》之典），于是千方百计讨好城市商人；这就使他变得同那些商人一样，亲拉基德、反地球人。在克林克，琼克万克王（King Jonkvank）有他的贵族撑腰，却忌惮城里的资产阶级，而我们付给他一大笔津贴，所以他亲地球人、反拉基德。在斯基尔克，拉基德来去公开；在克林克，他的人头挂着悬赏。”

“琼克万克可不是我们乐于大声夸耀的本钱之一，”秀吉·奥利里说着，在离席的半道上停了停。“他是那种你随便到哪儿都不想在小黑巷里撞见的心狠手辣的老杀人犯。”

“不管怎么说，我们背对着他，不必提防肩胛骨中间挨上一刀，”冯·施利希滕说。“而且他的话，唔，我们能信上八成，这比其他任何一个土著王公都强出六成——当然，坎卡德王（King Kankad）除外。克拉冈人（Kragan）是我们在这颗行星上仅有的真正的朋友。”他略想了想。“昆顿小姐，除了地外权利协会（Ex-Rights）方面的工作，你在这里还做社会志研究吗？”他问。“那么，恕我进言，劝你多留意留意克拉冈人。关于他们，唯一有稍许篇幅的论述，全在那部谬误大全——威拉德·斯坦利-布朗（Willard Stanley-Browne）的《水蛇座β二号行星社会志简史》（Short Sociographic History of Beta Hydrae II）——里头，而斯坦利-布朗论及他们的内容，百分之九十完全错误。”

“哦，可他们不过是个寄生在地球人身上的种族罢了，”保拉·昆顿博士提出异议。“这样的种族，在整个已勘探的银河系里比比皆是——一群可悲的文化杂种。”

===Original===
Both men laughed heartily. Colonel O’Leary, returning with the jugs, wanted to know what he’d missed. Blount told him.

“Ha! She’s been reading that thing of Stanley-Browne’s,” he said.

“What’s the matter with Stanley-Browne?” Paula demanded.

“Stanley-Browne is one author you can depend on,” O’Leary assured her. “If you read it in Stanley-Browne, it’s wrong. You know, I don’t think she’s run into many Kragans. We ought to take her over and introduce her to King Kankad.”

===Chinese===
两个男人朗声大笑。奥利里上校拎着酒壶回来，想知道自己错过了什么好戏。布朗特讲给他听。

“哈！她读的正是斯坦利-布朗那部大作，”他说。

“斯坦利-布朗怎么了？”保拉质问。

“斯坦利-布朗是位你可以放心信赖的作家，”奥利里向她保证。“只要出自斯坦利-布朗的书，准是错的。我说啊，看她这样子，怕是没接触过几个克拉冈人。咱们该带她过去一趟，引见给坎卡德王。”

===Original===
Von Schlichten allowed himself to be smitten by an idea. “By Allah, so we had!” he exclaimed. “Look, you’re going to Skilk, in the next week, aren’t you? Well, do you think you could get all your end-jobs cleared up here and be ready to leave by 08:00 Tuesday? That’s four days from today.”

“I’m sure I could. Why?”

“Well, I’m going to Skilk, myself, with the armed troopship *Aldebaran*. We’re stopping at King Kankad’s Town to pick up a battalion of Kragan Rifles for duty at the polar mines, where you’re going. Suppose we leave here in my command-car, go to Kankad’s Town, and wait there till the *Aldebaran* gets in. That would give us about two to three hours. If you think the Kragans are ‘pathetic cultural mongrels,’ what you’ll see there will open your eyes. And I might add that the nearest Stanley-Browne ever came to seeing Kankad’s Town was from the air, once, at a distance of four miles.”

===Chinese===
一个念头击中了冯·施利希滕，他索性任其发作。“安拉在上，可不是嘛！”他扬声道。“听我说，下一周你要去斯基尔克，对不对？那么，你想不想把手头的收尾活计全都在这里了结，赶在星期二 08:00 整装待发？离今天还有四天。”

“我想我能。怎么了？”

“是这样的：我自己也要去斯基尔克，搭乘武装运兵船「毕宿五」号（Aldebaran）。我们中途要在坎卡德王的城里停一停，接一个营的克拉冈来复枪团（Kragan Rifles）去极地矿区值勤——也就是你要去的地方。要不这样：我们坐我的指挥车从这儿出发，先去坎卡德城，在那儿等「毕宿五」号进港。这样我们大约能有两三个钟头。你要是认定克拉冈人是『可悲的文化杂种』，你在那儿见到的一切定会让你大开眼界。另外我还可以补一句：斯坦利-布朗此生离看见坎卡德城最近的一次，是在空中——有那么一回，隔着四英里。”

===Original===
“Well, they live entirely by serving as mercenary soldiers for the Uller Company, don’t they?”

“More or less. You see, when we came to Uller, they were barbarian brigands; had a string of forts along caravan-roads and at fords and mountain-passes, and levied tolls. They raided into Konkrook and Keegark territory, too. Well, we had to break that up. We fought a little war with them, beat them rather badly in a couple of skirmishes, and then made a deal with them. That was before my time, when old Jerry Kirke was Governor-General. He negotiated a treaty with their King, bought their rievers’-forts outright, and paid them a subsidy to compensate for loss of tolls and raid-spoil, and agreed to employ the whole tribe as soldiers. We’ve taught them a lot﻿—you’ll see how much when you visit their town﻿—but they aren’t cultural mongrels. You’ll like them.”

“Well, general, I’ll take you up,” she said. “But I warn you; if this is some scheme to indoctrinate me with the Uller Company’s side of the case and blind me to unjust exploitation of the natives here, I don’t propagandize very easily.”

“Fair enough, as long as you don’t let fear of being propagandized blind you to the good we’re doing here, or impair your ability to observe and draw accurate conclusions. Just stay scientific about it and I’ll be satisfied. Now, let’s take time out for lubrication,” he said, filling her glass and passing the jug.

===Chinese===
“呃，他们如今不是全靠给乌勒尔公司当雇佣兵过活吗？”

“多多少少是吧。你瞧，我们初到乌勒尔那阵子，他们还是野蛮的劫掠部族；沿着商路、渡口和山隘修了一串堡寨，坐收买路钱，还时常劫入孔克鲁克（Konkrook）和基加克（Keegark）的地界。这一套当然得破除。我们同他们打了一小仗，几场遭遇战把他们打得相当惨，随后便同他们做成了交易。那是在我来之前，老杰里·柯克（Jerry Kirke）任总督的时候。他同他们的王谈成了一项条约，干脆出钱把他们的劫掠者堡寨整个买了下来，又付给他们一笔津贴，补偿买路钱和劫掠赃物的损失，并约定雇佣整个部落当兵。我们教会了他们许多——等你参观了他们的城，自会看到有多少——可他们绝不是什么文化杂种。你会喜欢他们的。”

“好吧，将军，这个提议我接下了，”她说。“不过丑话说在前头；倘若这是什么计谋，想拿乌勒尔公司的一面之词给我洗脑，好让我对这里土著遭受的不公正剥削视而不见——那我可没那么容易被宣传攻心。”

“这话公道。只要你别因为怕被宣传，就看不见我们在这里办的好事，或者磨钝了自己观察并得出准确结论的本事，就行。只要在这上头保持科学态度，我就满意了。来，咱们暂停争论，先润一润嗓子，”他说着，给她斟满酒杯，把酒壶递了过去。

===Original===
Two hours and five cocktails later, they were still at the table, and they had taught Paula Quinton some twenty verses of *The Heathen Geeks, They Wear No Breeks*, including the four printable ones.

===Chinese===
两个小时、五杯鸡尾酒之后，一桌人还坐在原地；他们已经教会了保拉·昆顿《异教土佬不穿裤》（The Heathen Geeks, They Wear No Breeks）里约莫二十段歌词——其中包括那四段可以付印的。

## XI Of Princedoms Which Have Been Won by Conquest / XI 论凭征服得来的君主国

===Original===
Holstering his pistol, he raced for the telecast station, to receive a call from a Colonel Khalid ib’n Talal, a Zanzibar Arab, aboard the approaching ship.

“I’ve one of Jonkvank’s regiments, the Jeel-Feeders, armed with Terran 9 mm rifles and a few bazookas; I have a company of our Zirks, with their mounts, and a battalion of the Sixth N.U.N.I.; I also have four 90 mm guns, Terran-manned,” he reported. “What’s the situation, general, and where do you want me to land?”

Von Schlichten described the situation succinctly, in an ancient and unprintable military cliché. “Try landing south of the Reservation, a little west of the ruins of the labor-camp,” he advised. “The bulk of Firkked’s army is in that section, and I want them run out as soon as possible. We’ll give you all the contragravity and fire support we can.”

===Chinese===
他把手枪插回枪套，奔向电视广播站，去接听正驶近的飞船打来的电话——来电者是哈利德·伊本·塔拉勒上校（Khalid ib'n Talal），一位桑给巴尔阿拉伯人。

「我载着琼克万克王的一个团——饲吉尔团（Jeel-Feeders），装备地球制 9 毫米步枪和少量火箭筒；还有一个连的齐尔克骑兵，连人带坐骑；外加第六北乌勒尔土著步兵团的一个营；另有四门 90 毫米炮，炮手全是地球人。」他报告道，「将军，局势如何？您要我降在哪儿？」

冯·施利希滕用一句古老而不宜付印的军中老话，把局势概括得言简意赅。「试着降在公司特区以南、劳工营废墟偏西一点的地方。」他建议道，「菲尔克德的大军主力就在那一带，我要他们尽快被撵走。我们会竭尽所能，为你提供反重力火力与炮火支援。」

===Original===
The *Northern Star* let down slowly, firing her guns and dropping bombs; as she descended, rifle-fire spurted from all her lower-deck portholes. There was cheering, human and Ulleran, from inside the battered defense-perimeter; combat-cars, airjeeps, and improvised bombers lifted out to strafe the Skilkans on the ground, and the four airtanks moved out to take position and open fire with their 90 mm’s, helping to flush King Firkked’s regulars and auxiliaries out of the gullies and ruins and drive them south along the mountain, away from where the ship would land and also away from the city of Skilk. The *Northern Star* set down quickly, and troops and artillery began to be unloaded, joining in the fighting.

It was five hundred miles to Krink; three hours after lifting out, the *Northern Star* was back again, with two more of King Jonkvank’s infantry regiments, and by 13:00, when the fourth load arrived from Krink, the fighting was entirely on the eastern bank of the dry Hoork River. This last contingent of reinforcements was landed in the eastern suburbs of Skilk and began fighting their way into the city from the rear.

===Chinese===
「北极星」号缓缓压低，一边开炮一边投弹；下降途中，下层甲板所有的舷窗里喷吐出条条步枪火舌。饱经摧折的防御圈内爆出欢呼声，有人类的，也有乌勒尔人的；战斗车、飞行吉普和临时改装的轰炸机腾空而起，扫射地面上的斯基尔克人；四辆空中坦克开出去占据阵地，以 90 毫米炮开火，协助把菲尔克德王的正规军和辅军从沟壑与废墟里赶出来，沿山麓向南驱赶——既远离飞船将要降落的地点，也远离斯基尔克城。「北极星」号迅速落定，部队与火炮开始卸载，随即投入战斗。

克林克远在五百英里之外；升空三小时后，「北极星」号便又折返，载来琼克万克王另外两个步兵团；到 13:00，从克林克运来的第四批兵员抵达时，战事已完全移到干涸的霍尔克河东岸。这最后一批增援在斯基尔克东郊着陆，开始从背后一路杀进城去。

===Original===
It was evident, however, that the pacification of Skilk would not be accomplished as rapidly as von Schlichten wished﻿—street fighting, against a determined enemy, is notoriously slow work﻿—and he decided to risk the *Northern Star* in an attack against the Palace itself, and, over the objections of Paula Quinton, Jules Keaveney, and Barney Mordkovitz, to lead the attack in person.

Inside the city, he found that the Zirk cavalry from Krink had thrust up one of the broader streets to within a thousand yards of the Palace, and, supported by infantry, contragravity, and a couple of airtanks, were pounding and hacking at a mass of Skilkans whose uniform lack of costume prevented distinguishing between soldiery and townsfolk. Very few of these, he observed, seemed to be using firearms; with his glasses, he could see them shooting with long northern air-rifles and a few Takkad Sea crossbows. Either weapon would shoot clear through a Terran or halfway through an Ulleran at fifty yards, but at over two hundred they were almost harmless. There were a few fires still burning from the bombardment of the night before﻿—Ulleran, and particularly North Ulleran, cities did not burn well﻿—and the blaze which had consumed the bulk of Firkked’s stock of thermoconcentrate fuel had long ago burned out, leaving an area of six or eight blocks blackened and lifeless.

The ship let down, while the six combat-cars which had accompanied her buzzed the Palace roof, strafing it to keep it clear, and the Kragans aboard fired with their rifles. She came to rest on seven-eighths weight reduction, and even before the gangplanks were run out, the Kragans were dropping to the flat roof, running to stairhead penthouses and tossing grenades into them.

===Chinese===
然而，平定斯基尔克显然无法像冯·施利希滕指望的那样迅速完成——对顽敌打巷战，向来是出了名的慢工——于是他决定拿「北极星」号冒险，直捣王宫本身，并且不顾保拉·昆顿、朱尔斯·基夫尼和巴尼·莫德科维茨的反对，亲自领兵出击。

进了城，他发现从克林克来的齐尔克骑兵已沿一条较宽的街道突进到离王宫不足一千码处，在步兵、反重力艇和两辆空中坦克的支援下，正对一大群斯基尔克人冲杀砍劈——这些人清一色赤身无衣，根本无从分辨兵与民。他注意到，其中动用火器的寥寥无几；从望远镜里看得分明，他们使的是北方式的长气步枪，外加几具塔卡德海弩。五十码之内，这两种武器足以把地球人整个射穿、把乌勒尔人射进半个身子；可一到两百码开外，便几乎伤不了人了。前夜炮击燃起的大火还有几处在烧——乌勒尔的城池，尤其是北乌勒尔的城池，不大烧得起来——而那场吞掉了菲尔克德大部分热凝燃料存量的烈焰早已熄灭，只留下六八个街区的焦黑死地。

飞船压低降落，随行的六架战斗车贴着王宫屋顶低掠盘旋、扫射压制，不容屋顶上站住人；舰上的克拉冈人也以步枪开火。飞船在反重力抵去八分之七重量的状态下落定，跳板尚未搭好，克拉冈人已纷纷跃上平顶，直扑楼梯口的阁楼间，把手榴弹掷了进去。

===Original===
The taking of the Palace was a gruesome business. Knowing exactly how much mercy they would have shown had they been storming the Residency, Firkked’s soldiers and courtiers fought desperately and had to be exterminated, floor by floor, room by room, hallway by hallway. There was some attempt at escape from the ground floor as von Schlichten and his Kragans fought their way down from above, but the *Northern Star* and her escort of combat-cars and airjeeps bombed and machine-gunned and rocketed the fugitives from above, and the loyal Zirk cavalry, bursting through the mob, came up shooting and lancing. By this time, an aircar fitted with a sound-amplifier was circling overhead, while a loyal native-officer of the Sixth N.U.N.I. shouted offers of quarter and orders to the troops to spare any who surrendered.

Driving down from above, von Schlichten and his Kragans slithered over floors increasingly greasy with yellow Ulleran blood. He had picked up a broadsword at the foot of the first stairway down; a little later, he tossed it aside in favor of another, better balanced and with a better guard.
