There was a furious battle at the doorways of the throne room; finally, climbing over the bodies of their own dead and the enemy’s, they were inside.

Here there was no question of quarter whatever, at least as long as Firkked lived; North Ulleran nobles did not surrender under the eyes of their king, and North Ulleran kings did not surrender their thrones alive. There was also a tradition, of which von Schlichten was mindful, that a king must only be killed by his conqueror, in personal combat, with steel.

===Chinese===
夺取王宫是一场血腥的活计。菲尔克德的士兵与廷臣心里一清二楚，倘若换成他们攻打总督府，会得到怎样的怜悯；于是他们拼死顽抗，不得不被一层楼一层楼、一个房间一个房间、一条走廊一条走廊地消灭干净。冯·施利希滕和他的克拉冈人自上而下杀下来时，底层有人试图夺路而逃，但「北极星」号及其战斗车、飞行吉普护航编队自空中对逃亡者轰炸、扫射、发射火箭；忠心的齐尔克骑兵冲开乱民，一路开枪挺矛、疾冲而上。到这时，一辆装了扩音器的飞车正在头顶盘旋，第六北乌勒尔土著步兵团一名忠心的土著军官高声喊话，宣告可以受降，并命令部队饶过一切投降之人。

冯·施利希滕和他的克拉冈人自上层一路向下杀，脚下的地板被黄色的乌勒尔人血越浸越滑。下第一道楼梯时，他顺手捡了一把阔剑；不多时便把它丢开，换了另一把配重更顺、护手更牢的。王座大厅的几处门口爆发了恶战；最后，他们踩着自己人和敌人的尸体，冲了进去。

这里全然没有饶命一说——至少菲尔克德一息尚存时如此；北乌勒尔的贵族不在自家国王眼皮底下投降，北乌勒尔的国王也绝不活着让出王位。还有一条传统，冯·施利希滕铭记于心：国王只能死于征服者之手，须凭单打独斗，须用钢铁。

===Original===
With a wedge of Kragan bayonets around him and the picked-up broadsword in his hand, he fought his way to the throne, where Firkked waited, a sword in one of his upper hands, his Spear of State in the other, and a dagger in each lower hand. With his left hand, von Schlichten detached the bayonet from the rifle of one of his followers and went forward, trying not to think of the absurdity of a man of the Sixth Century AE, the representative of a civilized Chartered Company, dueling to the death with swords with a barbarian king for a throne he had promised to another barbarian, or of what could happen on Uller if he allowed this four-armed monstrosity to kill him.

It was not as bad as it looked, however. The ornate Spear of State, in spite of its long, cruel-looking blade, was not an especially good combat-weapon, at least for one hand, and Firkked seemed confused by the very abundance of his armament. After a few slashes and jabs, von Schlichten knocked the unwieldy thing from his opponent’s hand. This raised a fearful ululation from the Skilkan nobility, who had stopped fighting to watch the duel; evidently it was the very worst sort of a bad omen. Firkked, seemingly relieved to be disencumbered of the thing, caught his sword in both hands and aimed a roundhouse swing at von Schlichten’s head; von Schlichten dodged, crippled one of Firkked’s lower hands with a quick slash, and lunged at the royal belly. Firkked used his remaining dagger to parry, backed a step closer to his throne, and took another swing with his sword, which von Schlichten parried on the bayonet in his left hand. Then, backing, he slashed at the inside of Firkked’s leg with the thousand-year-old *coup-de-Jarnac*. Firkked, unable to support the weight of his dense-tissued body on one leg, stumbled; von Schlichten ran him neatly through the breast with his sword and through the throat with the bayonet.

There was silence in the throne room for an instant, and then, with a horrible collective shriek, the Skilkans threw down their weapons. One of von Schlichten’s Kragans slung his rifle and picked up the Spear of State with all four hands, taking his post ceremoniously behind the victor. A couple of others dragged the body of Firkked to the edge of the dais, and one of them drew his leaf-shaped short-sword and beheaded it.

===Chinese===
一队克拉冈刺刀在他四周结成楔阵，他手持那把捡来的阔剑，一路杀到王座前。菲尔克德正在那里等着：上手一只持剑，另一只擎着国矛，下面两只手各执一柄匕首。冯·施利希滕用左手从一名部下的步枪上卸下刺刀，走上前去，竭力不去想此事何其荒诞——一个原子纪元第六世纪的人、一家文明特许公司的代表，竟为一个早已许给另一个蛮王的王位，同蛮王持剑生死相搏；也不去想，倘若放任这个四条胳膊的怪物杀了自己，乌勒尔上会出什么乱子。

不过，实情并不像看上去那么糟。那杆装点华贵的国矛，矛刃虽长而狰狞，却算不得什么好兵刃——至少单手使唤是如此——而菲尔克德似乎恰恰因为兵器太多而自乱阵脚。劈刺了几个回合，冯·施利希滕便把那件笨家伙打脱了对手的手。斯基尔克的贵族中顿时响起一阵骇人的哀号——他们早已住手观战；显然，这是凶兆里至凶的一种。菲尔克德卸脱了那东西，看上去反倒松了口气，双手合握剑柄，抡圆了朝冯·施利希滕的脑袋扫来；冯·施利希滕闪身躲过，疾挥一刀废了菲尔克德一只下手，随即直取那王者的肚腹。菲尔克德用剩下的匕首格开，退后一步贴近王座，又挥剑扫来；这一剑被冯·施利希滕用左手的刺刀架住。接着他退步让过，使出传承千年的*雅尔纳克之击*（coup-de-Jarnac），一刀砍进菲尔克德的腿弯内侧。菲尔克德身躯组织致密、分量沉重，单腿再也支撑不住，一个趔趄；冯·施利希滕剑贯其胸、刺刀透其咽喉，干净利落。

王座大厅里静了一瞬，随即，斯基尔克人发出一声集体惨嚎，纷纷掷下武器。冯·施利希滕麾下一名克拉冈人把步枪往背上一挎，四只手齐上捧起国矛，仪式感十足地在胜利者身后侍立。另有几人把菲尔克德的尸身拖到宝座高台边，其中一人抽出叶形短剑，斩下首级。

---

===Original===
At mid-afternoon, von Schlichten was on the roof of the Palace, holding the Spear of State, with Firkked’s head impaled on the point, while a Terran technician aimed an audiovisual recorder.

“This,” he said, with the geek-speaker in his mouth, “is King Firkked’s Spear of State, and here, upon it, is King Firkked’s head. Two days ago, Firkked was at peace with the Company, and Firkked was King in Skilk. If he had not dared raise his feeble hand against the might of the Uller Company, he would still be alive, and his Spear would still be borne behind him. So must all those who rise against the Company perish.﻿ ﻿… Cut.”

The camera stopped. A Kragan came forward and took the Spear of State, with its grisly burden, carrying it to a nearby wall and leaning it up, like a piece of stage property no longer required for this scene but needed for the next. Von Schlichten took out his geek-speaker, wiped and pouched it, and took his cigarette case from his pocket.

===Chinese===
午后时分，冯·施利希滕站在王宫屋顶上，手持国矛，矛尖上挑着菲尔克德的首级，一名地球技师正把声像记录机对准他。

他口含土佬语发声器，说道：「这是菲尔克德国王的国矛，而这上面、矛尖之上，是菲尔克德国王的头颅。两天之前，菲尔克德还与公司相安无事，菲尔克德还是斯基尔克之王。倘若他未曾胆敢举起那只虚弱的手、对抗乌勒尔公司的伟力，他此刻仍然活着，他的国矛仍然随行于身后。凡起而反叛公司者，皆当如此灭亡。……停机。」

摄影机停了。一名克拉冈人上前接过国矛，连同上面那件可怖的累赘，把它拿到附近墙边倚好，活像一件本场戏用罢、下场戏还用得着的道具。冯·施利希滕取出口中的土佬语发声器，擦净收袋，又从口袋里掏出烟盒。

===Original===
“Well, this is the limit!” Paula Quinton, who had come up during the filming of the scene, exploded. “I thought you had to kill him yourself in order to encourage your soldiers; I didn’t think you wanted to make a movie of it to show your friends. I’m through; you can find yourself a new adjutant!”

Von Schlichten tapped the cigarette on the gold-and-platinum case and stared at her through his monocle.

“You can’t resign,” he told her. “Resignations of officers are not being accepted until the end of hostilities. In any case, I shouldn’t care to have you go; you’re the best adjutant, Hideyoshi O’Leary not excepted, I ever had. Sit down, colonel.” He lit the cigarette. “Your politico-military education still needs a little filling in.

===Chinese===
「好哇，这可真是登峰造极了！」拍摄这场戏时赶上楼来的保拉·昆顿发作了。「我原以为你非得亲手杀他，是为了激励士卒；没想到你还要拍成电影，放给朋友们看。我不干了；你另请高明，找位新副官去吧！」

冯·施利希滕在金铂烟盒上磕了磕香烟，隔着单片眼镜凝视她。

「你辞不了职。」他告诉她，「敌对行动结束之前，军官辞呈一概不受理。再说，我也不愿你走；你是我用过的最好的副官——秀吉·奥利里也不例外。坐下，上校。」他点着香烟。「你的政治军事学，还需稍稍补课。

===Original===
“At Grank, we have two ships. One is the *Northern Lights*, sister ship of the *Northern Star*. The other is the cruiser *Procyon*, the only real warship on Uller, with a main battery of four 200 mm guns. How King Yoorkerk was able to get control of those ships I don’t know, but there will be a board of inquiry and maybe a couple of courts-martial, when things get stabilized to a point where we can afford such luxuries. As it is, we need those ships desperately, and as soon as he gets in, I’m sending Hideyoshi O’Leary to Grank with the *Northern Star* and a load of Kragan Rifles, to pry them loose. The audiovisual of which this is the last scene is going to be one of the crowbars he’s going to use.”

“Oh! I get it!” Her eyes widened with pleasure at having finally caught on; she accepted the cigarette and the light von Schlichten offered. “Good old *nervenkrieg*!”

“Yes. A little idea I adapted from my Nazi ancestors of four hundred and fifty years ago. Hideyoshi’s going to treat King Yoorkerk to a movie-show. Want to bet he won’t loosen up and release *Procyon* and *Northern Lights* and unblockade the Grank Residency after he sees that shot of Firkked’s head leering at him off the point of that overgrown assegai? As I said, that’s only the last scene, too. I’ve been having scenes shot all through this fight; some of them are really horrifying.”

===Chinese===
「在格兰克，我们有两条船。一条是『北极光』号，『北极星』号的姊妹舰；另一条是巡洋舰『南河三』号——全乌勒尔唯一一条真正的军舰，主炮组为四门 200 毫米炮。约克克王是怎么把那两条船弄到手的，我不知道；但等局势稳定到我们供得起这类奢侈，自会有调查委员会，兴许还要办上几件军法审判。眼下我们急需那两条船，等他一到，我就派秀吉·奥利里带上『北极星』号和一船克拉冈来复枪团官兵去格兰克，把它们撬出来。这部影片——方才拍的正是它的最后一场——就是他要用的撬棍之一。」

「哦！我明白了！」终于开了窍，她高兴得两眼放光，接过冯·施利希滕递来的香烟和火。「好一招百试不爽的老法宝——神经战（nervenkrieg）！」

「不错。一点小心得，改编自四百五十年前我那些纳粹祖先的发明。秀吉要请约克克王看一场电影。要不要打个赌：等他看到菲尔克德的脑袋在那杆特大号阿塞盖矛（assegai）矛尖上冲他狞笑的镜头，还不乖乖松口，放还『南河三』号和『北极光』号、解除对格兰克总督府的封锁？再说一遍，那还只是最后一场。这场仗我从头到尾一直在让人拍片；有些镜头，着实骇人。」

===Original===
“But why did you have to fight Firkked yourself?” she asked. “You took an awful chance, with two hands to his four.”

“Not so awful, remember what I told you about the physical limitations of Ullerans. But I had to kill him myself, with a sword; according to local custom that makes me King of Skilk.”

“Why, your Majesty!” She rose and curtsied mockingly. “But I thought you were going to make Jonkvank King of Skilk.”

He shook his head. “Just Viceroy,” he corrected. “I’m handing the Spear of State down to him, not up to him; he’ll reign as my vassal, and, consequently, as vassal of the Company, and before long, he won’t be much more at Krink either. That’ll take a little longer﻿—there’ll have to be military missions, and economic missions, and trade-agreements, and all the rest of it, first﻿—but he’s on the way to becoming a puppet-prince.”

===Chinese===
「可你为什么非要亲自与菲尔克德交手？」她问，「两只手对四只手，你冒的险太大了。」

「没那么吓人——记得我跟你讲过的、乌勒尔人身体上的短处吧。但我必须亲自用剑杀他；按当地风俗，这使我成为斯基尔克之王。」

「哎呀，陛下！」她起身，戏谑地行了个屈膝礼。「可我原以为，你打算立琼克万克为斯基尔克之王呢。」

他摇头。「只是副王。」他纠正道，「我是把国矛颁赐于他，而非由他捧呈上来；他将作为我的封臣临朝，因而也是公司的封臣，而且要不了多久，他在克林克也做不了多大的主了。那还得多花些时日——先得有军事使团、经济使团、贸易协定，以及其他种种——不过，他已经在变成一位傀儡王公的路上了。」

===Original===
Half an hour later, a large and excessively ornate air-launch, specially built at the Konkrook shipyards for King Jonkvank, was sighted coming over the mountain from the east. An escort of combat-cars was sent to meet it, and a battalion of Kragans and the survivors of Firkked’s court were drawn up on the Palace roof.

“His Majesty, Jonkvank, King of Krink!” the former herald of King Firkked’s court, now herald to King Carlos von Schlichten, shouted, banging on a brass shield with the flat of his sword, as Jonkvank descended from his launch, attended by a group of his nobles and his Spear of State, with Hideyoshi O’Leary and Francis N. Shapiro shepherding them. As the guests advanced across the roof, the herald banged again on his shield.

“His Majesty, Carlos von Schlichten,”﻿—which came out more or less as Karlok vonk Zlikdenk﻿—“King, by right of combat, of Skilk!”

===Chinese===
半小时后，有人望见一艘硕大而极尽华饰的飞行游艇（air-launch）——专为琼克万克王在孔克鲁克船厂建造——正从东面翻山而来。一队战斗车受命前去迎接；一个营的克拉冈兵与菲尔克德宫廷的幸存者们在王宫屋顶列队肃立。

「琼克万克陛下，克林克之王！」菲尔克德宫廷的前任传令官——如今改侍卡洛斯·冯·施利希滕王的传令官——高声喝唱，以剑脊敲响一面黄铜盾。琼克万克从游艇上下来，一群贵族与他的国矛随侍在侧，由秀吉·奥利里和弗朗西斯·N·夏皮罗从旁照应。宾客们穿过屋顶行进时，传令官又敲了一记铜盾。

「卡洛斯·冯·施利希滕陛下」——这名字喊出口，大致成了「卡尔洛克·冯克·兹利克登克」（Karlok vonk Zlikdenk）——「凭交战之权，为斯基尔克之王！」

===Original===
Von Schlichten advanced to meet his fellow-monarch, his own Spear of State, with Firkked’s head still grinning from it, two paces behind him.

Jonkvank stopped, his face contorted with saurian rage.

“What is this?” he demanded. “You told me that I could be King of Skilk; is this how a Terran keeps his word?”

“A Terran’s word is always good, Jonkvank,” von Schlichten replied, omitting the titles, as was proper in one sovereign addressing another. “My word was that you should reign in Skilk, and my word stands. But these things must be done decently, according to custom and law. I killed Firkked in single combat. Had I not done so, the Spear of Skilk would have been left lying, for any of the young of Firkked to pick up. Is that not the law?”

===Chinese===
冯·施利希滕迎向这位君主同侪；他自己的国矛——菲尔克德的头颅仍在矛尖上龇牙而笑——持行在身后两步处。

琼克万克停住脚，一张脸因蜥龙般的狂怒而扭曲。

「这是什么意思？」他诘问，「你告诉过我，我可以做斯基尔克之王；地球人就是这样守信的吗？」

「地球人的话永远作数，琼克万克。」冯·施利希滕答道，径直省去了尊号——君主对君主发言，礼当如此。「我说过的话是：你将在斯基尔克临朝为王；这话至今有效。但这些事必须办得体面，依惯例、依法度。我在单打独斗中杀了菲尔克德。若非如此，斯基尔克国矛便会弃置在地，任凭菲尔克德的任何一个子嗣拾起。法律难道不是这样？」

===Original===
Jonkvank nodded grudgingly. “It is the law,” he admitted.

“Good. Now, since I killed Firkked in lawful manner, his Spear is mine, and what is mine I can give as I please. I now give you the Spear of Skilk, to carry in my name, as I promised.”

The Kragan who was carrying the ceremonial weapon tossed the head of Firkked from the point; another Kragan kicked it aside and advanced to wipe the spear-blade with a rag. Von Schlichten took the Spear and gave it to Jonkvank.

===Chinese===
琼克万克勉强点了点头。「法律是这样。」他承认。

「很好。如今，我既依法杀了菲尔克德，他的矛便归我所有；而我的东西，我乐意给谁便给谁。现在，我把斯基尔克国矛授予你，由你以我的名义执掌——一如我许诺过的。」

持着那件礼器的克拉冈人把菲尔克德的头颅从矛尖上甩落；另一名克拉冈人把它踢到一旁，上前用一块布擦拭矛刃。冯·施利希滕接过矛，交到琼克万克手中。

===Original===
“This is not good!” one of the Skilkan nobles protested. He had a better right than any of the others to protest; he had, a few hours before, ridden in at the head of a company of his retainers to swear loyalty to the Company. “That you should rule over us, yes. You killed Firkked in single combat, and you are the soldier of the Company, which is mighty, as all here have seen. But that this foreigner be given the Spear of Skilk, that is not good!”

Some of the others, emboldened by his example, were jabbering agreement.

“Listen, all of you!” von Schlichten shouted. “Here is no question of Krink ruling over Skilk. Does it matter who holds the Spear of Skilk, when he does so in my name? And King Jonkvank will be no foreigner. He will come and live among you, and later he will travel back and forth between Krink and Skilk, and he will leave the Spear of Krink in Krink, and the Spear of Skilk in Skilk, and in Skilk he will be a Skilkan.”

===Chinese===
「这不好！」一名斯基尔克贵族抗议道。他比在场任何人都有资格抗议：就在几个钟头前，他还率一队家臣策马而来，向公司宣誓效忠。「由你统治我们，可以。你在单打独斗中杀了菲尔克德，你是公司的战士，而公司的强大，此处人人有目共睹。可把这斯基尔克国矛交给这个外来人，这不好！」

其他人中有几个受他壮胆，正七嘴八舌地附和。

「都听着！」冯·施利希滕喝道，「这里根本不存在克林克统治斯基尔克的问题。持斯基尔克国矛者既是以我的名义持矛，矛在谁手又有什么要紧？再说，琼克万克王也算不得什么外来人。他会来住在你们中间，往后往返于克林克与斯基尔克之间；他会把克林克国矛留在克林克，把斯基尔克国矛留在斯基尔克；而在斯基尔克，他就是斯基尔克人。」

===Original===
That seemed to satisfy everybody except Jonkvank, and he had wit enough not to make an issue of it. He even had the Spear of Krink carried back aboard his launch, out of sight, and when he accompanied von Schlichten, an hour later, to see Hideyoshi O’Leary off for Grank, he had the Spear of Skilk carried behind him. When he was alone with von Schlichten, in the room that had been King Firkked’s bedchamber, however, he exploded: “What is all this foolishness which you promised these people in my name and which I must now carry out? That I am to leave the Spear of Skilk in Skilk and the Spear of Krink in Krink, and come here to live.﻿ ﻿…”

“You wish to hold Skilk?” von Schlichten asked.

“I intend to hold Skilk. To begin with, there shall be a great killing here. A very great killing: of all those who advised that fool of a Firkked to start this business; of those who gave shelter to the false prophet, Rakkeed, when he was here; of the faithless priests who gave ear to his abominable heresies and allowed him to spew out his blasphemies in the temples; of those who sent spies to Krink, to corrupt and pervert my soldiers and nobles; of those who.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
这话似乎让人人满意，唯独琼克万克例外；好在他够机敏，没有当场发作。他甚至让人把克林克国矛送回艇上藏起来，眼不见为净；一小时后，他陪冯·施利希滕去为动身赴格兰克的秀吉·奥利里送行时，也让斯基尔克国矛持行在自己身后。可是，单独同冯·施利希滕待在菲尔克德国王昔日的寝宫里时，他终于发作了：「你借我的名义向这些人许下的这一派胡言，如今要我一一来兑现，究竟算什么名堂？说什么我要把斯基尔克国矛留在斯基尔克、克林克国矛留在克林克，还要搬到这儿来住。……」

「你想要据有斯基尔克？」冯·施利希滕问。

「我不只想，我是志在必据。首先，此地要来一场大杀戮。一场天大的杀戮：杀光所有撺掇菲尔克德那蠢货挑起这场事端的人；杀光假先知拉基德在此地时窝藏收留过他的人；杀光那些听信他的邪说异端、任他在神庙里倾吐渎神之言的无信祭司；杀光那些派奸细去克林克、腐蚀败坏我的士兵和贵族的人；杀光那些……

===Original===
“All that is as it should be,” von Schlichten agreed. “Except that it must be done quickly and all at once, before the memories of these crimes fade from the minds of the people. And great care must be taken to kill only those who can be proven to be guilty of something; thus it will be said that the justice of King Jonkvank is terrible to evildoers but a protection and a shield to those who keep the peace and obey the laws. Thus you will gain the name of being a wise and just king. And when the priests are to be killed it should be done under the direction of those other priests who were faithful to the gods and whom King Firkked drove out of their temples, and it must be done in the name of the gods. Thus will you be esteemed a pious, and not an impious, king. As to why you must be a Skilkan in Skilk, you heard the words of Flurknurk, and how the others agreed with him. It must not be allowed to seem that the city has come under foreign rule. And you must not change the laws, unless the people petition you to do so, nor must you increase the taxes, and you must not confiscate the estates of those who are put to death, for the death of parents is always forgiven before the loss of patrimonies. And you should select certain Skilkan nobles, and become the father of their young, and above all, you must leave none of the young of Firkked alive, to raise rebellion against you later.”

Jonkvank nodded, deeply impressed. “By the gods, Karlok vonk Zlikdenk, this is wisdom! Now it is to be seen why the likes of Firkked cannot prevail against you, or against the Company as long as you are the Company’s upper sword-arm!”

Honesty tempted von Schlichten, for a moment, to disclaim originality for the principles he had just enunciated, even at the price of trying to pronounce the name of Niccolo Machiavelli with a geek-speaker. On second thought, however, considerations of policy restrained him. If Jonkvank ever heard of *The Prince*, nothing would satisfy him short of an Ulleran translation, and von Schlichten would have been just about as happy over an Ulleran translation of a complete set of Bethe-cycle bomb specifications.

===Chinese===
「这一切都合情合理。」冯·施利希滕深表赞同，「只是必须办快、一气办完，趁这些罪行的记忆尚未从民众心中淡去。而且要格外小心，只杀那些查有实据、确有罪责之人；如此，人们便会说，琼克万克王的严法令作恶者胆寒，却是安分守法者的庇护与盾牌。这样，你便赢得贤明公正之君的名声。至于处死祭司，应交由那些忠于诸神、被菲尔克德国王逐出神庙的祭司们主持，且必须以诸神之名行之。这样，你将被尊为敬神之君，而非渎神之君。至于为何在斯基尔克就得做斯基尔克人——弗卢克努克（Flurknurk）的话你听见了，众人如何附和他，你也听见了。万不可让这座城显得落入了外族统治。还有：不可更改法律，除非民众呈请变更；不可加征赋税；不可没收伏法者的产业，因为亲长之死总会得到宽恕，祖产之失却永远不能。你还应挑选若干斯基尔克贵族，做他们幼嗣的父亲；而最要紧的是，菲尔克德的子嗣一个也不能留，免得日后起来反叛你。」

琼克万克深为折服，连连点头。「众神在上，卡尔洛克·冯克·兹利克登克，这真是智慧！这下明白了，菲尔克德那号人物为何胜不了你——只要你还是公司的上首剑臂，他们也胜不了公司！」

一时之间，诚实心诱使冯·施利希滕声明，方才这套原则并非他的独创——哪怕代价是得含着土佬语发声器去念「尼可洛·马基雅维利」（Niccolo Machiavelli）的名字。然而再一转念，政策上的考量又把话拦了回去。倘若琼克万克有朝一日听说了《君主论》（The Prince），不给他一部乌勒尔语译本，他绝不肯罢休；而冯·施利希滕对一部《君主论》乌勒尔语译本的欣喜程度，大约也就同对一整套贝特循环炸弹技术规格书的乌勒尔语译本不相上下。

## Prologue On Satan’s Footstool / 序章 在撒旦的脚凳上

===Original===
The big armor-tender vibrated, gently and not unpleasantly, as the contragravity field alternated on and off, occasionally varying its normal rate of five hundred to the second when some thermal updraft lifted the vehicle and the automatic radar-altimeter control acted to alter the frequency and lower it again. Sometimes it rocked slightly, like a boat on the water, and, in the big screen which served in lieu of a window at the front of the control cabin, the dingy-yellow landscape would seem to tilt a little. If unshielded human eyes could have endured the rays of Nu Puppis, Niflheim’s primary, the whole scene would have appeared a vivid Saint Patrick’s Day green, the effect of the blue-predominant light on the yellow atmosphere. The outside ’visor-pickup, however, was fitted with filters which blocked out the gamma-rays and X-rays and most of the ultraviolet-rays, and added the longer light-waves of red and orange which were absent, so that things looked much as they would have under the light of a G0-type star like Sol. The air was faintly yellow, the sky was yellow with a greenish cast, and the clouds were green-gray.

A thousand feet below, the local equivalent of a forest grew, the trees, topped with huge ragged leaves, looking like hundred-foot stalks of celery. There would be animal life down there too—little round things, four inches across, like eight-legged crabs, gnawing at the vegetation, and bigger things, two feet long, with articulated shell-armor and sixteen legs, which fed on the smaller herbivores. Beyond, in the middleground, was open grassland, if one could so call a mat of wormlike colorless or pastel-tinted sprouts, and a river meandered through it. On the skyline, fifty miles away, was a range of low dunes and hills, none more than a thousand feet high.

No human had ever set foot on the surface, or breathed the air, of Niflheim. To have done so would have been instant death; the air was a mixture of free fluorine and fluoride gasses, the soil was metallic fluorides, damp with acid rains, and the river was pure hydrofluoric acid. Even the ordinary spacesuit would have been no protection; the glass and rubber and plastic would have disintegrated in a matter of minutes. People came to Niflheim, and worked the mines and uranium refineries and chemical plants, but they did so inside power-driven and contragravity-lifted armor, and they lived on artificial satellites two thousand miles off-planet. This vehicle, for instance, was built and protected as no spaceship ever had to be, completely insulated and entered only through a triple airlock—an outer lock, which would be evacuated outward after it was closed, a middle lock kept evacuated at all times, and an inner lock, evacuated into the interior of the vehicle before the middle lock could be opened. Niflheim was worse than airless, much worse.

The chief engineer sat at his controls, making the minor lateral adjustments in the vehicle’s position which were not possible to the automatic controls. One of the radiomen was receiving from the orbital base; the other was saying, over and over, in an exasperatedly patient voice: “Dr. Murillo. Dr. Murillo. Please come in, Dr. Murillo.” At his own panel of instruments, a small man with grizzled black hair around a bald crown, and a grizzled beard, chewed nervously at the stump of a dead cigar and listened intently to what was—or for what wasn’t—coming in to his headset receiver. A couple of assistants checked dials and refreshed their memories from notebooks and peered anxiously into the big screen. A large, plump-faced, young man in soiled khaki shirt and shorts, with extremely hairy legs, was doodling on his notepad and eating candy out of a bag. And a black-haired girl in a suit of coveralls three sizes too big for her, and, apparently, not much of anything else, lounged with one knee hooked over her chair-arm, staring into the screen at the distant horizon.

===Chinese===
大型装甲勤务车（armor-tender）微微震颤着，轻柔而不惹人嫌——那是反重力（contragravity）场开开关关交替循环的缘故；每逢一股热上升气流把车子托起、自动雷达高度控制仪便随即改变频率把车重新压低时，它那每秒五百次的常规频率也会偶尔变一变。有时它像水面上的小船那样轻轻摇晃，在控制舱前部那块权当窗户用的大屏幕上，昏黄的景物便仿佛微微倾侧。倘若毫无遮护的人眼受得了船尾座ν（Nu Puppis）——尼福尔海姆（Niflheim）的主星——的射线，眼前这整个景象本该是一片鲜艳的圣帕特里克节式的绿色：那是蓝光为主的星光作用于黄色大气的效果。不过，外部摄像器（'visor-pickup）装有滤镜，滤掉了伽马射线、X射线和大部分紫外线，又补上了所缺的红、橙等较长光波，好让万物看上去大致如同在太阳（Sol）那类G0型恒星的光照下一般。空气呈淡黄色，天空黄中泛绿，云则是绿灰色的。

下方一千英尺处，生长着本地相当于森林的植被：那些树顶着硕大而参差的叶片，看上去像一百英尺高的芹菜秆。那底下想必也有动物——小的是些直径四英寸的圆家伙，像长着八条腿的螃蟹，啃食草木；大些的体长两英尺，披着分节的甲壳，长十六条腿，专吃那些较小的食草动物。再往前，在中景地带，是开阔的“草原”——如果可以把一整片蠕虫状的、无色或淡彩的嫩芽称作草原的话——一条河蜿蜒其间。天际线上，五十英里开外，横着一道低矮的沙丘和山冈，没有一处高过一千英尺。

从未有人类踏上过尼福尔海姆的地面，也从未有人呼吸过它的空气。真要那么做，就是当场送命：空气是游离氟和氟化气体的混合物，土壤是金属氟化物，被酸雨浸得湿漉漉的，河则是纯粹的氢氟酸。就连普通宇航服也顶不了事；那上头的玻璃、橡胶和塑料不出几分钟就会分崩离析。人们照样来尼福尔海姆，照样开采矿山、经营铀精炼厂和化工厂，但都是待在动力驱动、反重力升举的装甲里干活的，而且住在离行星两千英里外的轨道人造卫星上。就拿眼前这辆车来说，它的建造与防护之严苛，是任何飞船都从来不必达到的：完全隔绝绝缘，进出只走三重气闸——外闸，关闭后向外抽空；中闸，时刻保持真空；内闸，须先把闸内空气抽回车体内部，中闸方可开启。尼福尔海姆比没有空气的行星还要糟，糟得多。

总工程师坐在操纵台前，做着自动控制无法完成的那种些微横向调整，修正车子的位置。两名无线电员中，一个在接收轨道基地的讯号；另一个正用一种强压着火气的耐心腔调，一遍又一遍地呼叫：“穆里略（Murillo）博士，穆里略博士，请回话，穆里略博士。”在他自己那面仪表板前，坐着一个个子矮小的男人，秃顶四周一圈花白的黑发，蓄着花白胡须，正紧张地嚼着一截掐灭的雪茄烟蒂，全神贯注地听着耳机接收器里传来的——或者说等那迟迟不来的——声音。两名助手核对着表盘，翻着笔记本加深记忆，忧心忡忡地盯着大屏幕。一个大块头、胖脸膛的年轻人，穿着脏污的卡其衬衫和短裤，两条腿的腿毛浓密至极，正在便笺簿上涂鸦，并从纸袋里摸糖吃。还有一个黑发姑娘，套着一身大了三号的连体工装——而且看样子里面也没穿什么别的——一条腿的膝弯勾着扶手，懒洋洋地靠着，凝望着屏幕上遥远的地平线。

===Original===
“Dr. Murillo. Dr. Mur—” The radioman broke off in mid-syllable and listened for a moment. “I hear you, doctor, go ahead.” Then, a moment later “What’s your position, now, doctor?”

“I can see them,” the girl said, lifting a hand in front of her. “At two o’clock, about one of my hand’s-breadths above the horizon.”

The man with the grizzled beard put his face into the fur around the eyepiece of the telescopic-’visor and twisted a dial. “You have good eyes, Miss Quinton,” he complimented. “Only four personal armors; Ahmed, ask him where the fifth is.”

“We only see four of your personal armors,” the radioman said. “Who’s missing, and why?” He waited for a moment, then lowered the hand-phone and turned. “The fifth one’s inside the handling-machine. One of the Ullerans. Gorkrink.”

The larger of the specks that had appeared on the horizon resolved itself into a handling-machine, a thing like an oversized contragravity-tank, with a bulldozer-blade, a stubby derrick-boom instead of a gun, and jointed, claw-tipped arms to the sides. The smaller dots grew into personal armor—egg-shaped things that sprouted arms and grab-hooks and pushers in all directions. The man with the grizzled beard began talking rapidly into his hand-phone, then hung it up. There was a series of bumps, and the armor-tender, weightless on contragravity, shook as the handling-machine came aboard.

===Chinese===
“穆里略博士。穆里——”无线电员在半个音节上戛然收声，听了片刻。“听到了，博士，请讲。”过了一会儿又说：“博士，您现在的位置？”

“我看见它们了，”姑娘说着，把一只手举到眼前。“两点钟方向，离地平线大约我一只手掌宽。”

蓄花白胡子的男人把脸埋进望远摄像镜（telescopic-'visor）目镜四周的皮毛里，拧动旋钮。“昆顿（Quinton）小姐，你眼神真好，”他夸道。“只有四具单兵装甲（personal armor）。艾哈迈德（Ahmed），问问他第五具在哪儿。”

“我们只看见你们的四具单兵装甲，”无线电员说。“缺了谁，为什么？”他等了一会儿，放下手持话筒，转过身来。“第五具在作业机（handling-machine）里面。一个乌勒尔人（Ulleran）。戈克林克（Gorkrink）。”

地平线上出现的那批小点中，较大的一个渐渐显形为一部作业机——那东西像一辆特大号的反重力坦克，装着推土铲，一截粗短的起重吊臂取代了火炮，两侧还各有带关节的、末端为爪的臂。较小的那些点则长成了单兵装甲——蛋形的东西，向四面八方萌生出机械臂、抓钩和推杆。蓄花白胡子的男人对着手持话筒急速地说了几句，随即挂断。接着响起一阵接一阵的碰撞声，靠反重力托得轻若无物的装甲勤务车晃动起来——作业机登上来了。

===Original===
“You ever see any nuclear bombing, Miss Quinton?” the young man with the hairy legs asked, offering her his candy bag.

“Only by telecast, back Sol-side,” she replied, helping herself. “Test-shots at the Federation Navy proving-ground on Mars. I never even heard of nuclear bombs being used for mining till I came here, though.”

“Well, if this turns out as well as the other job, three months ago, it’ll be something to see,” he promised. “These volcanoes have been dormant for, oh, maybe as long as a thousand years; there ought to be a pretty good head of gas down there. And the magma’ll be thick, viscous stuff, like basalt on Terra. Of course, this won’t be anything like basalt in composition—it’ll be intensely compressed metallic fluorides, with a very high metal-content. The volcanoes we shot three months ago yielded a fine flow of lava with all sorts of metals—nickel, beryllium, vanadium, chromium, indium, as well as copper and iron.”

“What sort of gas were you speaking about?” she asked.

“Hydrogen. That’s what’s going to make the fireworks; it combines explosively with fluorine. The hydrogen-fluorine combination is what passes for combustion here; the result is hydrofluoric acid, the local equivalent of water. See, the metallic core of this planet is covered, much less thickly than that of Terra, with fluoride rock—fluorspar, and that sort of thing. There’s nothing like granite here, for instance. That’s why those big dunes, out there, are the best Niflheim has in the way of mountains. The subsurface hydrogen is produced when the acid filters down through the rock, combines with pure metals underneath.”

===Chinese===
“昆顿小姐，你见过核爆破没有？”腿毛浓密的年轻人问，把糖袋递给她。

“只在太阳系那边看电视转播见过，”她答道，顺手自己拿糖。“在火星的联邦海军试验场看试射。不过，用核弹采矿这码事，我来这儿之前连听都没听说过。”

“嘿，要是这一炮打得跟三个月前那一炮一样好，那可就有看头了，”他打包票道。“这些火山已经休眠了——哦——兴许有一千年了；底下该蓄着相当可观的一股气压。岩浆呢，会是又稠又黏的家伙，跟地球（Terra）上的玄武岩似的。当然，论成分它跟玄武岩毫无相似之处——那是受强烈压缩的金属氟化物，金属含量非常高。三个月前我们轰的那几座火山，喷出了一股上好的岩浆，带着各种各样的金属——镍、铍、钒、铬、铟，还有铜和铁。”

“你说的是什么气体？”她问。

“氢气。放烟花的就指望它；它跟氟化合起来是要爆炸的。氢氟化合就是此地所谓的燃烧；产物是氢氟酸——本地相当于水的东西。这么说吧：这颗行星的金属地核上面盖着一层氟化岩——萤石之类的东西——比地球的盖层薄得多。比如说，这儿压根儿没有花岗岩这种东西。所以外面那些大沙丘，已经是尼福尔海姆拿得出手的最好的山了。地下氢的来历是这样的：酸液渗滤穿过岩层，与下方的纯金属化合，就生出了氢。”

===Original===
“Dr. Murillo’s inside, now,” the radioman said. “Just came out of the inner airlock. He’ll be up as soon as he gets out of his pressure-suit.”

“As soon as he gets here, I’ll touch it off,” the bearded man said. “Everything set, de Jong?”

“Everything ready, Dr. Gomes,” one of his assistants assured him.

The door at the rear of the control-cabin opened, and Juan Murillo, the seismologist, entered, followed by an assistant. Murillo was a big man, copper-skinned, barrel-chested; he looked like a third—or fourth—generation Martian, of Andes Indian ancestry. He came forward and stood behind Gomes’ chair, looking down at the instruments. His assistant stopped at the door. This assistant was not human. He was a biped, vaguely humanoid, but he had four arms and a face like a lizard’s, and, except for some equipment on a belt, he was entirely naked.

===Chinese===
“穆里略博士已经进来了，”无线电员说。“刚出内闸。他一脱掉压力服就上来。”

“他一到我就起爆，”蓄胡子的男人说。“都准备好了吗，德容（de Jong）？”

“一切就绪，戈麦斯（Gomes）博士，”他的一名助手向他保证。

控制舱后部的门开了，地震学家胡安·穆里略（Juan Murillo）走了进来，身后跟着一名助手。穆里略是个大个子，古铜色皮肤，桶状的胸膛；看上去像个第三代或第四代的火星移民，有安第斯印第安人血统。他走上前来，站在戈麦斯的椅子后面，低头看仪表。他的助手在门口停住。这位助手不是人类。他是两足动物，隐约有点人形，却长着四条胳膊和一张蜥蜴般的脸，除了腰带上挂的些装备，周身一丝不挂。

===Original===
He spoke rapidly to Murillo, in a squeaking jabber. Murillo turned.

“Yes, if you wish, Gorkrink,” he said, in the English-Spanish-Afrikaans-Portuguese mixture that was Sixth Century, AE, Lingua Terra. Then he turned back to Gomes as the Ulleran sat down in a chair by the door.

“Well, she’s all yours, Lourenço, shoot the works.”

Gomes stabbed the radio-detonator button in front of him. A voice came out of the P.A.-speaker overhead: “In sixty seconds, the bombs will be detonated … thirty seconds … fifteen seconds … ten seconds … five seconds, four seconds, three seconds, two seconds, one second. …”

Out on the rolling skyline, fifty miles away, a lancelike ray of blue-white light shot up into the gathering dusk—a clump of five rays, really, from five deep shafts in an irregular pentagon half a mile across, blended into one by the distance. An instant later, there was a blinding flash, like sheet-lightning, and a huge ball of varicolored fire belched upward, leaving a series of smoke-rings to float more slowly after it. That fireball flattened, then spread to form the mushroom-head of a column of incandescent gas that mounted to overtake it, engorging the smoke-rings as it rose, twisting, writhing, changing shape, turning to dark smoke in one moment and belching flame and crackling with lightning the next. The armor-tender began to pitch and roll; it was all the engineer and one of the assistants could do, together, to keep it level.

===Chinese===
他朝穆里略吱吱嘎嘎地飞快说了一通。穆里略转过身来。

“行，随你的便，戈克林克，”他说。他说的是原子纪元（AE）第六世纪的地球语（Lingua Terra）——一种英语、西班牙语、南非荷兰语、葡萄牙语的混合语。随后，趁那乌勒尔人在门边一张椅子上落座，他转回去面对戈麦斯。

“好，这就全归你了，洛伦索（Lourenço），放吧。”

戈麦斯猛地按下面前的无线电起爆钮。头顶的扩音喇叭里传出一个声音：“六十秒后，炸弹即将起爆……三十秒……十五秒……十秒……五秒，四秒，三秒，两秒，一秒。……”

五十英里外，在起伏的天际线上，一道长矛般的蓝白色光柱射入渐浓的暮色——其实是五道光，出自五个深井，排成直径半英里的不规则五边形，被距离揉成了一束。转瞬间，炫目的闪光乍现，宛如片状闪电，一大团五色斑斓的火球向上喷薄而出，身后留下一串烟环，慢悠悠地跟着往上升。火球先是变扁，继而摊开，成为一根白炽气柱顶端的蘑菇头；气柱蒸腾直上，追赶火球，一路把烟环吞进肚里，扭曲着，翻搅着，变幻着形状，一时化作黑烟，一时又喷出烈焰、炸响串串闪电。装甲勤务车开始颠簸摇摆；总工程师和一名助手两人合力，才算勉强把它稳平。

===Original===
“In about half an hour,” the large young man told the girl, “the real fireworks should be starting. What’s coming up now is just small debris from the nuclear blast. When the shockwaves get down far enough to crack things open, the gas’ll come up, and then steam and ash, and then the magma. This one ought to be twice as good as the one we shot three months ago; it ought to be every bit as good as Krakatoa, on Terra, in 59 Pre-Atomic.”

“Well, even this much was worth staying over for,” the girl said, watching the screen.

“You going on to Uller on the *City of Canberra*?” Lourenço Gomes asked. “I wish I were; I have to stay over and make another shot, in a month or so, and I’ve had about all of Niflheim I can take, now. The sooner I get onto a planet where they don’t ration the air, the better I’ll like it.”

“Well, what do you know!” the large young man with the hairy legs mock-marveled. “He doesn’t like our nice planet!”

“Nice planet!” Gomes muttered something. “They call Terra God’s Footstool; well, I’ll give you one guess who uses this thing to prop his cloven hoofs on.”

===Chinese===
“再过半个钟头左右，”大块头年轻人告诉姑娘，“真正的烟花就该开场了。现在升上来的，不过是核爆崩起的小碎屑。等冲击波钻得够深、把地层震裂，气体就会上来，然后是蒸汽和灰烬，然后是岩浆。这一炮该比三个月前那一炮强上一倍；该跟前原子纪元（Pre-Atomic）59年地球上喀拉喀托（Krakatoa）火山那一场完全不相上下。”

“嗯，就算只有这点看头，多留这一趟也值了，”姑娘望着屏幕说。

“你要乘「堪培拉城」号（City of Canberra）去乌勒尔（Uller）？”洛伦索·戈麦斯问。“我真想也去；可我还得留下来，一个月左右之后再打一炮，而尼福尔海姆这地方，我已经受够够的了。能早点登上一个空气不用配给的行星，我才高兴呢。”

“嗬，这可稀奇了！”腿毛浓密的大块头年轻人假意惊叹。“他居然不喜欢咱们这颗好行星！”

“好行星！”戈麦斯咕哝了一句什么。“人家管地球叫上帝的脚凳；好吧，谁拿这玩意儿垫他那双分瓣的蹄子，我让你猜一次。”

===Original===
“When are you going to Terra?” the girl asked him.

“Terra? I don’t know, a year, two years. But I’m going to Uller on the next ship—the *City of Pretoria*—if we get the next blast off in time. They want me to design some improvements on a couple of power-reactors, so I’ll probably see you when I get there.”

“Here she comes!” the chief engineer called. “Watch the base of the column!”

The pillar of fiery smoke and dust, still boiling up from where the bombs had gone off far underground, was being violently agitated at the bottom. A series of new flashes broke out, lifting and spreading the incandescent radioactive gasses, and then a great gush of flame rose. A column of pure hydrogen must have rushed up into the vacuum created by the explosion; the next blast of flame, in a lateral sheet, came at nearly ten thousand feet above the ground, and great rags of fire, changing from red to violet and back through the spectrum to red again, went soaring away to dissipate in the upper atmosphere. Then geysers of hot ash and molten rock spouted upward; some of the white-hot debris landed almost at the acid river, halfway to the armor-tender.

“We’ve started a first-class earthquake, too,” the Hispano-Indian Martian Murillo said, looking at the instruments. “About six big cracks opening in the rock-structure. You know, when this quiets down and cools off, we’ll have more ore on the surface than we can handle in ten years, and more than we could have mined by ordinary means in fifty.”

===Chinese===
“你什么时候去地球？”姑娘问他。

“地球？说不准，一年，两年吧。不过下一班船——「比勒陀利亚城」号（City of Pretoria）——我就去乌勒尔，只要我们下一炮能按时打出去。他们要我给几座动力反应堆搞些改进设计，所以到那儿以后，咱们八成还能见着。”

“来了！”总工程师喊道。“看烟柱底下！”

那根烈焰般的烟尘之柱还在从深深的地下爆心不断翻腾上涌，柱底却已遭到猛烈的搅动。一连串新的闪光迸发出来，把白炽的放射性气体掀升、摊散，紧接着一大股烈焰腾起。准是有一柱纯氢冲进了爆炸造成的真空；紧接着那阵火焰化作横里的一片，在离地将近一万英尺的高空炸开，大片大片破布似的火焰从红转紫、又循着光谱一路变回红色，翻飞着远去，在高空大气中消散。随后，滚烫的灰烬和熔岩像间歇泉一般向上喷涌；一些白炽的碎屑几乎落到那条酸河边上——那已经是爆点与装甲勤务车之间的半途了。

“咱们还引发了一场一等一的地震，”西班牙-印第安裔的火星人穆里略看着仪表说。“岩层结构里大约张开了六道大裂缝。你们想想，等这一切平息下来、冷却之后，露在地表的矿石会多得咱们十年也处理不完，比用常规手段五十年采出来的还要多。”

===Original===
About four miles from the original blast, another eruption began with a terrific gas-explosion.

“Well, that finishes our work,” the large young man said, going to a kitbag in the corner of the cabin and getting out a bottle. “Get some of those plastic cups, over there, somebody; this one calls for a drink.”

“That’s right,” Gomes said. “You do something once, it may be an accident; you repeat the performance, and it’s a success.” He began pushing papers aside on his desk, and the girl in the too-ample coveralls brought drinking cups.

The Ulleran, in the background, rose quickly and squeaked apologetically. Murillo nodded. “Yes, of course, Gorkrink. No need for you to stay here.” The Ulleran went out, closing the door behind him.

===Chinese===
距原爆点约四英里处，又一场喷发以一声骇人的气体爆炸拉开了序幕。

“好，咱们的活儿到此完工，”大块头年轻人说着，走到舱角的行囊前，掏出一瓶酒来。“哪位去把塑料杯拿几个来，那边就有；这一炮值得喝一杯。”

“说的是，”戈麦斯道。“一件事干成一次，兴许是撞大运；再干成一次，那就是成功了。”他动手把桌上的文件推到一边，那个穿着过于肥大的连体工装的姑娘把杯子拿了来。

坐在后排的乌勒尔人急忙起身，吱吱地道歉。穆里略点点头。“是的，当然，戈克林克。你不必待在这儿。”乌勒尔人出去了，随手把门带上。

===Original===
“That taboo against Ullerans and Terrans watching each other eat and drink,” Murillo said. “What is that, part of their religion?”

“No, it’s their version of modesty,” the girl replied. “Like some of our sex-inhibitions, which they can’t even begin to understand. … But you were speaking to him in Lingua Terra; I didn’t know any of them understood it.”

“Gorkrink does,” Murillo said, uncorking the bottle and pouring into the plastic cups. “None of them can speak it, of course, because of the structure of their vocal organs, any more than we can speak their languages without artificial aids. But I can talk to him in Lingua Terra without having to put one of those damn gags in my mouth, and he can pass my instructions on to the others. He’s been a big help; I’ll be sorry to lose him.”

“Lose him?”

===Chinese===
“那个不许乌勒尔人和地球人（Terran）互相观看吃喝的禁忌，”穆里略说。“那是怎么档子事，他们宗教的一部分？”

“不，那是他们的羞耻观，”姑娘答道。“就像我们的一些性禁忌，他们压根儿没法理解。……不过你刚才跟他讲的是地球语；我还不知道他们中间有人听得懂呢。”

“戈克林克就听得懂，”穆里略说着，拔开瓶塞，往塑料杯里斟酒。“当然，他们谁也说不了地球语，因为发声器官构造不对——就像我们不借助人工器械也说不了他们的语言一样。可我跟他讲地球语，不必往嘴里塞那类该死的口衔，他还能把我的指示转达给其他人。他帮了大忙；要送走他，我怪舍不得的。”

“送走他？”

===Original===
“Yes, his year’s up; he’s going back to Uller on the *Canberra*. You know, it’s impossible to keep some trace of fluorine from the air in the handling-machines, or even out on the orbiters, and it plays the devil with their lungs. He wanted to stay on another three months, to help with the next shot, but the medics wouldn’t hear of it. … He’s from Keegark, wherever on Uller that is; claims to be a prince, or something. I know all the other geeks kowtow to him. But he’s a damn good worker. Very smart; picks things up the first time you tell him. I’ll recommend him unqualifiedly for any kind of work with contragravity or mechanized equipment.”

They all had drinks, now, except the chief engineer, who wanted a rain-check on his.

“Well, here’s to us,” Murillo said. “The first A-bomb miners in history. …”

===Chinese===
“是啊，他的一年期满，要乘「堪培拉」号（Canberra）回乌勒尔去了。要知道，作业机里的空气总免不了带些氟的痕迹，连外面的轨道站上也免不了，这对他们的肺害处大着呢。他还想再留三个月，帮忙打下一炮，可医官们说什么也不答应。……他是基加克（Keegark）人——管它在乌勒尔的什么地方；自称是什么亲王之类的。反正我就知道，别的土佬（geek）个个对他磕头。不过他干活是真没得挑。脑子非常灵；什么事教一遍就会。凡是反重力或机械化设备的活儿，我都可以毫无保留地推荐他。”

这时人人都有了酒，唯独总工程师除外——他那杯要求记下，改日再补。

“来，为咱们干杯，”穆里略说。“有史以来第一批原子弹矿工。……”

## II Rakkeed, Stalin, and the Rev. Keeluk / 第二章 拉基德、斯大林与基卢克神父

===Original===
Von Schlichten, in a fresh uniform, sat at the end of the table in Sidney Harrington’s office; Harrington and Eric Blount, the Lieutenant-Governor, faced each other across it, over the three-foot disc of an Ulleran chessboard. Harrington had the white, or center, position. Blount, sandy-haired and considerably younger, was playing black, and his pieces were closing in relentlessly from the outer rim.

“Well, then what?” Harrington asked.

Von Schlichten dropped ash from his cigarette into the tray that served all three of them.

===Chinese===
冯·施利希滕换上一身崭新制服，坐在希德尼·哈灵顿办公室的桌子一端；哈灵顿与副总督埃里克·布朗特隔着桌子对坐，当中是乌勒尔棋盘那三英尺的圆盘面。哈灵顿执白，也就是居中之位。头发沙色、年轻得多的布朗特执黑，他的棋子正从外缘毫不留情地步步围拢。

「那么，后来怎么样？」哈灵顿问。

冯·施利希滕把香烟上的烟灰弹进三人合用的烟灰盘里。

===Original===
“Nothing much,” he replied. “Keeluk bugged out as soon as he saw my car let down. We picked up a few of his ragtag-and-bobtail, and they’re being questioned now, but I doubt if they’ll tell us anything we don’t know already. The dog had been kept in a lean-to back of the house; it had been removed, probably as soon as Keeluk called in his goon-gang. At least one of the rabbits had been kept on the premises, too, some time ago. No trace of the goat.”

He watched Blount move one of his pieces and nodded approvingly. “The riot’s been put down,” he continued, “but we’re keeping two companies of Kragans in the city, and about a dozen airjeeps patrolling the section from Eightieth down to Sixty-fourth, and from the waterfront back to Eighth Avenue. There is also the equivalent of a regiment of King Jaikark’s infantry﻿—spearmen, crossbowmen, and a few riflemen﻿—and two of those outsize cavalry companies of his, helping hold the lid down. They’re making mass arrests, indiscriminately. More slaves for Jaikark’s court favorite, of course.”

“Or else Gurgurk wants them to use for patronage,” Blount added. “He’s been building quite a political organization, lately. Getting ready to shove Jaikark off the throne, I’d say.”

===Chinese===
「没什么大事，」他答道。「基卢克一看见我的车降落，撒腿就溜了。我们抓了他几个下三滥的喽啰，眼下正在受审，不过我看他们说不出什么我们还不知道的事。那条狗原先养在屋后一间披棚里；已经被转移了，多半是基卢克刚一把他的打手帮召来就挪走了。至少还有一只兔子，前些时候也在这宅子里养过。山羊则毫无踪影。」

他看着布朗特走了一枚子，赞许地点点头。「暴乱已经压下去了，」他接着说，「城里我们还留着两个连的克拉冈人，外加约莫一打飞行吉普，在从第八十街到第六十四街、从滨水区到第八大道的地段巡逻。另外还有相当于一个团的贾伊卡克王步兵——长矛手、弩手，再加少量步枪手——以及他那两支编制超大的骑兵连，帮着把场面按住。他们正不分青红皂白地大抓人。当然，这又是给贾伊卡克的宫廷宠臣添奴隶。」

「要不然，就是古古尔克想把这批人留作自己笼络人心的本钱，」布朗特补充道。「他近来正在搭一个颇为像样的政治组织。依我看，这是准备把贾伊卡克从王座上掀下去。」

===Original===
Harrington pushed one of his pieces out along a radial line toward the rim. Blount promptly took a pawn, which, under Ulleran rules, entitled him to a second move. He shifted another piece, a sort of combination knight and bishop, to threaten the piece Harrington had moved.

“Oh, Gurgurk wouldn’t dare try anything like that,” the Governor-General said. “He knows we wouldn’t let him get away with it. We have too much of an investment in King Jaikark.”

“Then why’s Gurgurk been supporting this damned Rakkeed?” Blount wanted to know, hastily interposing a piece. “Gurgurk can follow one of two lines of policy. He can undertake to heave Jaikark off the throne and seize power, or he has to support Jaikark on the throne. We’re subsidizing Jaikark. Rakkeed has been preaching this crusade against the Terrans, and against Jaikark, whom we control. Gurgurk has been subsidizing Rakkeed.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
哈灵顿把一枚棋子沿辐射线朝外缘推出。布朗特随即吃掉一兵——按乌勒尔棋规，吃兵者有权再走一步。他又挪动另一枚棋子——一种马象合体的角色——去威胁哈灵顿走出的那枚子。

「噢，古古尔克可不敢来这一手，」总督说。「他知道我们不会让他得逞。我们在贾伊卡克王身上下的本钱太大了。」

「那古古尔克干吗一直资助这个该死的拉基德？」布朗特急忙插上一子隔挡，非要问个明白。「古古尔克的政策路线无非两条：要么动手把贾伊卡克掀下王座、自己夺权；要么就得扶持贾伊卡克稳坐王座。我们在出钱供养贾伊卡克。拉基德一直在鼓吹这场圣战，既反地球人，也反我们掌控的贾伊卡克。而古古尔克一直在出钱供养拉基德……」

===Original===
“You haven’t any proof of that,” Harrington protested.

“My Intelligence Section has,” von Schlichten put in. “We can give sums of money, and dates, and the names of the intermediaries through whom they were paid to Rakkeed. Eric is absolutely correct in making that statement.”

“Personally, I think Gurgurk’s plan is something like this: Rakkeed will stir up anti-Terran sentiment here in Konkrook, and direct it against our puppet, Jaikark, as well as against us,” Blount said. “When the outbreak comes, Jaikark will be killed, and then Gurgurk will step in, seize the Palace, and use the Royal army to put down the revolt that he’s incited in the first place. That will put him in the position of the friend of the Company, and most of his dupes will be rounded up and sold as slaves, and King Gurgurk’ll pocket the proceeds. The only question is, will Rakkeed let himself be used that way? I think Rakkeed’s bigger than Gurgurk ever can be. And more of a threat to the Company. Everywhere we turn, Rakkeed’s at the bottom of whatever happens to be wrong. This business, for instance; Keeluk’s one of Rakkeed’s followers.”

===Chinese===
「你可拿不出任何证据，」哈灵顿抗议道。

「我的情报处拿得出，」冯·施利希滕插话。「金额、日期，还有经手把钱转给拉基德的中间人的名字，我们都能一一列出。埃里克这话说得千真万确。」

「依我个人看，古古尔克的盘算大致是这样：拉基德会在孔克鲁克这里煽起反地球人的情绪，把这股情绪既引向我们的傀儡贾伊卡克，也引向我们，」布朗特说。「等暴乱一发，贾伊卡克就会被杀，古古尔克随即出手，夺取王宫，动用王家军队去平定这场本来就是他煽起来的叛乱。这么一来，他便坐上了公司之友的位置，而他那些受骗的喽啰，大多数会被一网打尽、卖作奴隶，古古尔克王则把进账揣进腰包。唯一的问题是：拉基德肯让人这么利用吗？依我看，拉基德的格局，是古古尔克一辈子也够不着的。对公司而言，他也是更大的威胁。我们走到哪儿，哪儿出了岔子，追到根上总有拉基德。就拿这桩事来说——基卢克就是拉基德的党羽。」

===Original===
“Eric, you have Rakkeed on the brain!” Harrington exclaimed impatiently, then moved the threatened piece counterclockwise on the circle where he had placed it. “He’s just a barbarian caravan-driver.”

Eric Blount moved the piece that had taken Harrington’s pawn.

“Your king’s in danger,” he warned. “And Hitler was just a paperhanger.”

“Rakkeed has no following, except among the rabble.” Harrington puffed furiously at his pipe, trying to figure the best protection for his king.

===Chinese===
「埃里克，你满脑子都是拉基德！」哈灵顿不耐烦地叫道，一面把受威胁的那枚子沿它所在的圆圈逆时针挪开。「他不过是个蛮子商队赶牲口的。」

埃里克·布朗特走动了那枚吃掉哈灵顿之兵的棋子。

「你的王告急了，」他提醒道。「而希特勒当年也不过是个裱糊匠。」

「拉基德没有追随者，除了那帮地痞贱民。」哈灵顿猛抽着烟斗，琢磨怎么给自家的王寻一条最稳妥的护身之路。

===Original===
“You just think he hasn’t,” Blount retorted. “Here in Konkrook, he’s always entertained by one or another of the big ship-owning nobles. They probably deprecate his table-manners, but they just love his politics. And the same thing at Keegark, and at the Free Cities along the Eastern Shore.”

“The last time Rakkeed was in Konkrook, he was the guest of the Keegarkan Ambassador,” von Schlichten stated. “Intelligence got that from a spy we’d planted among the embassy servants.”

“You sure this spy wasn’t just romancing?” Harrington asked. “You get so confounded many wild stories about Rakkeed. Three days after he was reported here at Konkrook, he was reported at Skilk, five thousand miles away, said to be having an audience with King Firkked.”

===Chinese===
「那只是你以为没有，」布朗特反唇相讥。「就在这孔克鲁克，回回款待他的，不是这个就是那个拥船巨室的大贵族。他们多半看不上他的餐桌做派，可爱极了他的政治主张。基加克如此，东岸一线的自由城邦也是如此。」

「拉基德上一回来孔克鲁克，做的是基加克大使的客人，」冯·施利希滕说。「情报处这条消息，出自我们安插在使馆仆役中的一个密探。」

「你敢担保这密探不是在瞎编？」哈灵顿问。「关于拉基德的天方夜谭多得要命。这边刚报他现身孔克鲁克，三天后又报他在五千英里外的斯基尔克，据说正蒙菲尔克德王召见。」

===Original===
“No mystery to that,” von Schlichten said. “He travels on our ships, in disguise, coolie-class, on the geek-deck.”

“Be a good idea if he could be caught at it, some time,” Blount said, making another move. “One of the lower-deck loading ports could be left unlocked, by carelessness, and he could blunder overboard at about five thousand feet.” He watched Harrington make a deceptively pointless-looking move. “Sid, this damn dog business worries me.”

“Worries me, too. I’m fond of that mutt, and God only knows what sort of stuff he’s been getting to eat. And I hate to think of why those geeks stole him, too.”

===Chinese===
「这没什么可怪的，」冯·施利希滕说。「他坐我们的船旅行，化了装，买苦力舱的票，蹲在土佬甲板上。」

「哪回要是能叫他在船上栽个跟头就好了，」布朗特说着又走一步。「下层甲板某个装货口，完全可能因为疏忽没上锁，他呢，也就完全可能在五千英尺上下一脚踩空，栽出船外。」他看着哈灵顿走了一步看似毫无意义、实则暗藏机锋的棋。「席德，这条狗的破事让我心里发毛。」

「我也发毛。我喜欢那条狗，天知道它这一向吃进去的是些什么玩意儿。还有，那些土佬为什么要偷它——我也不敢往深里想。」

===Original===
“Well, at risk of seeming heartless, I’m not so much worried for Stalin as I am about why Keeluk was hiding him, and why he was willing to murder the only two Terrans in Konkrook who trust him, to prevent our finding out that he had him.”

“A Mr. Keeluk, a clergyman,” von Schlichten quoted. He chain-lit another cigarette and stubbed out the old one. “Maybe the Rev. Keeluk wanted Stalin for sacramental purposes.”

Blount looked up sharply. “Ritual killing?” he asked. “Or sympathetic magic?”

===Chinese===
「这么说吧，哪怕显得我没心没肺：我担心的与其说是斯大林的安危，不如说是基卢克为什么要把他藏起来，为什么为了不让我们发现这事，竟肯谋杀孔克鲁克全城仅有的两个信任他的地球人。」

「一位基卢克先生，神职人员，」冯·施利希滕引述道。他就着旧烟点起一支新烟，随手把烟蒂摁灭。「说不定基卢克神父要斯大林，是为了做圣事。」

布朗特霍地抬起头。「仪式杀祭？」他问。「还是交感巫术？」

===Original===
Von Schlichten shrugged. “Take your choice. Maybe Rakkeed wanted the dog, to kill before a congregation of his followers, killing us by proxy, or in effigy. Or maybe they think we worship Stalin, and getting control of him would give them power over us. I wish we knew a little more about Ulleran psychology.”

That wasn’t the first time he’d made that wish. Even if sex weren’t the paramount psychological factor the ancient Freudians believed, it was an extremely important one, and on Uller most of the fundamental terms of Terran psychology were meaningless. At the same time, the average Ulleran probably had complexes and neuroses that would have had Freud talking to himself, and they certainly indulged in practices that would have even stood Krafft-Ebing’s hair on end.

“One thing,” Blount said. “It doesn’t take any Ulleran psychologist to know that about eighty percent of them hate us poisonously.”

===Chinese===
冯·施利希滕耸耸肩。「随你挑。也许是拉基德想要那条狗，好当着他满堂信徒的面宰了它，算是借手要我们的命，或是杀我们的替身草人。也许他们以为我们崇拜斯大林，控制了他，就等于攥住了拿捏我们的把柄。真希望我们对乌勒尔人的心理能多知道一点。」

这个愿望，他不是头一回许了。即便性并非古代弗洛伊德学派信奉的那种压倒一切的心理因素，它也是极端重要的一环；而在乌勒尔，地球人心理学的那些基本概念大半都无从谈起。可与此同时，寻常一个乌勒尔人，心里的情结与神经症，恐怕多得能让弗洛伊德自言自语；他们沉溺的种种勾当，更是连克拉夫特-埃宾（Krafft-Ebing）见了都要毛发倒竖。

「有一点，」布朗特说。「这用不着什么乌勒尔心理学家：他们十个里头约莫八个，恨我们恨得入骨。」

===Original===
“Oh, rubbish!” Harrington blew the exclamation out around his pipe-stem with a gush of smoke. “A few fanatics hate us, and a few merchants who lost money when we replaced this primitive barter economy of theirs, but nine-tenths of them have benefited enormously from us, and continue to benefit.﻿ ﻿…”

“And hate us more deeply with each new benefit,” Blount added. “They resent everything we’ve done for them.”

“Yes, this spaceport proposition of King Orgzild of Keegark looks like it, now doesn’t it?” Harrington retorted. “He hates and resents us so much that he’s offered us a spaceport at his city.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「哦，胡扯！」哈灵顿含着烟斗嘴，一口烟气把这句斥骂喷了出来。「恨我们的，不过是一小撮狂热分子，外加几个商人——我们废了他们那套原始的以物易物经济，叫他们亏了本——可他们十之八九从我们身上得了天大的好处，如今还在继续得好处……」

「而且每多得一回好处，就把我们恨得更深一层，」布朗特补上一句。「我们替他们做的每一件事，他们都记恨。」

「可不是嘛，基加克的奥格齐尔德王这个航天港提议，正说明他们记恨，对不对？」哈灵顿反唇相讥。「他恨我们、怨我们恨成那样，恨得主动提出在他城里给我们修一座航天港……」

===Original===
“What’s it going to cost him?” Blount asked. “He furnishes the land﻿—sequestered from the estate of some noble he executed for treason﻿—and the labor﻿—all forced. We furnish the structural steel, the machine-equipment, the engineering. We get a spaceport we don’t really need, and he gets all the business it’ll bring to Keegark. Considering the fact that Rakkeed is a welcome guest at his embassy here, and at the Royal Palace at Keegark, I’m beginning to wonder if he isn’t fomenting trouble for us here at Konkrook to make us willing to move our main base to his city.”

He made a move. Instantly, Harrington slashed out from the middle of the board with one of his heavy-duty, all-purpose pieces and took a piece, then moved again.

“Now look whose king’s threatened!” he crowed.

===Chinese===
「这要他破费什么？」布朗特问。「地是他出——从一个让他以叛国罪处决的贵族名下抄没来的——劳工也是他出——清一色强征。结构钢、机器设备、工程技术，全是我们出。我们捞到一座自己并不真需要的航天港，他捞到这座港带给基加克的全部生意。再想想，拉基克在这里的基加克使馆是座上宾，到了基加克王宫也是座上宾，我倒犯起了嘀咕：他在孔克鲁克给我们搅的这些乱子，是不是就是要弄得我们甘愿把主基地挪到他城里去。」

他走了一步。哈灵顿立刻用一枚重型全能的棋子从盘心劈杀出去，吃掉一子，接着又走一步。

「现在瞧瞧谁的王告急了！」他得意地叫道。

===Original===
“Yes, I see.” Blount brought a piece clockwise around the board and took the threatening piece, then moved again. “I hope you see whose king’s threatened, now.”

Harrington swore, reached out to move a piece, and then jerked his hand back as though the piece were radioactive. For a while, he sat puffing his pipe and staring at the board.

“In fact, Orgzild’s so sure that we’re going to accept his offer that he’s started building two new power-reactors, to handle the additional power-demand that’ll result from the increased business,” Blount continued.

===Chinese===
「是，我瞧见了。」布朗特把一枚棋子沿盘面顺时针绕过来，吃掉那枚威胁之子，然后又走一步。「但愿你也瞧见了，现在告急的是谁的王。」

哈灵顿骂了一句，伸手要动子，又猛地缩手，仿佛那棋子带放射性。有一阵子，他只是坐着喷烟吐雾，两眼死盯着棋盘。

「实际上，奥格齐尔德吃准我们会接受他的提议，都已经动工在建两座新的动力反应堆了，专门应付生意做大之后新增的电力需求，」布朗特接着说。

===Original===
“Where’s he getting the plutonium?” von Schlichten asked.

“Where can he get it?” Harrington replied. “He just bought four tons of it from us, off the *City of Pretoria*.”

“That’s a hell of a lot of plutonium,” Blount said. “I wonder if he mightn’t have some idea of what else plutonium can be used for, beside generating power.”

===Chinese===
「他的钚从哪儿来？」冯·施利希滕问。

「还能从哪儿来？」哈灵顿答道。「他刚从我们手里买了四吨，货就从『比勒陀利亚城』号上交割。」

「四吨钚，可不是个小数目，」布朗特说。「我纳闷的是：除了发电，钚还能派什么用场——这一点，他心里是不是也有点数。」

===Original===
“Oh, God, I hope not!” Harrington exclaimed. “You’re going to get me started seeing burglars under the bed, next.﻿ ﻿…”

“Maybe there are burglars,” Blount said, pointing with his cigarette-holder to Harrington’s threatened king. “Can’t you do something about that, Sid?” Then he turned to von Schlichten. “Before we get off the subject, how about those letters the Rev. Keeluk gave to the Quinton girl?”

“All addressed to Skilkans known to be Rakkeed disciples and rabidly anti-Terran,” von Schlichten replied. “We radioed the list to Skilk; Colonel Cheng-Li, our intelligence man there, teleprinted us back a lot of material on them that looks like the Newgate Calendar. We turned the letters themselves over to Doc Petrie, the Ulleran philology sharp, who is a pretty fair cryptanalyst. He couldn’t find any indications of cipher, but there was a lot of gossip about Keeluk’s friends and parishioners which might have arbitrary code-meanings. I’m going to explain the situation to Miss Quinton, and advise her to have nothing to do with any of the people Keeluk gave her letters to.”

===Chinese===
「哦，上帝，可千万别！」哈灵顿叫道。「再这么说下去，你就要弄得我疑神疑鬼，连床底下都开始看见窃贼了……」

「说不定床底下真有窃贼，」布朗特说着，用烟嘴指了指哈灵顿那枚告急的王。「你就不能想点法子吗，席德？」说完他转向冯·施利希滕。「趁还没扯远——基卢克神父交给昆顿那姑娘的那些信，到底是怎么回事？」

「收信人清一色是斯基尔克人，个个都是挂了号的拉基德门徒、反地球人的死硬狂热分子，」冯·施利希滕答道。「我们把名单用无线电发到了斯基尔克；我们在那边的情报官郑立上校，回传过来他们的一大堆材料，读上去活脱一部《新门监狱纪事》（Newgate Calendar）。信件本身，我们交给了佩特里大夫（Doc Petrie）——乌勒尔语文学的行家，业余还是个不赖的密码分析员。他找不出什么密码的迹象，不过信里谈到基卢克的教友和教区信众的那些闲话，未必没有随意约定的暗码含义。我打算向昆顿小姐说明情况，劝她别跟基卢克托她带信的这些人里任何一个打交道。」

===Original===
Harrington had gotten his king temporarily out of danger, losing a piece doing it.

“Think she’ll listen to you?” he asked. “These Extraterrestrials’ Rights Association people are a lot of blasted fanatics, themselves. We’re a gang of bloody-handed, flint-hearted, imperialistic sons of bitches in their book, and anything we say’s sure to be a Hitler-sized lie.”

“Oh, they’re not as bad as all that. I never met the girl before today, but old Mohammed Ferriera’s a decent bloke. And their association’s really done a lot of good. For one thing, they put an end to the peonage system on Yggdrasill, and I know what conditions were like, there, before they did.”

===Chinese===
哈灵顿已让自家的王暂且脱险——代价是折损一子。

「你以为她会听你的？」他问。「地外种族权利协会那帮家伙，自己就是一群该死的狂热分子。在他们那本账上，我们是一伙血手黑心、帝国主义的狗娘养的；我们嘴里说出来的话，一准是希特勒量级的大谎。」

「哦，也没到那个地步。那姑娘我今天才头一回见，不过老穆罕默德·费雷拉是个正派汉子。他们那个协会也确实办过不少好事。就说一件吧，伊格德拉西尔（Yggdrasill）星上的债役制就是他们终结的，而在那之前，那地方是什么光景，我可是亲眼见识过的。」

===Original===
A calculating look came into Harrington’s eye. He puffed slowly at his pipe and slid a piece from the center toward the sector of the board nearest him. Blount whistled softly and made a quick rearrangement.

“Carlos, did you say she told you she was going to Skilk, in the near future?” Harrington asked. “Well, look here; you’re going up that way, yourself, with that battalion of Kragans, on the *Aldebaran*. Why don’t you invite her to make the trip with you? You can be quite attractive to young ladies, when you try, and she’ll be grateful for that rescue this afternoon, which is always a good foundation. Maybe you can plant a couple of ideas where they’ll do the most good. She’s only been here for three months﻿—since the *Canberra* got in from Niflheim. You know and I know and we all know that there are a lot of things up there at the polar mines that would look like hell to anybody who didn’t understand local conditions.﻿ ﻿…”

“Well, Miss Quinton’s company won’t be any particularly heavy cross for me to bear,” von Schlichten replied. “I won’t guarantee anything, of course.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
哈灵顿眼里浮起一丝精于算计的神色。他慢条斯理地抽着烟斗，把一枚棋子从盘心推向靠自己最近的那个扇区。布朗特轻轻吹了声口哨，飞快地重新布了防。

「卡洛斯，你方才是不是说，她告诉你她最近要上斯基尔克去？」哈灵顿问。「嘿，这么着——你自己不久也要往那边去，带上那个营的克拉冈人，坐『毕宿五』号。何不请她与你同行？只要你肯上心，你在年轻小姐跟前还是挺有魅力的；再说今天下午你救她于水火，她必然心存感激——这向来是块好地基。兴许你还能顺势栽进去几个念头，叫它们在最见效的地方生根。她来这儿不过三个月——『堪培拉』号从尼福尔海姆抵达至今。你知我知，大家全知道，极地矿区那边有些事，落在不懂当地实情的人眼里，简直就是地狱……」

「唔，有昆顿小姐作伴，这副十字架背起来倒也不算特别沉重，」冯·施利希滕答道。「当然，我什么也不担保……」

===Original===
The intercom-speaker on the table whistled several times. Harrington swore, laid down his pipe, and got up, brushing ashes from the front of his coat. He flipped a switch and spoke into the box.

“Governor,” a voice replied out of it, “there’s a geek procession just landed from a water-barge in front, and is coming up the roadway to Company House. A platoon of Jaikark’s Household Guards, with rifles; the Spear of State; a royal litter; about thirty geek nobles, on foot; a gift-litter; another platoon of riflemen, if you say the last syllable quick enough.”

“That’ll be Gurgurk, coming to tell us how unhappy his Sodden and Inebriated Geekship is about that fracas on Seventy-second Street,” Harrington said. “The gift-litter will contain the customary indemnity, at the current market quotation. Have Gurgurk and party admitted, all but the rifle-platoons; give him an honor guard of our Kragans, and keep his own gun-toters outside. Take them to the Reception Hall, and hold them there till I signal from the Audience Hall, and then herd them in.”

===Chinese===
桌上的内部通话喇叭哨音连响了几声。哈灵顿骂了一句，搁下烟斗起身，掸去上衣前襟的烟灰。他扳下一个开关，对着匣子说话。

「总督，」匣子里一个声音答道，「前面有一队土佬仪仗刚下了水驳船，正沿大路朝公司大楼过来。一个排的贾伊卡克王宫卫队，带步枪；国矛一柄；王辇一乘；约莫三十名土佬贵族，步行；礼辇一乘；再一个排的步枪兵——这最后一样，得您末一个音节报得够快才算有。」

「那准是古古尔克，来向我们转达他那酩酊烂醉、泡在酒缸里的土佬王上，对七十二街那场乱子有多么痛心，」哈灵顿说。「礼辇里装的照例是赔偿，按现时行情折算。放古古尔克一行进来，两个步枪排除外；给他配一队我们的克拉冈人作仪仗，他自家那些挎枪的都留在门外。把他们带到接待厅，在那儿候着，等我从谒见厅发信号，再把他们领进去。」

===Original===
He came back and made a move. Immediately, Blount took one of his pieces, moved again, took another, and made the third move to which he was entitled.

“I’ll mate you in four moves,” he predicted. “Want to play it out, before we go down?”

“Sure; what’s time to a geek? Gurgurk’d think we were worried about something if we didn’t keep him waiting.﻿ ﻿… Good Lord! You do have me over a barrel, Eric!”

===Chinese===
他回到座上走了一步。布朗特应声吃掉他一子，再走一步，又吃一子，然后把他有权走的第三步也走完。

「再走四步，我就将死你，」他预言。「下去之前，把这盘下完如何？」

「下完当然好；土佬还怕等？咱们要是不晾他一晾，古古尔克还当咱们在为什么事犯愁呢……天哪！艾瑞克，你可是真把我按在案板上了！」

## VI The Bad News Came After the Coffee / 六 咖啡过后，噩耗传来

===Original===
The last clatter of silverware and dishes ceased as the native servants finished clearing the table. There was a remaining clatter of cups and saucers; liqueur-glasses tinkled, and an occasional cigarette-lighter clicked. At the head table, the voices seemed louder.

“… don’t like it a millisol’s worth,” Brigadier-General Barney Mordkovitz, the Skilk military C.O., was saying to the lady on his right. “They’re too confounded meek. Nowadays, nobody yells ‘*Znidd suddabit!*’ at you. Nobody sticks all four thumbs in his mouth and waves his fingers. Nobody commits nuisance on the sidewalk in front of you. They just stand and look at you like a farmer looking at a turkey the week before Christmas, and that I don’t like!”

“Oh, bosh!” Jules Keaveney, the Skilk Resident-Agent, at the head of the table, exclaimed. “You soldiers are all alike﻿—begging your pardon, General von Schlichten,” he nodded in the direction of the guest of honor. “If they don’t bow and scrape to you and get off the sidewalk to let you pass, you say they’re insolent and need a lesson. If they do, you say they’re plotting insurrection.”

“What I said,” Mordkovitz repeated, “was that I expect a certain amount of disorder, and a certain minimum show of hostility toward us from some of these geeks, to conform to what I know to be our unpopularity with many of them. When I don’t find it, I want to know why.”

===Chinese===
土著仆役收拾完餐桌，银餐具和碟盘的最后一阵磕碰声终于停了。杯碟的脆响犹在；利口酒杯叮叮当当，打火机偶尔咔哒一响。主桌上，人声似乎更响亮了些。

“……这事我一毫索尔都不喜欢。”斯基尔克驻军司令、准将巴尼·莫德科维茨正对他右手边的女士说。“他们温顺得邪门。如今再没人冲你喊「Znidd suddabit!」（土佬语咒骂）了。再没人把四根大拇指全塞进嘴里朝你晃手指了。再没人当着你的面在人行道上便溺了。他们只是站在那儿打量你，活像农夫在圣诞节前一个星期打量一只火鸡——偏偏这种样子，我不喜欢！”

“哦，胡扯！”坐在首席的斯基尔克驻扎代理朱尔斯·基夫尼嚷道。“你们当兵的全一个样——多包涵，冯·施利希滕将军，”他朝主宾那边点了点头。“他们要是不对你打躬作揖、不让开人行道给你过，你们就说他们无礼、欠教训；他们要是让了，你们又说他们图谋叛乱。”

“我方才说的，”莫德科维茨重复道，“是我指望看到一定分量的骚乱，指望这些土佬当中有些人对我们摆出起码的敌意，这才符合我明知的事实——我们在这伙人当中的许多家伙眼里并不受欢迎。如今找不到这种敌意了，我就想知道为什么。”

===Original===
“I’m inclined,” von Schlichten came to his subordinate’s support, “to agree. This sudden absence of overt hostility is disquieting. Colonel Cheng-Li,” he called on the local Intelligence officer and Constabulary chief. “This fellow Rakkeed was here, about a month ago. Was there any noticeable disorder at that time? Anti-Terran demonstrations, attacks on Company property or personnel, shooting at aircars, that sort of thing?”

“No more than usual, general. In fact, it was when Rakkeed came here that the condition General Mordkovitz was speaking of began to become conspicuous. We did catch some of Rakkeed’s disciples trying to get in among the enlisted men of the Tenth N.U.N.I. and the Fifth Zirk Cavalry and promote disaffection. That was reported at the time, sir.”

“And acted upon, as far as the civil administration would permit,” von Schlichten replied. “And I might say that Lieutenant-Governor Blount has reported from Keegark, where he is now, that the same unnatural absence of hostility exists there.”

“Well, of course, general,” Keaveney said patronizingly. “King Orgzild has things under pretty tight control at Keegark. He’d not allow a few fanatics to do anything to prejudice these spaceport negotiations.”

===Chinese===
“我倒倾向于，”冯·施利希滕声援部下道，“同意这个看法。公然敌意突然不见了，这本身就令人心神不定。郑立上校，”他点名当地的情报军官兼宪兵主管。“那个拉基德大约一个月前来过这里。当时可有什么显眼的骚乱？反地球人示威、袭击公司财产或人员、朝飞车开枪，诸如此类？”

“不比平常多，将军。事实上，正是在拉基德来过之后，莫德科维茨将军说的那种情况才开始显眼起来。我们确实抓到过拉基德的几个门徒，他们企图混进第十北乌勒尔土著步兵团和第五齐尔克骑兵团的士兵里煽动不满。这事当时就上报了，长官。”

“也处理了，在民政当局容许的范围内。”冯·施利希滕答道。“而且我可以说，副总督布朗特从他现在所在的基加克发来报告：那里同样存在这种反常的敌意缺失。”

“哦，那当然，将军。”基夫尼一副屈尊俯就的口气。“奥格齐尔德王在基加克把局面管得相当严。他绝不会容许几个狂热分子闹出什么事，坏了这场太空港谈判。”

===Original===
“I wonder if the idea back of that spaceport proposition isn’t to get us concentrated at Keegark, where Orgzild could wipe us all out in one surprise blow,” somebody down the table suggested.

“Oh, Orgzild wouldn’t be crazy enough to try anything like that,” Commander Dirk Prinsloo, of the *Aldebaran*, declared. “He’d get away with it for just twelve months﻿—the time it would take to get the news to Terra and for a Federation Space Navy task-force to get here. And then, there’d be little bits of radioactive geek floating around this system as far out as the orbit of Beta Hydrae VII.”

“That’s quite true,” von Schlichten agreed. “The point is, does Orgzild know it? I doubt if he even believes there is a Terra.”

“Then where in Space does he think we come from?” Keaveney demanded.

===Chinese===
“我倒怀疑，那项太空港提案背后的用意，是不是把我们调到基加克集中起来，好让奥格齐尔德一记闷棍把我们全体端掉。”桌子下首有人这么猜测。

“哦，奥格齐尔德还没疯到会试那一手。”“毕宿五”号的德克·普林斯鲁中校宣称。“他至多逍遥十二个月——消息传到地球，再等一支联邦太空海军特混舰队开到这儿，前后正好这么些时间。到那时候，这个星系里从内到外、直到水蛇座β第七行星的轨道，到处飘的都是一小块一小块带放射性的土佬。”

“这话不假。”冯·施利希滕表示同意。“问题在于：奥格齐尔德知道这一点吗？我怀疑他连有个地球都不信。”

“那他认为我们到底是从太空里哪儿来的？”基夫尼追问。

===Original===
“I believe he thinks Niflheim is our home world,” von Schlichten replied. “Or, rather, the string of orbiters and artificial satellites around Niflheim. Where he thinks Niflheim is, I wouldn’t even try to guess.”

“Well, it takes six months for a ship to go between here and Nif,” Prinsloo considered. “Because of the hyperdrive effects, the experienced time of the voyage, inside the ship, is of the order of three weeks. Taking that as the figure, he’d estimate the distance at about a quarter-million miles, assuming the velocity as being the speed of one of our contragravity-ships here on Uller. I’m assuming he doesn’t even know there is a hyperdrive.”

“Yes. After he’d wiped us out, he might even consider the idea of an invasion of Niflheim with captured contragravity ships,” Hideyoshi O’Leary chuckled. “That would be a big laugh﻿—if any of us were alive, then, to do any laughing.”

“You don’t really believe that, general?” Keaveney asked. His tone was still derisive, but under the derision was uncertainty. After all, von Schlichten had been on Uller for fifteen years, to his two.

===Chinese===
“我相信，他认为尼福尔海姆是我们的母星。”冯·施利希滕答道。“或者更确切地说，是环绕尼福尔海姆的那一串轨道站和人造卫星。至于他认为尼福尔海姆在什么地方，我连猜都不想猜。”

“唔，一艘船从这儿到尼夫要跑六个月。”普林斯鲁盘算道。“由于超光速驱动的效应，船上的人亲历的航程时间，约莫三个星期的量级。拿这个数当准数，再把航速假设成我们反重力飞船在乌勒尔本地的速度，他就会把距离估成二十五万英里上下。我这是假定他压根不知道世上有超光速驱动。”

“是啊。等把我们消灭干净，他没准儿还真会盘算着用缴获的反重力飞船远征尼福尔海姆。”秀吉·奥利里呵呵一笑。“那才叫天大的笑话——前提是到那时，我们中间还有活人笑得出来。”

“您不会真信这一套吧，将军？”基夫尼问。语气里仍是讥诮，可讥诮底下透着没底。毕竟，冯·施利希滕在乌勒尔待了十五年，他才两年。

===Original===
“Any question of geek psychology is wide open as far as I’m concerned; the longer I stay here, the less I understand it.” Von Schlichten finished his brandy and got out cigarette-case and lighter. “I have an idea of the sort of garbled reports these spies of his who spend a year on Niflheim as laborers bring back.”

“You know the line Rakkeed’s been taking, of course,” Colonel Cheng-Li put in. “He as much as says that Niflheim’s our home, and that the farms where we raise food here, and those evergreen plantings on Konk Isthmus and between here and Grank are the beginning of an attempt to drive all native life from this planet and make it over for ourselves.”

“And that savage didn’t think an idea like that up for himself; he got it from somebody like Orgzild,” the black-bearded brigadier-general added. “You know, the main base off Niflheim is practically self-supporting, with hydroponic-gardens and animal-tissue culture vats. And it’s enough bigger than one of the *City* ships to pass for a little world. Yes, somebody like Orgzild, or King Firkked here, could easily pick up the idea that that’s our home planet.”

“But King Kankad was talking about.﻿ ﻿…” Paula Quinton began.

===Chinese===
“依我看，土佬心理学的一切问题至今全无定论；我在这里待得越久，越是弄不懂。”冯·施利希滕喝干白兰地，取出烟盒和打火机。“他手下的间谍在尼福尔海姆装一年劳工再回去，带回的是些什么走了样的报告，我心里多少有数。”

“拉基德近来放的口风，诸位当然知道。”郑立上校插话。“他差不多等于明说：尼福尔海姆是我们的老家；我们在这儿经营产粮的那些农场，还有孔克地峡上和从这里到格兰克之间的那些常绿种植带，就是把本地一切生灵逐出这颗行星、把这行星改造成我们自家家园的第一步。”

“再说，那种想法不是那野蛮人自己想出来的；他是从奥格齐尔德那号人那儿贩来的。”黑胡子准将补充道。“你们知道，尼福尔海姆轨道上的主基地差不多自给自足，有水培园圃和动物组织培养槽；又比「城」级飞船大出足够多，足可冒充一个小世界。是啊，奥格齐尔德那号人，或者此地的菲尔克德王，轻而易举就会认定那是我们的母星。”

“可坎卡德王当时谈的正是……”保拉·昆顿刚起了个头。

===Original===
“We were speaking of geeks, not Kragans.” Von Schlichten lit his cigarette and held his lighter for hers. “You saw that big Beta Hydrae orrery at Kankad’s observatory. Well, there’s quite a little story about that. You know, it’s generally realized by the natives here that Uller is a globe. The North Zirks have ridden all the way around it, on hipposaur-back, in the high latitudes, and the thalassic peoples at the Equator have sailed all the five equatorial seas and portaged all the isthmuses between. But, of course, Uller is the center of the universe; the sun travels around it, on a rather complicated double-spiral track. As a theory, it explains most of what they’re able to observe, and any minor effects that don’t conform to it are just ignored. They have a model, a most ingenious affair run by clockwork, at the University of Konkrook, to show the apparent movement and position of Beta Hydrae in the sky; it does so fairly accurately.

“Well, some of our astronomers constructed this orrery, and exhibited it to a gathering of the leading native scholars, who are also the high-priests of the local religion. Sort of combined Academy of Arts and Sciences and College of Cardinals. They almost were massacred. As soon as the assembled pundits saw this thing and grasped its meaning, they began geeking and skreeking and yorking and squawking and brandishing knives﻿—it was blasphemous, and sacrilegious, and undermined the Faith, and invalidated the whole logic-system.

“I was brigadier-general, in command of Konkrook military district, then﻿—the post Them M’zangwe has now. When I got a riot-call from the University, I hustled around with a company of Kragans, and we cleared the hall with the bayonet and ran the reverend professors out onto the campus, and after we got things in hand, the Kragans crowded around the orrery, trying to set it up to show the existing position of the planet relative to the primary and figure out the theory back of it. They were very much interested; some of them must have sent word home about it, because Kankad came in on the next ship, wanting to see it. He was so much taken with it that Sid Harrington gave it to him. It’s one of his most cherished possessions, but the Konkrook pundits bite all four thumbs and wave their fingers every time they think of it.” He warmed his coffee from a controlled-temperature pot. “You can’t use Kragan thinking on any subject as a criterion of what somebody like Orgzild’s opinions will be.”

===Chinese===
“我们谈的是土佬，不是克拉冈人。”冯·施利希滕点燃香烟，又举着打火机替她点上。“你在坎卡德的天文台见过那座巨大的水蛇座β行星仪（orrery）。嗯，它背后还有段不大不小的故事。你知道，这里的土著普遍都晓得乌勒尔是个圆球。北方齐尔克人骑着河马龙在高纬度绕着它整整走过一圈，赤道上的航海部族更是把五个赤道海航了个遍，其间的地峡也一一扛船陆渡。可是当然喽，乌勒尔依然是宇宙的中心；太阳沿一条相当繁复的双螺旋轨道绕着它转。这套学说能解释他们观察得到的大部分现象，凡是对不上号的零星效应，一概视而不见。孔克鲁克大学有一台模型，靠钟表机构驱动，精巧得很，用来演示水蛇座β在天空中的视运动与视位置；演示得还算准确。

“后来，我们的几位天文学家造出这座行星仪，在本地头面学者的集会上展示——这些学者同时又是本地宗教的大祭司，算是一所艺术科学院兼枢机主教团。他们几个差点儿遭了屠杀。与会的博学诸公一见这东西、一咂摸出其中的含义，登时叽里呱啦、尖啸怪叫、鼓噪喧哗、大呼小叫，还挥舞起刀子——渎神哪，亵渎圣物啊，动摇信仰啊，把整套逻辑体系都作废啦。

“我当时是准将，指挥孔克鲁克军区——就是如今地米·姆赞圭担任的职务。接到大学发来的暴乱警报，我带一连克拉冈人火速赶到，用刺刀清空大厅，把可敬的教授们轰到校园里去。等局面拿稳，克拉冈人却团团围住行星仪，摆弄着让它显示出行星此刻相对恒星的位置，琢磨背后的道理。他们兴致浓得很；其中必定有人捎信回了家，因为坎卡德搭下一班飞船赶来，点名要见识。他喜欢得什么似的，席德·哈灵顿索性把它送给了他。如今那是他最珍爱的藏品之一，而孔克鲁克的博学诸公每回想起它，都要把四根大拇指咬进嘴里、朝人晃手指。”他从控温壶里温热了咖啡。“不论什么题目，都不能拿克拉冈人的想法，去当奥格齐尔德那号人见解的标尺。”

===Original===
“I never could understand the admiration some of you military people have for those cutthroats,” Keaveney declared. “Oh, yes, I can. You like them because they do your dirty work for you.”

“He reads Stanley-Browne, too, I’ll bet,” Hideyoshi O’Leary said. “Miss Quinton, how did you like your visit to Kankad’s Town? Still think the Kragans are cultural mongrels?”

“Why, they’re wonderful! I never expected anything like it. They just seem to have picked up everything they could from us, and then gone on from there to develop a culture of their own with our techniques. For instance, those big guns, the ones they call the Ridge Battery, that they built for themselves. They aren’t copies of Terran guns. They don’t look like our work, or give you the feel our work would. And that telescope at the observatory,” she continued. “Did they build that, too?”

===Chinese===
“我就从来闹不懂，你们军人里头怎么有人那么赏识那帮杀人不眨眼的家伙。”基夫尼宣称。“哦不对，我懂得很。你们喜欢他们，是因为脏活儿都是他们替你们干的。”

“我敢打赌，他八成也读斯坦利-布朗。”秀吉·奥利里说。“昆顿小姐，您这趟坎卡德城之行观感如何？还觉得克拉冈人是文化上的杂种吗？”

“哎呀，他们了不起极了！我压根没想到会见到那样的东西。他们像是把能从我们这儿拿的都拿了去，然后以此为基础，用我们的技术接着发展出一套自己的文化。比如那几门大炮，他们自建的、管它叫「山脊炮台」的那几门。那不是地球火炮的仿制品。看着不像我们的活儿，摸上去也没有我们活儿的那种手感。还有天文台上那架望远镜，”她接着说，“也是他们自己造的吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, all we furnished was a couple of textbooks on lens-grinding and telescope-design, and a book on optics. You see, when we made that deal with them, they realized that we weren’t any better fighters than they were; we just had better weapons. To have the same kind of weapons, they’d have to learn to make them, and once they began studying technology, they found that they had to study science. Weapon-making was the entering-wedge; after that, they found that they could use the same skills to make anything else they wanted. Give them another century or so and they’ll be one of the great races of the galaxy.”

“Yes, and it’s a good thing they’re our friends, too,” Mordkovitz added. “I’m only sorry there are so few of them, and so many of the geeks.”

“Yes, the Company ought to let us stockpile nuclear weapons here, just to be on the safe side,” another officer, farther down the table, said.

“Well, I’m not exactly in favor of that,” von Schlichten replied. “It’s the same principle as not allowing guards who have to go in among the convicts to carry firearms. If somebody like Orgzild got hold of a nuclear bomb, even a little old First-Century H-bomb, he could use it for a model and construct a hundred like it, with all the plutonium we’ve been handing out for power reactors. And there are too few of us, and we’re concentrated in too few places, to last long if that happened. What this planet needs, though, is a visit by a fifty-odd-ship task-force of the Space Navy, just to show the geeks what we have back of us. After a show like that, there’d be a lot less *znidd suddabit* around here.”

===Chinese===
“是的，我们提供的全部，不过是两本教科书——一本讲磨制透镜，一本讲望远镜设计——外加一本论光学的书。要知道，我们跟他们做成那笔交易的时候，他们就明白了：论战斗，我们并不比他们强；我们只是武器更好。要有同样的武器，就得学会造，而他们一旦着手钻研技术，便发现还非得钻研科学不可。造武器是切入的楔子；打那以后，他们发现凭同样的手艺，想要什么就能造什么。再给他们一个世纪上下，他们会成为银河系的大种族之一。”

“是啊，再说幸亏他们是我们的朋友。”莫德科维茨补充道。“我只是遗憾，他们人这么少，土佬又那么多。”

“可不是，公司就该让我们在这儿囤存一批核武器，有备无患。”桌子更靠下首的另一位军官说。

“唔，这个嘛，我并不怎么赞成。”冯·施利希滕答道。“道理就跟不许看守带枪械进犯人堆里一样。要是叫奥格齐尔德那号人弄到一颗核弹——哪怕是颗老掉牙的第一世纪氢弹——他就能拿它当样板，再造出一百颗来，材料就用我们一直发下去供动力反应堆用的钚。而我们人这么少，据点又这么集中，真到那一步，撑不了多久。不过，这颗行星真正需要的，是太空海军一支五十来艘船的特混舰队来访一趟，让土佬瞧瞧我们背后站着什么。那样亮过一次相，这地方的 znidd suddabit 准会少上一大截。”

===Original===
“General, I deplore that sort of talk,” Keaveney said. “I hear too much of this mailed-fist-and-rattling-saber stuff from some of the junior officers here, without your giving countenance and encouragement to it. We’re here to earn dividends for the stockholders of the Uller Company, and we can only do that by gaining the friendship, respect and confidence of the natives.﻿ ﻿…”

“Mr. Keaveney,” Paula Quinton spoke up. “I doubt if even you would seriously accuse the Extraterrestrials’ Rights Association of favoring what you call a mailed-fist-and-rattling-saber policy. We’ve done everything in our power to help these people, and if anybody should have their friendship, we should. Well, only five days ago, in Konkrook, Mr. Mohammed Ferriera and I were attacked by a mob, our native aircar driver was murdered, and if it hadn’t been for General von Schlichten and his soldiers, we’d have lost our own lives. Mr. Ferriera is still hospitalized as a result of injuries he received. It seems that General von Schlichten and his Kragans aren’t trying to get friendship and confidence; they’re willing to settle for respect, in the only way they can get it﻿—by hitting harder and quicker than the geeks can.”

Somebody down the table﻿—one of the military, of course﻿—said, “Hear, hear!” Von Schlichten came as close as a man wearing a monocle can to winking at Paula. Good girl, he thought; she’s started playing on the Army team!

===Chinese===
“将军，这种论调令我深感痛心。”基夫尼说。“本地某些下级军官那一套挥铁拳、晃军刀的高论，我听得已经太多，您就别再给它撑腰打气了。我们来这里，是为乌勒尔公司的股东挣红利，而要挣到红利，只有一条路：赢得土著的友谊、尊重和信任……”

“基夫尼先生，”保拉·昆顿开了口。“我看连您也不会当真指控地外种族权利协会赞成您所谓挥铁拳、晃军刀的政策吧。为帮助这些人，我们已经尽力做了能做的一切；要说谁最有资格得到他们的友谊，那就是我们。可是就在五天前，在孔克鲁克，穆罕默德·费雷拉先生和我遭到暴民袭击，我们的土著飞车司机被杀害；若不是冯·施利希滕将军和他的士兵，我们已经把命丢了。费雷拉先生因所受的伤，至今还在住院。照这么看，冯·施利希滕将军和他的克拉冈人并没有去求友谊和信任；他们宁可退而求尊重——用唯一行得通的法子：出手比土佬更狠、更快。”

桌子下首有人——不用问，是位军人——喊道：“好哇，说得好！”冯·施利希滕朝保拉递过去一个近乎眨眼的眼色——戴单片眼镜的人所能做到的极限也就如此了。好姑娘，他心想；她已经上场替陆军那一队打球了！

===Original===
“Well, of course.﻿ ﻿…” Keaveney began. Then he stopped, as a Terran sergeant came up to the table and bent over Barney Mordkovitz’ shoulder, whispering urgently. The black-bearded brigadier rose immediately, taking his belt from the back of his chair and putting it on. Motioning the sergeant to accompany him, he spoke briefly to Keaveney and then came around the table to where von Schlichten sat, the Resident-Agent accompanying him.

“Message just came in from Konkrook, general,” he said softly. “Sid Harrington’s dead.”

It took von Schlichten all of a second to grasp what had been said. “Good God! When? How?”

“Here’s all we know, sir,” the sergeant said, giving him a radioprint slip. “Came in ten minutes ago.”

It was an all-station priority telecast. Governor-General Harrington had died suddenly, in his room, at 22:10; there were no details. He glanced at his watch; it was 22:43. Konkrook and Skilk were in the same time-zone; that was fast work. He handed the slip to Mordkovitz, who gave it to Keaveney.

===Chinese===
“哦，那当然……”基夫尼刚起了个头。随即他打住了：一名地球人中士走到桌旁，俯在巴尼·莫德科维茨肩头急促地低语。黑胡子准将立刻起身，从椅背上取过腰带束上。他示意中士随他同行，对基夫尼简短交代了两句，便绕过桌子走到冯·施利希滕座前，驻扎代理也跟了过来。

“孔克鲁克刚来了消息，将军。”他低声说。“席德·哈灵顿死了。”

冯·施利希滕足足用了一秒钟才听明白。“天哪！什么时候？怎么死的？”

“我们知道的就是这些，长官。”中士说着，递给他一张无线电电传纸条。“十分钟前收到的。”

那是一份发往全体台站的优先通报：哈灵顿总督于22时10分在本人房间里猝然去世，没有细节。他瞥了一眼手表：22时43分。孔克鲁克与斯基尔克同在一个时区；动作够快的。他把纸条递给莫德科维茨，莫德科维茨又递给基夫尼。

===Original===
“You from the telecast station, sergeant?” he asked. “All right, let’s go.”

“Wait a minute, general.” Keaveney put out a hand to detain him as he took his belt and put it on. “How about this?” He gestured nervously with the radioprint slip.

“Get up and make an announcement, now,” von Schlichten told him, fastening the buckle and hitching his pistol and survival-kit into place. “It’ll be out all over the planet in half an hour. Never hold news out unnecessarily.” He stubbed out his cigarette. “Come on, sergeant.”

As he hurried from the banquet-room, he could hear Keaveney tapping on his wineglass.

“Everybody, please! Let me have your attention! There has just come in a piece of the most tragic news.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“你是电视广播站来的吧，中士？”他问。“好，走吧。”

“稍等，将军。”基夫尼伸手拦住他，这时他正束上腰带。“这事怎么处置？”他攥着那张电传纸条，紧张地比划着。

“站起来，马上宣布。”冯·施利希滕吩咐道，一边扣好腰带扣，把手枪和救生包归置到位。“半小时之内，消息就会传遍全行星。永远不要没来由地扣住消息不放。”他掐灭香烟。“走吧，中士。”

他匆匆走出宴会厅时，还听得见基夫尼敲响酒杯的声音。

“各位，请了！请大家注意！刚刚传来一则极其不幸的消息……”

## X The Geek Luftwaffe and the Kragan Airlift / 十 土佬空军与克拉冈空运

===Original===
At 02:45, an attack developed on the northwestern corner of the Reservation, in the direction of the explosives magazines. It turned out to be relatively trivial. Remnants of the mob that had been broken up by air attack on the road had gotten together and were making rushes in small bands, keeping well spread out. Beating them off took considerable ammunition, but it was accomplished with negligible casualties to the defenders. They finally stopped coming around daylight.

In the meantime, Themistocles M’zangwe called from Konkrook, appearing in the screen with his left arm in a freshly white sling.

“What the hell have you been doing to yourself?” von Schlichten wanted to know.

===Chinese===
02时45分，特区西北角朝着炸药库的方向出现了一次攻击。结果不过是小事一桩。先前在公路上被空袭冲散的暴民残部重新聚拢，分成小股一波波冲击，彼此散得很开。把他们打退耗费了不少弹药，但守方伤亡微不足道。到天亮前后，他们总算不再来了。

与此同时，地米斯托克利·姆赞圭从孔克鲁克打来电话，出现在屏幕上，左臂吊着一条崭新的白色悬带。

“你这是把自己怎么弄的？”冯·施利希滕想知道。

===Original===
“Crossbow-bolt, about half an hour ago. A couple of inches lower and acting Brigadier-General Colbert’d have been talking to you, now, instead of me.”

“Lucky it didn’t have a nitro-capsule on the end. How are you making out? Have Kankad’s people started coming in, yet?”

“Oh, yes, about six hundred of them have gotten in already, in the damnedest collection of vehicles you ever saw. Kankad must be using every scrap of contragravity he has; it’s a regular airborne Dunkirk-in-reverse. Kankad sent word that he’s coming here in person, as soon as he has things organized at his place. And the geeks here have scraped together an air-force of their own﻿—farm-lorries, aircars, that sort of thing﻿—and they’re using them to bomb us here and at the mainland farm, mostly with nitroglycerine. We’ve shot down about twenty of them, but they’re still coming. They tried a boat-attack across the Channel; that’s how I got this. We’ve been doing some bombing, ourselves; we made a down payment for Eric Blount and Hendrik Lemoyne. Took a fifty-ton tank off a fuel-lorry, fitted it with a detonator, filled it with thermoconcentrate, and ferried it over on the *Elmoran* and dumped it on the Keegarkan Embassy. It must have landed in the middle of the central court; in about fifteen seconds, flames were coming out every window in the place.” His face became less jovial. “We had something pretty bad happen here, too,” he said. “That Konkrook Fencibles rabble of Prince Jaizerd’s mutinied, along with the others; they got into the hospital and butchered everybody in the place, patients and staff. The Kragans got there too late to save anybody, but they wiped out the Fencibles. Jaizerd himself was the only one they took alive, and he didn’t stay that way very long.”

===Chinese===
“弩箭，大约半个钟头前。再低上两英寸，此刻跟您说话的就是代理准将科尔伯特，而不是我了。”

“算你走运，箭头上没装硝化甘油胶囊。你们那边情形如何？坎卡德的人开始到了吗？”

“哦，到了，已经有大约六百人进来了，坐的是你生平所见最不像话的一支拼凑车队。坎卡德准是把手头每一件反重力家当都用上了；俨然一场反着来的空中敦刻尔克。坎卡德捎话说，等他把家里的事情安排妥当，就亲自过来。这边的土佬也七拼八凑出一支自己的空军——农用卡车、飞车，诸如此类——用它来轰炸我们这里和大陆农场，弹药多半是硝化甘油。我们已经打下来二十来架，可他们还在来。他们还试过一次从水道对面用船攻击；我这伤就是这么来的。我们也回敬了些轰炸；算是替埃里克·布朗特和亨德里克·勒莫因付了头一笔账。我们从一辆运燃料的卡车上卸下一只五十吨的槽罐，装上起爆器，灌满热凝燃料，用「埃尔莫兰」号运过去，卸在了基加克使馆头上。它准是正正落在中央院子里；约莫十五秒钟，那地方的每一扇窗子都往外冒火。”他的脸色沉了下来。“我们这儿也出了件相当糟的事。”他说。“亚泽尔德亲王那帮孔克鲁克义勇军的乌合之众，跟着别人一起哗变了；他们冲进医院，把里面的所有人——伤员和医护人员——屠了个干净。克拉冈人赶到时已经晚了，谁也没救下，但他们把那帮义勇军斩尽杀绝。亚泽尔德本人是唯一被活捉的，而他那个活人也没当多久。”

===Original===
“How are you making out with your Civil Administration crowd?”

M’zangwe grimaced. “I haven’t had to put any of them under actual arrest, so far, but we’ve had to keep Buhrmann away from the communications equipment by force. He wanted to call you up and chew you out for not evacuating everybody in the north to Konkrook.”

“Is he crazy?”

===Chinese===
“你手下那班民政官员，你还摆弄得动吗？”

姆赞圭做了个苦脸。“到目前为止，我还没到真逮捕谁的地步，可我们已经不得不硬把布尔曼从通讯设备跟前架开。他要打电话给你，骂你为什么不着把北部所有的人都撤到孔克鲁克去。”

“他疯了？”

===Original===
“No, just scared. He says you’re going to get everybody on Uller massacred by detail, when you could save Konkrook by bringing them all here.”

“You tell him I’m going to hold this planet, not just one city. Tell him I have a sense of my duty to the Company and its stockholders, if he hasn’t; put it in those terms and he may understand you.”

“Yes, I’ll try that out on Meyerstein, too. He’s in a hell of a state about the losses the Banking Cartel are taking on this deal.﻿ ﻿… Well, I’ll call you when there’s anything new.”

===Chinese===
“没疯，只是吓坏了。他说你这是要让乌勒尔上的所有人被零敲碎打地杀光，其实只要把他们全弄到这儿来，孔克鲁克就能保住。”

“你告诉他，我要守住的是这颗行星，不只是一座城。告诉他，就算他没有，我也知道自己对公司及其股东负有什么责任；就这么说，他或许听得懂。”
